word by word · translation

om om apsu in the waters me to me somaḥ Soma abravīt declared antaḥ within viśvāni are all bheṣajā remedies agnim Agni ca and viśva-śambhuvam who blesses all āpaḥ the waters ca and viśva-bheṣajīḥ that heal all candra-graha-adhidevatāḥ the presiding deities of the planet Moon sa-aṅgāḥ with their limbs sa-āyudhāḥ their weapons sa-vāhanāḥ their mounts sa-śaktīḥ their powers putra-parivāra-sametāḥ and with sons and retinue candra-grahasya of the planet Moon dakṣiṇataḥ to the south apaḥ the Waters āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

“Soma told me that within the waters are all remedies, and Agni who blesses all, and the waters that heal all.” The Waters, presiding deities of the Moon, are invoked on his right.

oṃ gaurīrmimāya salilāni takṣatyekapadī dvipadī sā catuṣpadī |
aṣṭāpadī navapadī babhūvuṣī sahasrākṣarā parame vyoman ||
candragrahapratyadhidevatāṃ gaurīṃ sāṅgāṃ sāyudhāṃ savāhanāṃ saśaktiṃ
putraparivārasametāṃ candragrahasyottarataḥ gaurīmāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om gaurīḥ the buffalo-cow mimāya has lowed salilāni the waters takṣatī fashioning eka-padī one-footed dvi-padī two-footed sā she catuḥ-padī four-footed aṣṭā-padī eight-footed nava-padī nine-footed babhūvuṣī having become sahasra-akṣarā of a thousand syllables parame in the highest vyoman heaven candra-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Moon gaurīm Gaurī sa-aṅgām with her limbs sa-āyudhām her weapons sa-vāhanām her mount sa-śaktim her power putra-parivāra-sametām and with sons and retinue candra-grahasya of the planet Moon uttarataḥ to the north gaurīm Gaurī āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

“The buffalo-cow lowed …” Gaurī, counter-deity of the Moon, is invoked on his left.

3 · Aṅgāraka (Kuja) ↑

kujāriṣṭe tu samprāpte kujapūjāṃ ca kārayet |
kujadhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi rogapīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

kuja-ariṣṭe when affliction from Mars tu indeed samprāpte has come kuja-pūjām the worship of Mars ca then kārayet one should have performed kuja-dhyānam the meditation on Mars pravakṣyāmi I shall declare roga-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of the affliction of disease

When affliction from Mars befalls, let Mars be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Mars, to quiet the suffering of disease.

agnirmūrdhetyasya mantrasya virūpa ṛṣiḥ | aṅgārakagraho devatā | triṣṭup chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitāṅgārakagrahaprasādasiddhyarthe
aṅgārakagrahārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

agniḥ mūrdhā (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra virūpaḥ Virūpa ṛṣiḥ is the seer aṅgāraka-grahaḥ the planet Mars devatā is the deity triṣṭup Triṣṭubh chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-aṅgāraka-graha-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the planet Mars with its presiding and co-presiding deities aṅgāraka-graha-ārādhane in the worship of the planet Mars viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “agnirmūrdhā” the seer is Virūpa, the deity Mars; it is applied in the worship of Mars.

yāmye gadāśaktidharaśca śūlī varaprado yāmyamukho'tiriktaḥ |
kujastvavantīviṣayastrikoṇastasminbharadvājakule prasūtaḥ ||
raktāmbaro raktavapuḥ kirīṭī caturbhujo meṣagamo gadābhṛt |
dharāsutaśśaktidharaśca śūlī sadā mama syādvaradaḥ praśāntaḥ ||

word by word · translation

yāmye in the south gadā-śakti-dharaḥ bearing mace and spear ca and śūlī with a trident vara-pradaḥ granting boons yāmya-mukhaḥ facing south atiriktaḥ surpassing kujaḥ Mars tu indeed avantī-viṣayaḥ of the Avantī country tri-koṇaḥ with a triangular seat tasmin there bharadvāja-kule in the family of Bharadvāja prasūtaḥ born rakta-ambaraḥ red-robed rakta-vapuḥ red-bodied kirīṭī crowned catuḥ-bhujaḥ four-armed meṣa-gamaḥ riding a ram gadā-bhṛt mace-bearer dharā-sutaḥ son of the Earth śakti-dharaḥ bearing a spear ca and śūlī with a trident sadā always mama to me syāt may he be vara-daḥ a giver of boons praśāntaḥ serene

Dhyāna: in the south, bearing mace, spear and trident, giver of boons, facing south, fierce; Mars, of the Avantī country, on a triangular seat, born in Bharadvāja's line. Red-robed, red of body, crowned, four-armed, riding a ram, mace-bearing — may the son of the Earth, holding spear and trident, be ever gracious and calm toward me.

oṃ agnirmūrdhā divaḥ kakutpatiḥ pṛthivyā ayam |
apāgṃ retāgṃsi jinvati ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ aṅgārakagrahehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om agniḥ Agni mūrdhā is the head divaḥ of heaven kakut the summit patiḥ lord pṛthivyāḥ of the earth ayam this one apām of the waters retāṃsi the seeds jinvati he quickens om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven aṅgāraka-graha O planet Mars iha here āgaccha come

“Agni is the head, the summit of heaven, this lord of the earth; he quickens the seed of the waters.” — Come here, Mars.

aṅgārakagrahaṃ raktavarṇaṃ raktagandhaṃ raktapuṣpaṃ raktamālyāmbaradharaṃ
raktacchatradhvajapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ meṣavāhanaṃ dakṣiṇābhimukhaṃ caturbhujaṃ
gadāśūlaśaktidharam avantīdeśādhipatiṃ bhāradvājasagotraṃ
rākṣasanāmasaṃvatsare āṣāḍhamāse śuklapakṣe daśamyāṃ bhaumavāsare
anūrādhānakṣatrajātaṃ meṣavṛścikarāśyadhipatiṃ kirīṭinaṃ sukhāsīnaṃ
patnīputraparivārasametaṃ grahamaṇḍale praviṣṭamasminnadhikaraṇe
sūryagrahasya dakṣiṇadigbhāge trikoṇākāramaṇḍale
sthāpitatāmrapratimārūpeṇa aṅgārakagrahamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

aṅgāraka-graham the planet Mars rakta-varṇam red in colour rakta-gandham with red sandal paste rakta-puṣpam with red flowers rakta-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing red garlands and garments rakta-chatra-dhvaja-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with red parasol, banner, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru pradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the right meṣa-vāhanam riding a ram dakṣiṇa-abhimukham facing south catuḥ-bhujam four-armed gadā-śūla-śakti-dharam bearing mace, trident and spear avantī-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Avantī country bhāradvāja-sa-gotram of the Bhāradvāja lineage rākṣasa-nāma-saṃvatsare in the year named Rākṣasa āṣāḍha-māse in the month of Āṣāḍha śukla-pakṣe in the bright fortnight daśamyām on the tenth day bhauma-vāsare on Tuesday anūrādhā-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Anūrādhā meṣa-vṛścika-rāśi-adhipatim lord of the signs Aries and Scorpio kirīṭinam crowned sukha-āsīnam seated at ease patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam with wife, sons and retinue graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun dakṣiṇa-dik-bhāge in the southern quarter tri-koṇa-ākāra-maṇḍale in a triangular diagram sthāpita-tāmra-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the copper image set up aṅgāraka-graham the planet Mars āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Mars — red, with red sandal, flowers, garland and garments, riding a ram, facing south, four-armed, bearing mace, trident and spear, lord of Avantī, of Bharadvāja's gotra, born in the year Rākṣasa, in Āṣāḍha, the bright tenth, a Tuesday, under Anurādhā, lord of Aries and Scorpio — I invoke in the copper image set in the triangular maṇḍala south of the Sun.

oṃ syonā pṛthivi bhavānṛkṣarā niveśanī |
yacchā naśśarma saprathāḥ ||
aṅgārakagrahādhidevatāṃ pṛthivīṃ sāṅgāṃ sāyudhāṃ savāhanāṃ saśaktiṃ
putraparivārasametām aṅgārakagrahasya dakṣiṇataḥ pṛthivīmāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om syonā pleasant pṛthivi O Earth bhava be anṛkṣarā thornless niveśanī a resting place yaccha grant naḥ us śarma shelter sa-prathāḥ far-spreading aṅgāraka-graha-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the planet Mars pṛthivīm the Earth sa-aṅgām with her limbs sa-āyudhām her weapons sa-vāhanām her mount sa-śaktim her power putra-parivāra-sametām and with sons and retinue aṅgāraka-grahasya of the planet Mars dakṣiṇataḥ to the south pṛthivīm the Earth āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

“Be kindly, Earth, thornless, a resting-place; grant us wide shelter.” Earth, presiding deity of Mars, is invoked on his right.

oṃ kṣetrasya patinā vayagṃ hiteneva jayāmasi |
gāmaśvaṃ poṣayitnvā sa no mṛḍātīdṛśe ||
aṅgārakagrahapratyadhidevatāṃ kṣetrapālakaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
aṅgārakagrahasyottarataḥ kṣetrapālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om kṣetrasya of the field patinā through the lord vayam we hitena through a friend iva as jayāmasi win gām cow aśvam and horse poṣayitnu what nourishes ā hither saḥ he naḥ to us mṛḍāti is gracious īdṛśe in such gain aṅgāraka-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Mars kṣetra-pālakam the Guardian of the field sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue aṅgāraka-grahasya of the planet Mars uttarataḥ to the north kṣetra-pālakam the Guardian of the field āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

“With the Lord of the Field, as with a friend, we win cows and horses and all that nourishes; may he be gracious to us in such things.” The Guardian of the Field, counter-deity of Mars, is invoked on his left.

4 · Budha ↑

budhāriṣṭe tu samprāpte budhapūjāṃ ca kārayet |
budhadhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi buddhipīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

budha-ariṣṭe when affliction from Mercury tu indeed samprāpte has come budha-pūjām the worship of Mercury ca then kārayet one should have performed budha-dhyānam the meditation on Mercury pravakṣyāmi I shall declare buddhi-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of affliction of the intellect

When affliction from Mercury befalls, let Mercury be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Mercury, to quiet troubles of the intellect.

udbudhyasvetyasya mantrasya praskaṇva ṛṣiḥ | budhagraho devatā | triṣṭup chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitabudhagrahaprasādasiddhyarthe
budhagrahārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

udbudhyasva (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra praskaṇvaḥ Praskaṇva ṛṣiḥ is the seer budha-grahaḥ the planet Mercury devatā is the deity triṣṭup Triṣṭubh chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-budha-graha-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the planet Mercury with its presiding and co-presiding deities budha-graha-ārādhane in the worship of the planet Mercury viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “udbudhyasva” the seer is Praskaṇva, the deity Mercury, the metre triṣṭubh; it is applied in the worship of Mercury.

udaṅmukho māgadhajo haristhaścātreyagotraśśaramaṇḍalasthaḥ |
sakhaḍgacarmorugadādharo jñastvīśānabhāge varadassupītaḥ ||
pītāmbaraḥ pītavapuḥ kirīṭī caturbhujo daṇḍadharaśca saumyaḥ |
carmāsidhṛk somasutassumerussiṃhādhirūḍho varado budhaśca ||

word by word · translation

udak-mukhaḥ facing north māgadha-jaḥ born in Magadha hari-sthaḥ mounted on a lion ca and ātreya-gotraḥ of the Ātreya lineage śara-maṇḍala-sthaḥ in an arrow-shaped diagram sa-khaḍga-carma-uru-gadā-dharaḥ bearing sword, shield and great mace jñaḥ Mercury, the knower tu indeed īśāna-bhāge in the north-east quarter vara-daḥ giver of boons su-pītaḥ bright yellow pīta-ambaraḥ yellow-robed pīta-vapuḥ yellow-bodied kirīṭī crowned catuḥ-bhujaḥ four-armed daṇḍa-dharaḥ bearing a staff ca and saumyaḥ the gentle one carma-asi-dhṛk holding shield and sword soma-sutaḥ son of the Moon su-meruḥ lofty as Meru siṃha-adhirūḍhaḥ mounted on a lion vara-daḥ giver of boons budhaḥ Mercury ca and

Dhyāna: facing north, born in Magadha, riding a lion, of Atri's gotra, on an arrow-shaped maṇḍala, bearing sword, shield and great mace — Budha in the north-east, deep yellow, giver of boons. Yellow-robed, yellow of body, crowned, four-armed, staff in hand, gentle, bearing shield and sword, son of the Moon, riding a lion — may Budha grant boons.

oṃ udbudhyasvāgne pratijāgṛhyenamiṣṭāpūrte sagṃsṛjethāmayaṃ ca |
punaḥ kṛṇvagṃstvā pitaraṃ yuvānamanvātāgṃsīttvayi tantumetam ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ budhagrahehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om udbudhyasva awake agne O Agni prati-jāgṛhi keep watch enam over him iṣṭā-pūrte in sacrifice and good works saṃsṛjethām may you two unite ayam this one ca and punaḥ again kṛṇvan making tvā you pitaram the father yuvānam young anvātāṃsīt he has stretched tvayi in you tantum thread etam this om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven budha-graha O planet Mercury iha here āgaccha come

“Awake, Agni, and watch over him; may you and he be joined in sacrifice and good works; making the father young again, he has spun this thread out through you.” — Come here, Mercury.

budhagrahaṃ pītavarṇaṃ pītagandhaṃ pītapuṣpaṃ pītamālyāmbaradharaṃ
pītacchatradhvajarathapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ siṃhavāhanam udaṅmukhaṃ magadhadeśādhipatiṃ
caturbhujaṃ khaḍgacarmāmbaradharam ātreyasagotram
āṅgīrasanāmasaṃvatsare mārgaśīrṣamāse śuklapakṣe saptamyāṃ saumyavāsare
pūrvābhādrānakṣatrajātaṃ mithunakanyārāśyadhipatiṃ kirīṭinaṃ sukhāsīnaṃ
patnīputraparivārasametaṃ grahamaṇḍale praviṣṭamasminnadhikaraṇe
sūryagrahasya īśānyadigbhāge bāṇākāramaṇḍale
sthāpitakāṃsyapratimārūpeṇa budhagrahamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

budha-graham the planet Mercury pīta-varṇam yellow in colour pīta-gandham with yellow sandal paste pīta-puṣpam with yellow flowers pīta-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing yellow garlands and garments pīta-chatra-dhvaja-ratha-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with yellow parasol, banner, chariot, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru pradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the right siṃha-vāhanam riding a lion udak-mukham facing north magadha-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Magadha country catuḥ-bhujam four-armed khaḍga-carma-ambara-dharam bearing sword, shield and garment ātreya-sa-gotram of the Ātreya lineage āṅgīrasa-nāma-saṃvatsare in the year named Āṅgīrasa mārgaśīrṣa-māse in the month of Mārgaśīrṣa śukla-pakṣe in the bright fortnight saptamyām on the seventh day saumya-vāsare on Wednesday pūrvābhādrā-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Pūrvābhādrā mithuna-kanyā-rāśi-adhipatim lord of the signs Gemini and Virgo kirīṭinam crowned sukha-āsīnam seated at ease patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam with wife, sons and retinue graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun īśānya-dik-bhāge in the north-east quarter bāṇa-ākāra-maṇḍale in an arrow-shaped diagram sthāpita-kāṃsya-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the bell-metal image set up budha-graham the planet Mercury āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Mercury — yellow, with yellow sandal, flowers, garland and garments, riding a lion, facing north, lord of Magadha, four-armed, with sword and shield, of Atri's gotra, born in the year Āṅgīrasa, in Mārgaśīrṣa, the bright seventh, a Wednesday, under Pūrvābhādrā, lord of Gemini and Virgo — I invoke in the bell-metal image set in the arrow-shaped maṇḍala north-east of the Sun.

mahāviṣṇuṃ śaṅkhapadmasudarśanagadādharam |
dhyāye'haṃ nīlagaurāṅgaṃ padmasthaṃ kamalāpatim ||
oṃ idaṃ viṣṇurvicakrame tredhā nidadhe padam | samūḍhamasya pāgṃsure ||
budhagrahādhidevatāṃ viṣṇuṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
budhagrahasya dakṣiṇataḥ viṣṇumāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

mahā-viṣṇum great Viṣṇu śaṅkha-padma-sudarśana-gadā-dharam bearing conch, lotus, discus and mace dhyāye meditate on aham I nīla-gaura-aṅgam of dark and fair limbs padma-stham standing on a lotus kamalā-patim lord of Lakṣmī om om idam this viṣṇuḥ Viṣṇu vicakrame strode over tredhā thrice nidadhe he set down padam his foot samūḍham gathered up asya in his pāṃsure dusty footprint budha-graha-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the planet Mercury viṣṇum Viṣṇu sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue budha-grahasya of the planet Mercury dakṣiṇataḥ to the south viṣṇum Viṣṇu āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

I meditate on Mahāviṣṇu, bearing conch, lotus, the discus Sudarśana and mace, of blue and fair limbs, seated on a lotus, lord of Kamalā. “Viṣṇu strode over this …” Viṣṇu, presiding deity of Mercury, is invoked on his right.

pītapadmāsanāsīnaṃ caturbāhuṃ kirīṭinam |
cintaye śaṅkhacakrābhyāṃ gadādhāriṇamacyutam ||
oṃ sahasraśīrṣā puruṣaḥ sahasrākṣassahasrapāt |
sa bhūmiṃ viśvato vṛtvā atyatiṣṭhaddaśāṅgulam ||
budhagrahapratyadhidevatāṃ nārāyaṇaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
budhagrahasyottarataḥ nārāyaṇamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

pīta-padma-āsana-āsīnam seated on a yellow lotus seat catuḥ-bāhum four-armed kirīṭinam crowned cintaye I think of śaṅkha-cakrābhyām with conch and discus gadā-dhāriṇam bearing a mace acyutam Acyuta om om sahasra-śīrṣā thousand-headed puruṣaḥ is the Person sahasra-akṣaḥ thousand-eyed sahasra-pāt thousand-footed saḥ he bhūmim the earth viśvataḥ on every side vṛtvā covering atyatiṣṭhat stood beyond it daśa-aṅgulam by ten fingers budha-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Mercury nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue budha-grahasya of the planet Mercury uttarataḥ to the north nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

I contemplate Acyuta, seated on a yellow lotus, four-armed, crowned, holding conch, discus and mace. “The Puruṣa has a thousand heads, a thousand eyes, a thousand feet; enveloping the earth on every side, he stands beyond it by ten fingers.” Nārāyaṇa, counter-deity of Mercury, is invoked on his left.

5 · Bṛhaspati (Guru) ↑

gurvariṣṭe tu samprāpte gurupūjāṃ ca kārayet |
gurudhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi putrapīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

guru-ariṣṭe when affliction from Jupiter tu indeed samprāpte has come guru-pūjām the worship of Jupiter ca then kārayet one should have performed guru-dhyānam the meditation on Jupiter pravakṣyāmi I shall declare putra-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of affliction to one's sons

When affliction from Jupiter befalls, let Jupiter be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Jupiter, to quiet troubles concerning children.

bṛhaspate atiyadaryetyasya mantrasya gṛtsamada ṛṣiḥ |
bṛhaspatigraho devatā | triṣṭup chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitabṛhaspatigrahaprasādasiddhyarthe
bṛhaspatigrahārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

bṛhaspate ati yat aryaḥ (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra gṛtsamadaḥ Gṛtsamada ṛṣiḥ is the seer bṛhaspati-grahaḥ the planet Jupiter devatā is the deity triṣṭup Triṣṭubh chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-bṛhaspati-graha-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the planet Jupiter with its presiding and co-presiding deities bṛhaspati-graha-ārādhane in the worship of the planet Jupiter viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “bṛhaspate ati yadaryaḥ” the seer is Gṛtsamada, the deity Bṛhaspati, the metre triṣṭubh; it is applied in the worship of Jupiter.

saumye sudīrghe caturaśrapīṭhe rathe'ṅgirāḥ pūrvamukhasvabhāvaḥ |
daṇḍākṣamālājalapātradhārī sindhvākhyadeśe varadassureśaḥ ||
pītāmbaraḥ pītavapuḥ kirīṭī caturbhujo devaguruḥ praśāntaḥ |
tathāsidaṇḍaṃ ca kamaṇḍaluṃ ca tathākṣasūtraṃ varado'stu mahyam ||

word by word · translation

saumye in the north su-dīrghe on a long catuḥ-aśra-pīṭhe rectangular seat rathe in a chariot aṅgirāḥ the son of Aṅgiras pūrva-mukha-svabhāvaḥ by nature facing east daṇḍa-akṣa-mālā-jala-pātra-dhārī bearing staff, rosary and water-pot sindhu-ākhya-deśe in the country called Sindhu vara-daḥ giver of boons sura-īśaḥ lord of the gods pīta-ambaraḥ yellow-robed pīta-vapuḥ yellow-bodied kirīṭī crowned catuḥ-bhujaḥ four-armed deva-guruḥ teacher of the gods praśāntaḥ serene tathā likewise asi-daṇḍam sword and staff ca and kamaṇḍalum a water-pot ca and tathā likewise akṣa-sūtram a rosary vara-daḥ a giver of boons astu may he be mahyam to me

Dhyāna: in the north, on a long rectangular seat, in his chariot, Āṅgirasa by descent, facing east, bearing staff, rosary and water-pot, of the Sindhu country — the lord among gods, giver of boons. Yellow-robed, yellow of body, crowned, four-armed, the calm teacher of the gods, with sword and staff, water-pot and rosary — may he grant me boons.

oṃ bṛhaspate ati yadaryo arhāddyumadvibhāti kratumajjaneṣu |
yaddīdayacchavasa ṛtaprajāta tadasmāsu draviṇaṃ dhehi citram ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ bṛhaspatigrahehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om bṛhaspate O Bṛhaspati ati beyond yat what aryaḥ the noble one arhāt may deserve dyu-mat brilliant vibhāti shines kratu-mat full of power janeṣu among men yat what dīdayat blazes śavasā with might ṛta-prajāta O you born of order tat that asmāsu on us draviṇam wealth dhehi bestow citram wondrous om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven bṛhaspati-graha O planet Jupiter iha here āgaccha come

“Bṛhaspati, the wealth that the noble one deserves, that shines brightly and with power among the peoples, that blazes with strength, O you born of truth — that wondrous wealth place in us.” — Come here, Jupiter.

bṛhaspatigrahaṃ pītavarṇaṃ pītagandhaṃ pītapuṣpaṃ pītamālyāmbaradharaṃ
pītacchatradhvajapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ pūrvābhimukhaṃ padmāsanasthaṃ caturbhujaṃ
daṇḍākṣamālādhāriṇaṃ sindhudvīpadeśādhipatim āṅgīrasagotram
āṅgīrasanāmasaṃvatsare vaiśākhamāse śuklapakṣe ekādaśyāṃ guruvāsare
uttarānakṣatrajātaṃ dhanurmīnarāśyadhipatiṃ kirīṭinaṃ sukhāsīnaṃ
patnīputraparivārasametaṃ grahamaṇḍale praviṣṭamasminnadhikaraṇe
sūryagrahasyottaradigbhāge dīrghacaturasramaṇḍale
sthāpitatrapupratimārūpeṇa bṛhaspatigrahamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

bṛhaspati-graham the planet Jupiter pīta-varṇam yellow in colour pīta-gandham with yellow sandal paste pīta-puṣpam with yellow flowers pīta-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing yellow garlands and garments pīta-chatra-dhvaja-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with yellow parasol, banner, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru pradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the right pūrva-abhimukham facing east padma-āsana-stham seated on a lotus catuḥ-bhujam four-armed daṇḍa-akṣa-mālā-dhāriṇam bearing staff and rosary sindhu-dvīpa-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Sindhu island country āṅgīrasa-gotram of the Āṅgīrasa lineage āṅgīrasa-nāma-saṃvatsare in the year named Āṅgīrasa vaiśākha-māse in the month of Vaiśākha śukla-pakṣe in the bright fortnight ekādaśyām on the eleventh day guru-vāsare on Thursday uttarā-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Uttarā dhanuḥ-mīna-rāśi-adhipatim lord of the signs Sagittarius and Pisces kirīṭinam crowned sukha-āsīnam seated at ease patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam with wife, sons and retinue graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun uttara-dik-bhāge in the northern quarter dīrgha-catuḥ-asra-maṇḍale in a rectangular diagram sthāpita-trapu-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the tin image set up bṛhaspati-graham the planet Jupiter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Jupiter — yellow, with yellow sandal, flowers, garland and garments, facing east, seated on a lotus, four-armed, bearing staff and rosary, lord of the Sindhu country, of Aṅgiras's gotra, born in the year Āṅgīrasa, in Vaiśākha, the bright eleventh, a Thursday, under Uttarā, lord of Sagittarius and Pisces — I invoke in the tin image set in the rectangular maṇḍala north of the Sun.

brahmāṇaṃ raktagaurāṅgaṃ caturvaktraṃ jagatprabhum |
akṣasrakkuṇḍikābhītivarapāṇiṃ vicintaye ||
oṃ brahma jajñānaṃ prathamaṃ purastādvi sīmatassuruco vena āvaḥ |
sa budhniyā upamā asya viṣṭhāssataśca yonimasataśca vivaḥ ||
bṛhaspatigrahādhidevatāṃ brahmāṇaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
bṛhaspatigrahasya dakṣiṇataḥ brahmāṇamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

brahmāṇam Brahmā rakta-gaura-aṅgam of ruddy-fair limbs catuḥ-vaktram four-faced jagat-prabhum lord of the world akṣa-srak-kuṇḍikā-abhīti-vara-pāṇim whose hands hold rosary and water-pot and grant fearlessness and boons vicintaye I contemplate om om brahma the sacred Word jajñānam born prathamam first purastāt in the east vi apart sīmataḥ from the horizon su-rucaḥ the bright ones venaḥ the seer āvaḥ has revealed saḥ he budhniyāḥ the deepest upamāḥ and nearest asya its vi-ṣṭhāḥ forms sataḥ of the existent ca and yonim the womb asataḥ of the non-existent ca and vi vaḥ he has uncovered bṛhaspati-graha-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the planet Jupiter brahmāṇam Brahmā sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue bṛhaspati-grahasya of the planet Jupiter dakṣiṇataḥ to the south brahmāṇam Brahmā āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

I contemplate Brahmā, of red and fair limbs, four-faced, lord of the world, holding rosary and water-pot, his hands granting fearlessness and boons. “Brahman, first born in the east — Vena unveiled the shining ones from the horizon; he revealed the deepest forms of it, the womb of the existent and the non-existent.” Brahmā, presiding deity of Jupiter, is invoked on his right.

indramairāvatārūḍhaṃ vajrāyudhadharaṃ prabhum |
pūrvadikpālakaṃ devaṃ sarvadevanamaskṛtam ||
oṃ indraṃ vo viśvataspari havāmahe janebhyaḥ | asmākamastu kevalaḥ ||
bṛhaspatigrahapratyadhidevatām indraṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
bṛhaspatigrahasya uttarataḥ indramāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

indram Indra airāvata-ārūḍham mounted on Airāvata vajra-āyudha-dharam bearing the thunderbolt prabhum the lord pūrva-dik-pālakam guardian of the east devam the god sarva-deva-namaskṛtam saluted by all the gods om om indram Indra vaḥ for you viśvataḥ from every side pari above havāmahe we call janebhyaḥ all peoples asmākam ours astu may he be kevalaḥ alone bṛhaspati-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Jupiter indram Indra sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue bṛhaspati-grahasya of the planet Jupiter uttarataḥ to the north indram Indra āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Indra, mounted on Airāvata, bearing the thunderbolt, the lord, guardian of the east, saluted by all the gods. “We call Indra for you from among all peoples; may he be ours alone.” Indra, counter-deity of Jupiter, is invoked on his left.

6 · Śukra ↑

śukrāriṣṭe tu samprāpte śukrapūjāṃ ca kārayet |
śukradhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi patnīpīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

śukra-ariṣṭe when affliction from Venus tu indeed samprāpte has come śukra-pūjām the worship of Venus ca then kārayet one should have performed śukra-dhyānam the meditation on Venus pravakṣyāmi I shall declare patnī-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of affliction to one's wife

When affliction from Venus befalls, let Venus be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Venus, to quiet troubles concerning one's wife.

śukraṃ te anyadityasya mantrasya bharadvāja ṛṣiḥ | śukragraho devatā | triṣṭup chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitaśukragrahaprasādasiddhyarthe
śukragrahārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

śukram te anyat (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra bharadvājaḥ Bharadvāja ṛṣiḥ is the seer śukra-grahaḥ the planet Venus devatā is the deity triṣṭup Triṣṭubh chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-śukra-graha-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the planet Venus with its presiding and co-presiding deities śukra-graha-ārādhane in the worship of the planet Venus viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “śukraṃ te anyat” the seer is Bharadvāja, the deity Venus, the metre triṣṭubh; it is applied in the worship of Venus.

prācyāṃ bhṛgurbhojakaṭipradeśassabhārgavaḥ pūrvamukhasvabhāvaḥ |
sa pañcakoṇeśarathādhirūḍho daṇḍākṣamālāvarado'mbupātraḥ ||
śvetāmbaraḥ śvetavapuḥ kirīṭī caturbhujo daityaguruḥ praśāntaḥ |
tathāsidaṇḍaṃ ca kamaṇḍaluṃ ca tathākṣasūtraṃ varado'stu mahyam ||

word by word · translation

prācyām in the east bhṛguḥ the son of Bhṛgu bhojakaṭi-pradeśaḥ of the Bhojakaṭa region saḥ he bhārgavaḥ the Bhārgava pūrva-mukha-svabhāvaḥ by nature facing east saḥ he pañca-koṇa-īśa-ratha-adhirūḍhaḥ mounted on a lordly chariot in the pentagon daṇḍa-akṣa-mālā-vara-daḥ with staff, rosary and boon-giving hand ambu-pātraḥ with a water-pot śveta-ambaraḥ white-robed śveta-vapuḥ white-bodied kirīṭī crowned catuḥ-bhujaḥ four-armed daitya-guruḥ teacher of the Daityas praśāntaḥ serene tathā likewise asi-daṇḍam sword and staff ca and kamaṇḍalum a water-pot ca and tathā likewise akṣa-sūtram a rosary vara-daḥ a giver of boons astu may he be mahyam to me

Dhyāna: in the east, Bhṛgu of the Bhojakaṭa country, of Bhārgava line, facing east, mounted in his chariot on a five-cornered seat, with staff, rosary and water-pot, granting boons. White-robed, white of body, crowned, four-armed, the calm teacher of the daityas, with sword and staff, water-pot and rosary — may he grant me boons.

oṃ śukraṃ te anyadyajataṃ te anyadviṣurūpe ahanī dyaurivāsi |
viśvā hi māyā avasi svadhāvo bhadrā te pūṣanniha rātirastu ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ śukragrahehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om śukram bright te of yours anyat is one form yajatam worthy of worship te of yours anyat is the other viṣu-rūpe of differing forms ahanī are day and night dyauḥ heaven iva like asi you are viśvāḥ all hi indeed māyāḥ wondrous powers avasi you favour svadhā-vaḥ O self-sustaining one bhadrā auspicious te your pūṣan O Pūṣan iha here rātiḥ gift astu may be om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven śukra-graha O planet Venus iha here āgaccha come

“One form of yours is bright, another worshipful; like heaven you are day and night, two in appearance. You guard all wondrous powers, O self-reliant one; Pūṣan, may your kind gift be here.” — Come here, Venus.

śukragrahaṃ śvetavarṇaṃ śvetagandhaṃ śvetapuṣpaṃ śvetamālyāmbaradharaṃ
śvetacchatradhvajapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ pūrvābhimukhaṃ padmāsanasthaṃ caturbhujaṃ
daṇḍākṣamālājaṭāvalkaladhāriṇaṃ kāmbhojadeśādhipatiṃ bhārgavasagotraṃ
pārthivasaṃvatsare śrāvaṇamāse śuklapakṣe aṣṭamyāṃ bhṛguvāsare
svātīnakṣatrajātaṃ tulāvṛṣabharāśyadhipatiṃ kirīṭinaṃ sukhāsīnaṃ
patnīputraparivārasametaṃ grahamaṇḍale praviṣṭamasminnadhikaraṇe
sūryagrahasya prāgbhāge pañcakoṇākāramaṇḍale
sthāpitasīsapratimārūpeṇa śukragrahamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

śukra-graham the planet Venus śveta-varṇam white in colour śveta-gandham with white sandal paste śveta-puṣpam with white flowers śveta-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing white garlands and garments śveta-chatra-dhvaja-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with white parasol, banner, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru pradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the right pūrva-abhimukham facing east padma-āsana-stham seated on a lotus catuḥ-bhujam four-armed daṇḍa-akṣa-mālā-jaṭā-valkala-dhāriṇam bearing staff and rosary, with matted hair and bark garments kāmbhoja-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Kāmbhoja country bhārgava-sa-gotram of the Bhārgava lineage pārthiva-saṃvatsare in the year Pārthiva śrāvaṇa-māse in the month of Śrāvaṇa śukla-pakṣe in the bright fortnight aṣṭamyām on the eighth day bhṛgu-vāsare on Friday svātī-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Svātī tulā-vṛṣabha-rāśi-adhipatim lord of the signs Libra and Taurus kirīṭinam crowned sukha-āsīnam seated at ease patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam with wife, sons and retinue graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun prāk-bhāge in the eastern quarter pañca-koṇa-ākāra-maṇḍale in a pentagonal diagram sthāpita-sīsa-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the lead image set up śukra-graham the planet Venus āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Venus — white, with white sandal, flowers, garland and garments, facing east, seated on a lotus, four-armed, bearing staff, rosary, matted hair and bark garment, lord of Kāmboja, of Bhṛgu's gotra, born in the year Pārthiva, in Śrāvaṇa, the bright eighth, a Friday, under Svātī, lord of Libra and Taurus — I invoke in the lead image set in the five-cornered maṇḍala east of the Sun.

siṃhāsanasthāṃ dvibhujāṃ svarṇābhāṃ ca susundarīm |
śilāsanāṃ śacīṃ dhyāye raktāmbujakarāmbujām ||
oṃ indrāṇīmāsu nāriṣu supatnīmahamaśravam |
na hyasyā aparaṃ cana jarasā marate patiḥ ||
śukragrahādhidevatām indrāṇīṃ sāṅgāṃ sāyudhāṃ savāhanāṃ saśaktiṃ
patiputraparivārasametāṃ śukragrahasya dakṣiṇataḥ indrāṇīmāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

siṃha-āsana-sthām seated on a lion-throne dvi-bhujām two-armed svarṇa-ābhām golden in lustre ca and su-sundarīm most beautiful śilā-āsanām on a seat of stone śacīm Śacī dhyāye I meditate on rakta-ambuja-kara-ambujām with a red lotus in her lotus hand om om indrāṇīm Indrāṇī āsu among these nāriṣu women su-patnīm as the best of wives aham I aśravam have heard na never hi indeed asyāḥ her aparam in time to come cana at all jarasā of old age marate dies patiḥ husband śukra-graha-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the planet Venus indrāṇīm Indrāṇī sa-aṅgām with her limbs sa-āyudhām her weapons sa-vāhanām her mount sa-śaktim her power pati-putra-parivāra-sametām and with husband, sons and retinue śukra-grahasya of the planet Venus dakṣiṇataḥ to the south indrāṇīm Indrāṇī āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

I meditate on Śacī, enthroned, two-armed, golden and most lovely, a red lotus in her lotus hand. “Among these women I have heard Indrāṇī called most fortunate in her husband; for her lord will never die of old age.” Indrāṇī, presiding deity of Venus, is invoked on his right.

indraṃ sitaṃ caturbāhuṃ sahasranayanojjvalam |
vajrāṅkuśādisaṃyuktapāṇiṃ dhyāyetsusaṃyutam ||
oṃ indra marutva iha pāhi somaṃ yathā śāryāte apibassutasya |
tava praṇītī tava śūra śarmannā vivāsanti kavayassuyajñāḥ ||
śukragrahapratyadhidevatām indraṃ marutvantaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
śukragrahasya uttarataḥ indraṃ marutvantamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

indram Indra sitam white catuḥ-bāhum four-armed sahasra-nayana-ujjvalam radiant with a thousand eyes vajra-aṅkuśa-ādi-saṃyukta-pāṇim whose hands hold thunderbolt, goad and the rest dhyāyet one should meditate on su-saṃyutam well composed om om indra O Indra marutvaḥ with the Maruts iha here pāhi drink somam the Soma yathā as śāryāte at Śāryāta's apibaḥ you drank sutasya of the pressed juice tava under your praṇītī guidance tava in your śūra O hero śarman shelter ā vivāsanti seek to win you kavayaḥ the sages su-yajñāḥ skilled in sacrifice śukra-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Venus indram Indra marutvantam with the Maruts sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue śukra-grahasya of the planet Venus uttarataḥ to the north indram Indra marutvantam with the Maruts āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Let one meditate on Indra, white, four-armed, blazing with a thousand eyes, holding thunderbolt and goad. “Indra, with the Maruts, drink the Soma here, as you drank the pressed juice at Śāryāta's; under your guidance, hero, in your shelter, the poets of good sacrifice seek to win you.” Indra Marutvān, counter-deity of Venus, is invoked on his left.

7 · Śanaiścara ↑

śanyariṣṭe tu samprāpte śanipūjāṃ ca kārayet |
śanidhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi prāṇipīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

śani-ariṣṭe when affliction from Saturn tu indeed samprāpte has come śani-pūjām the worship of Saturn ca then kārayet one should have performed śani-dhyānam the meditation on Saturn pravakṣyāmi I shall declare prāṇi-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of affliction to living beings

When affliction from Saturn befalls, let Saturn be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Saturn, to quiet the sufferings of living beings.

śamagniragnibhirityasya mantrasya hiḷimbhiṣi ṛṣiḥ | śanaiścaragraho devatā | uṣṇik chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitaśanaiścaragrahaprasādasiddhyarthe
śanaiścaragrahārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

śam agniḥ agnibhiḥ (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra hiḷimbhiṣiḥ Hiḷimbhiṣi ṛṣiḥ is the seer śanaiścara-grahaḥ the planet Saturn devatā is the deity uṣṇik Uṣṇih chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-śanaiścara-graha-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the planet Saturn with its presiding and co-presiding deities śanaiścara-graha-ārādhane in the worship of the planet Saturn viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “śam agnir agnibhiḥ” the seer is named Hiḷimbhiṣi, the deity Saturn, the metre uṣṇih; it is applied in the worship of Saturn.

cāpāsano gṛdhrarathassunīlaḥ pratyaṅmukhaḥ kāśyapajaḥ pratīcyām |
saśūlacāpeṣuvarapradaśca saurāṣṭradeśe prabhavaśca sauriḥ ||
nīladyutirnīlavapuḥ kirīṭī gṛdhrasthitaścāpakaro dhanuṣmān |
caturbhujassūryasutaḥ praśāntassa cāstu mahyaṃ varamandagāmī ||

word by word · translation

cāpa-āsanaḥ on a bow-shaped seat gṛdhra-rathaḥ with a vulture for chariot su-nīlaḥ deep blue pratyak-mukhaḥ facing west kāśyapa-jaḥ born of the Kāśyapa line pratīcyām in the west sa-śūla-cāpa-iṣu-vara-pradaḥ with trident, bow and arrow, and granting boons ca and saurāṣṭra-deśe in the Saurāṣṭra country prabhavaḥ born ca and sauriḥ the son of the Sun nīla-dyutiḥ of dark radiance nīla-vapuḥ dark-bodied kirīṭī crowned gṛdhra-sthitaḥ seated on a vulture cāpa-karaḥ bow in hand dhanuṣmān the archer catuḥ-bhujaḥ four-armed sūrya-sutaḥ son of the Sun praśāntaḥ serene saḥ he ca and astu may be mahyam to me vara-manda-gāmī the boon-giving slow mover

Dhyāna: in the west, on a bow-shaped seat, his chariot drawn by a vulture, deep blue, facing west, born of Kaśyapa's line, with trident, bow and arrows, granting boons — the son of the Sun, arisen in Saurāṣṭra. Blue-shining, blue of body, crowned, seated on a vulture, bow in hand, four-armed, the Sun's calm son, the slow-moving — may he be the giver of boons to me.

oṃ śamagniragnibhiskaracchaṃ nastapatu sūryaḥ |
śaṃ vāto vātvarapā apa sridhaḥ ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ śanaiścaragrahehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om śam gracious agniḥ Agni agnibhiḥ with his fires karat may he be śam graciously naḥ on us tapatu may shine sūryaḥ the Sun śam graciously vātaḥ the wind vātu may blow arapāḥ free from harm apa away sridhaḥ with the foes om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven śanaiścara-graha O planet Saturn iha here āgaccha come

“May Agni with his fires bring us good; may the Sun warm us kindly; may the wind blow gently; away with the foes.” — Come here, Saturn.

śanaiścaragrahaṃ nīlavarṇaṃ nīlagandhaṃ nīlapuṣpaṃ nīlamālyāmbaradharaṃ
nīlacchatradhvajarathapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ cāpāsanasthaṃ pratyaṅmukhaṃ gṛdhrarathaṃ
caturbhujaṃ śūlāyudhadharaṃ saurāṣṭradeśādhipatiṃ kāśyapasagotraṃ
viśvāmitraṛṣiṃ vibhavasaṃvatsare pauṣamāse śuklapakṣe navamyāṃ sthiravāsare
bharaṇīnakṣatrajātaṃ makarakumbharāśyadhipatiṃ kirīṭinaṃ sukhāsīnaṃ
patnīputraparivārasametaṃ grahamaṇḍale praviṣṭamasminnadhikaraṇe
sūryagrahasya paścimadigbhāge dhanurākāramaṇḍale
sthāpita ayaḥpratimārūpeṇa śanaiścaragrahamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

śanaiścara-graham the planet Saturn nīla-varṇam dark blue in colour nīla-gandham with dark blue sandal paste nīla-puṣpam with dark blue flowers nīla-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing dark blue garlands and garments nīla-chatra-dhvaja-ratha-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with dark blue parasol, banner, chariot, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru pradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the right cāpa-āsana-stham on a bow-shaped seat pratyak-mukham facing west gṛdhra-ratham with a vulture for chariot catuḥ-bhujam four-armed śūla-āyudha-dharam bearing a trident as weapon saurāṣṭra-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Saurāṣṭra country kāśyapa-sa-gotram of the Kāśyapa lineage viśvāmitra-ṛṣim with Viśvāmitra as his seer vibhava-saṃvatsare in the year Vibhava pauṣa-māse in the month of Pauṣa śukla-pakṣe in the bright fortnight navamyām on the ninth day sthira-vāsare on Saturday bharaṇī-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Bharaṇī makara-kumbha-rāśi-adhipatim lord of the signs Capricorn and Aquarius kirīṭinam crowned sukha-āsīnam seated at ease patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam with wife, sons and retinue graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun paścima-dik-bhāge in the western quarter dhanuḥ-ākāra-maṇḍale in a bow-shaped diagram sthāpita-ayaḥ-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the iron image set up śanaiścara-graham the planet Saturn āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Saturn — blue, with blue sandal, flowers, garland and garments, seated on a bow, facing west, in a vulture-chariot, four-armed, bearing a trident, lord of Saurāṣṭra, of Kaśyapa's gotra, born in the year Vibhava, in Pauṣa, the bright ninth, a Saturday, under Bharaṇī, lord of Capricorn and Aquarius — I invoke in the iron image set in the bow-shaped maṇḍala west of the Sun.

daṇḍapāṇiṃ yamaṃ devaṃ mahiṣottamavāhanam |
yamunābhrātaraṃ prītyā yamamāvāhayāmyaham ||
oṃ yamāya somagṃ sunuta yamāya juhutā haviḥ |
yamagṃ ha yajño gacchatyagnidūto araṅkṛtaḥ ||
śanaiścaragrahādhidevatāṃ yamaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
śanaiścaragrahasya dakṣiṇataḥ yamamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

daṇḍa-pāṇim staff in hand yamam Yama devam the god mahiṣa-uttama-vāhanam whose mount is the finest buffalo yamunā-bhrātaram brother of Yamunā prītyā with love yamam Yama āvāhayāmi invoke aham I om om yamāya for Yama somam Soma sunuta press yamāya to Yama juhuta offer haviḥ the oblation yamam to Yama ha indeed yajñaḥ the sacrifice gacchati goes agni-dūtaḥ with Agni as messenger aram-kṛtaḥ well prepared śanaiścara-graha-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the planet Saturn yamam Yama sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue śanaiścara-grahasya of the planet Saturn dakṣiṇataḥ to the south yamam Yama āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

With love I invoke Yama, staff in hand, riding a great buffalo, brother of the Yamunā. “Press Soma for Yama, offer oblation to Yama; to Yama goes the sacrifice, well prepared, with Agni as its messenger.” Yama, presiding deity of Saturn, is invoked on his right.

viriñciṃ vākpatiṃ śvetaṃ paṅkajāsanamacyutam |
akṣasrakkuṇḍikābhītivarapāṇiṃ vicintaye ||
oṃ prajāpate na tvadetānyanyo viśvā jātāni pari tā babhūva |
yatkāmāste juhumastanno astu vayagṃ syāma patayo rayīṇām ||
śanaiścaragrahapratyadhidevatāṃ prajāpatiṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
śanaiścaragrahasyottarataḥ prajāpatimāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

viriñcim Brahmā vāk-patim lord of speech śvetam white paṅkaja-āsanam seated on a lotus acyutam the unfailing akṣa-srak-kuṇḍikā-abhīti-vara-pāṇim whose hands hold rosary and water-pot and grant fearlessness and boons vicintaye I contemplate om om prajā-pate O Prajāpati na none tvat than you etāni these anyaḥ other viśvā all jātāni created things pari around tā them babhūva has encompassed yat-kāmāḥ with whatever desire te to you juhumaḥ we offer tat that naḥ ours astu may be vayam we syāma may be patayaḥ lords rayīṇām of riches śanaiścara-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Saturn prajā-patim Prajāpati sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue śanaiścara-grahasya of the planet Saturn uttarataḥ to the north prajā-patim Prajāpati āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

I contemplate Viriñci, lord of speech, white, lotus-seated, imperishable, holding rosary and water-pot, granting fearlessness and boons. “Prajāpati, none but you encompasses all these created things; may we gain what we desire in offering to you; may we be lords of riches.” Prajāpati, counter-deity of Saturn, is invoked on his left.

8 · Rāhu ↑

rāhvariṣṭe tu samprāpte rāhupūjāṃ ca kārayet |
rāhudhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi cakṣuḥpīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

rāhu-ariṣṭe when affliction from Rāhu tu indeed samprāpte has come rāhu-pūjām the worship of Rāhu ca then kārayet one should have performed rāhu-dhyānam the meditation on Rāhu pravakṣyāmi I shall declare cakṣuḥ-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of affliction of the eyes

When affliction from Rāhu befalls, let Rāhu be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Rāhu, to quiet troubles of the eyes.

kayānaścitretyasya mantrasya vāmadeva ṛṣiḥ | rāhugraho devatā | gāyatrī chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitarāhugrahaprasādasiddhyarthe
rāhugrahārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

kayā naḥ citraḥ (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra vāmadevaḥ Vāmadeva ṛṣiḥ is the seer rāhu-grahaḥ the planet Rāhu devatā is the deity gāyatrī Gāyatrī chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-rāhu-graha-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the planet Rāhu with its presiding and co-presiding deities rāhu-graha-ārādhane in the worship of the planet Rāhu viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “kayā naścitraḥ” the seer is Vāmadeva, the deity Rāhu, the metre gāyatrī; it is applied in the worship of Rāhu.

paiṭhīnaso barbaradeśajātaśśūrpāsanassiṃhagatasvabhāvaḥ |
yāmyānano nairṛtidikkarālo varapradaśśūlasacarmakhaḍgaḥ ||
nīlāmbaro nīlavapuḥ kirīṭī karālavaktraḥ karavālaśūlī |
caturbhujaścarmadharaśca rāhussiṃhādhirūḍho varado'stu mahyam ||

word by word · translation

paiṭhīnasaḥ of the Paiṭhīnasa lineage barbara-deśa-jātaḥ born in the Barbara country śūrpa-āsanaḥ on a seat shaped like a winnowing fan siṃha-gata-svabhāvaḥ by nature riding a lion yāmya-ānanaḥ facing south nairṛti-dik-karālaḥ terrible in the south-west quarter vara-pradaḥ granting boons śūla-sa-carma-khaḍgaḥ with trident, shield and sword nīla-ambaraḥ dark-robed nīla-vapuḥ dark-bodied kirīṭī crowned karāla-vaktraḥ of dreadful face karavāla-śūlī with sword and trident catuḥ-bhujaḥ four-armed carma-dharaḥ bearing a shield ca and rāhuḥ Rāhu siṃha-adhirūḍhaḥ mounted on a lion vara-daḥ a giver of boons astu may he be mahyam to me

Dhyāna: of the Paiṭhīnasa line, born in the Barbara country, seated on a winnow-shaped seat, riding a lion, facing south, terrible in the south-west, granting boons, with trident, shield and sword. Blue-robed, blue of body, crowned, fearsome-faced, bearing sword and trident, four-armed, holding a shield, mounted on a lion — may Rāhu grant me boons.

oṃ kayā naścitra ā bhuvadūtī sadāvṛdhassakhā |
kayā śaciṣṭhayā vṛtā ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ rāhugrahehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om kayā with what naḥ to us citraḥ the wondrous one ā near bhuvat will he be ūtī help sadā-vṛdhaḥ ever-increasing sakhā friend kayā with what śaciṣṭhayā most mighty vṛtā company om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven rāhu-graha O planet Rāhu iha here āgaccha come

“With what help will he come to us, the wondrous, ever-waxing friend? With what most powerful company?” — Come here, Rāhu.

rāhugrahaṃ nīlavarṇaṃ nīlagandhaṃ nīlapuṣpaṃ nīlamālyāmbaradharaṃ
nīlacchatradhvajarathapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
merumapradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ siṃhāsanaṃ nairṛtimukhaṃ śūrpāsanasthaṃ
caturbhujaṃ karālavaktraṃ khaḍgacarmadharaṃ paiṭhīnasagotraṃ
barbaradeśādhipatiṃ rākṣasanāmasaṃvatsare bhādrapadamāse kṛṣṇapakṣe
caturdaśyāṃ bhānuvāsare viśākhānakṣatrajātaṃ siṃharāśiprayuktaṃ
kirīṭinaṃ sukhāsīnaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
