word by word · translation

rāhu-graham the planet Rāhu nīla-varṇam dark blue in colour nīla-gandham with dark blue sandal paste nīla-puṣpam with dark blue flowers nīla-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing dark blue garlands and garments nīla-chatra-dhvaja-ratha-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with dark blue parasol, banner, chariot, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru apradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the left siṃha-āsanam on a lion-seat nairṛti-mukham facing south-west śūrpa-āsana-stham on a seat shaped like a winnowing fan catuḥ-bhujam four-armed karāla-vaktram of dreadful face khaḍga-carma-dharam bearing sword and shield paiṭhīnasa-gotram of the Paiṭhīnasa lineage barbara-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Barbara country rākṣasa-nāma-saṃvatsare in the year named Rākṣasa bhādrapada-māse in the month of Bhādrapada kṛṣṇa-pakṣe in the dark fortnight caturdaśyām on the fourteenth day bhānu-vāsare on Sunday viśākhā-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Viśākhā siṃha-rāśi-prayuktam joined to the sign Leo kirīṭinam crowned sukha-āsīnam seated at ease sa-śaktim with his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam with wife, sons and retinue graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun nairṛti-dik-bhāge in the south-west quarter śūrpa-ākāra-maṇḍale in a diagram shaped like a winnowing fan sthāpita-loha-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the metal image set up rāhu-graham the planet Rāhu āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Rāhu — blue, with blue sandal, flowers, garland and garments, circling Meru counter-sunwise, on a lion seat, facing south-west, on a winnow seat, four-armed, fearsome-faced, with sword and shield, of the Paiṭhīnasa gotra, lord of the Barbara country, born in the year Rākṣasa, in Bhādrapada, the dark fourteenth, a Sunday, under Viśākhā, joined with Leo — I invoke in the metal image set in the winnow-shaped maṇḍala south-west of the Sun.

nīlotpaladalaśyāmāṃ dīrghaśṛṅgopaśobhitām |
mūrdhajaṃ pāśadaṇḍaṃ ca dadhānāṃ sarvamaṅgalām ||
oṃ āyaṅgauḥ pṛśnirakramīdasadanmātaraṃ puraḥ |
pitaraṃ ca prayantsuvaḥ ||
rāhugrahādhidevatāṃ gāṃ sāṅgāṃ sāyudhāṃ savāhanāṃ saśaktiṃ
putraparivārasametāṃ rāhugrahasya dakṣiṇataḥ gāmāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

nīla-utpala-dala-śyāmām dark as a blue-lotus petal dīrgha-śṛṅga-upaśobhitām adorned with long horns mūrdha-jam a tuft of hair pāśa-daṇḍam noose and staff ca and dadhānām bearing sarva-maṅgalām wholly auspicious om om ā hither ayam this gauḥ bull pṛśniḥ the dappled akramīt has come asadat he has sat mātaram before his mother puraḥ in the east pitaram to his father ca and prayan going on suvaḥ heaven rāhu-graha-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the planet Rāhu gām the Cow sa-aṅgām with her limbs sa-āyudhām her weapons sa-vāhanām her mount sa-śaktim her power putra-parivāra-sametām and with sons and retinue rāhu-grahasya of the planet Rāhu dakṣiṇataḥ to the south gām the Cow āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

The Cow, dark as a blue lotus petal, graced with long horns, bearing noose and staff, all-auspicious. “This dappled bull has come forth and sat before the Mother, going to the Father, heaven.” The Cow, presiding deity of Rāhu, is invoked on his right.

kirīṭinaṃ dvibāhuṃ ca khaḍgacarmagadādharam |
karālavadanaṃ bhīmaṃ dīrghapucchaṃ mahābalam ||
oṃ namo astu sarpebhyo ye ke ca pṛthivīmanu |
ye antarikṣe ye divi tebhyassarpebhyo namaḥ ||
rāhugrahapratyadhidevatāṃ sarpaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
rāhugrahasya uttarataḥ sarpamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

kirīṭinam crowned dvi-bāhum two-armed ca and khaḍga-carma-gadā-dharam bearing sword, shield and mace karāla-vadanam of dreadful face bhīmam fearsome dīrgha-puccham long-tailed mahā-balam of great strength om om namaḥ salutation astu be sarpebhyaḥ to the serpents ye those ke ca whichever pṛthivīm the earth anu upon ye those antarikṣe in the mid-region ye those divi in heaven tebhyaḥ to those sarpebhyaḥ serpents namaḥ salutation rāhu-graha-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the planet Rāhu sarpam the Serpent sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue rāhu-grahasya of the planet Rāhu uttarataḥ to the north sarpam the Serpent āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

The Serpent — crowned, two-armed, with sword, shield and mace, fearsome-faced, terrible, long-tailed, of great strength. “Salutation to the serpents, whichever are on the earth, in the mid-air and in heaven: to those serpents, salutation.” The Serpent, counter-deity of Rāhu, is invoked on his left.

9 · Ketu ↑

ketvariṣṭe tu samprāpte ketupūjāṃ ca kārayet |
ketudhyānaṃ pravakṣyāmi jñānapīḍopaśāntaye ||

word by word · translation

ketu-ariṣṭe when affliction from Ketu tu indeed samprāpte has come ketu-pūjām the worship of Ketu ca then kārayet one should have performed ketu-dhyānam the meditation on Ketu pravakṣyāmi I shall declare jñāna-pīḍā-upaśāntaye for the calming of affliction of knowledge

When affliction from Ketu befalls, let Ketu be worshipped. I will tell the meditation on Ketu, to quiet troubles of understanding.

ketuṃ kṛṇvannityasya mantrasya madhucchandā ṛṣiḥ | ketugaṇo devatā | gāyatrī chandaḥ |
(mama) yajamānasyādhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahitaketugaṇaprasādasiddhyarthe
ketugaṇārādhane viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

ketum kṛṇvan (the opening words) iti thus beginning asya of this mantrasya mantra madhucchandāḥ Madhucchandas ṛṣiḥ is the seer ketu-gaṇaḥ the host of Ketus devatā is the deity gāyatrī Gāyatrī chandaḥ is the metre mama of me yajamānasya the sacrificer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita-ketu-gaṇa-prasāda-siddhi-arthe to gain the grace of the host of Ketus with its presiding and co-presiding deities ketu-gaṇa-ārādhane in the worship of the host of Ketus viniyogaḥ is its use

Of the mantra “ketuṃ kṛṇvan” the seer is Madhucchandas, the deity the host of Ketus, the metre gāyatrī; it is applied in the worship of Ketu.

dhvajāsano jaiminigotrajo'ntarvedeṣu deśeṣu vicitravarṇaḥ |
yāmyāsano vāyudiśaḥ prakhaḍgaścarmāsibhiścāṣṭasutaśca ketuḥ ||
dhūmro dvibāhurvarado gadābhṛdgṛdhrāsanastho vikṛtānanaśca |
kirīṭakeyūravibhūṣitāṅgassa cāstu me ketugaṇaḥ praśāntaḥ ||

word by word · translation

dhvaja-āsanaḥ on a banner-shaped seat jaimini-gotra-jaḥ born in the Jaimini lineage antarvedeṣu in the Antarvedi deśeṣu lands vicitra-varṇaḥ of variegated colour yāmya-āsanaḥ seated towards the south vāyu-diśaḥ of the north-west quarter pra-khaḍgaḥ with a great sword carma-asibhiḥ with shields and swords ca and aṣṭa-sutaḥ with eight sons ca and ketuḥ Ketu dhūmraḥ smoke-coloured dvi-bāhuḥ two-armed vara-daḥ giver of boons gadā-bhṛt mace-bearer gṛdhra-āsana-sthaḥ seated on a vulture vikṛta-ānanaḥ of misshapen face ca and kirīṭa-keyūra-vibhūṣita-aṅgaḥ his limbs adorned with crown and armlets saḥ he ca and astu may be me to me ketu-gaṇaḥ the host of Ketus praśāntaḥ serene

Dhyāna: on a banner-shaped seat, of Jaimini's gotra, of the Antarvedī lands, many-coloured, seated towards the south in the north-west, bearing sword and shield. Smoke-grey, two-armed, giver of boons, mace-bearing, seated on a vulture, of strange visage, adorned with crown and armlets — may the host of Ketus be calm toward me.

oṃ ketuṃ kṛṇvannaketave peśo maryā apeśase |
samuṣadbhirajāyathāḥ ||
oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ ketugaṇehāgaccha ||

word by word · translation

om om ketum a banner kṛṇvan making aketave for what had none peśaḥ form maryāḥ O men apeśase for the formless sam together uṣadbhiḥ with the dawns ajāyathāḥ you were born om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven ketu-gaṇa O host of Ketus iha here āgaccha come

“Making light where there was no light, form where there was no form, O mortals, you were born together with the dawns.” — Come here, host of Ketus.

ketugaṇaṃ citravarṇaṃ citragandhaṃ citrapuṣpaṃ citramālyāmbaradharaṃ
citracchatradhvajarathapatākādiśobhitaṃ divyarathasamārūḍhaṃ
merumapradakṣiṇīkurvāṇaṃ dhvajāsanasthaṃ dakṣiṇābhimukham
antarvedideśādhipatiṃ dvibāhuṃ gadādharaṃ jaiminigotraṃ
rākṣasanāmasaṃvatsare caitramāse kṛṣṇapakṣe caturdaśyām induvāsare
revatīnakṣatrajātaṃ karkaṭakarāśiprayuktaṃ siṃhāsanāsīnaṃ
grahamaṇḍale praviṣṭamasminnadhikaraṇe sūryagrahasya vāyavyadigbhāge
dhvajākāramaṇḍale sthāpitapañcalohapratimārūpeṇa ketugaṇamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

ketu-gaṇam the host of Ketus citra-varṇam many-coloured citra-gandham with many-coloured sandal paste citra-puṣpam with many-coloured flowers citra-mālya-ambara-dharam wearing many-coloured garlands and garments citra-chatra-dhvaja-ratha-patākā-ādi-śobhitam adorned with many-coloured parasol, banner, chariot, flags and the rest divya-ratha-samārūḍham mounted on a divine chariot merum Mount Meru apradakṣiṇī-kurvāṇam circling to the left dhvaja-āsana-stham on a banner-shaped seat dakṣiṇa-abhimukham facing south antarvedi-deśa-adhipatim lord of the Antarvedi country dvi-bāhum two-armed gadā-dharam bearing a mace jaimini-gotram of the Jaimini lineage rākṣasa-nāma-saṃvatsare in the year named Rākṣasa caitra-māse in the month of Caitra kṛṣṇa-pakṣe in the dark fortnight caturdaśyām on the fourteenth day indu-vāsare on Monday revatī-nakṣatra-jātam born under the star Revatī karkaṭaka-rāśi-prayuktam joined to the sign Cancer siṃha-āsana-āsīnam seated on a lion-throne graha-maṇḍale the circle of planets praviṣṭam who has entered asmin on this adhikaraṇe base sūrya-grahasya of the planet Sun vāyavya-dik-bhāge in the north-west quarter dhvaja-ākāra-maṇḍale in a banner-shaped diagram sthāpita-pañca-loha-pratimā-rūpeṇa in the form of the five-metal image set up ketu-gaṇam the host of Ketus āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

The host of Ketus — many-coloured, with varied sandal, flowers, garland and garments, circling Meru counter-sunwise, on a banner seat, facing south, lord of Antarvedī, two-armed, mace-bearing, of Jaimini's gotra, born in the year Rākṣasa, in Caitra, the dark fourteenth, a Monday, under Revatī, joined with Cancer, seated on a lion throne — I invoke in the five-metal image set in the banner-shaped maṇḍala north-west of the Sun.

citraguptaṃ mahāprājñaṃ vetradhāriṇamavyayam |
raktavarṇāmbaradharaṃ sarvapāpaharaṃ vibhum ||
oṃ sacitra citraṃ citayantamasme citrakṣatra citratamaṃ vayodhām |
candraṃ rayiṃ puruvīraṃ bṛhantaṃ candra candrābhirgṛṇate yuvasva ||
ketugaṇādhidevatāṃ citraguptaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
ketugaṇasya dakṣiṇataḥ citraguptamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

citraguptam Citragupta mahā-prājñam greatly wise vetra-dhāriṇam bearing a cane avyayam imperishable rakta-varṇa-ambara-dharam wearing red garments sarva-pāpa-haram remover of all sin vibhum the lord om om saḥ as such citra O wondrous one citram wondrous citayantam conspicuous asme to us citra-kṣatra O you of wondrous dominion citra-tamam most wondrous vayaḥ-dhām giving vigour candram shining rayim wealth puru-vīram with many heroes bṛhantam great candra O shining one candrābhiḥ with shining gifts gṛṇate to the singer yuvasva grant ketu-gaṇa-adhidevatām the presiding deity of the host of Ketus citraguptam Citragupta sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue ketu-gaṇasya of the host of Ketus dakṣiṇataḥ to the south citraguptam Citragupta āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

Citragupta, greatly wise, cane-bearing, unfailing, in red garments, remover of all sin, the lord. “O bright one of bright dominion, bring us bright, shining wealth that gives vigour, rich in heroes, great; O radiant one, with your radiance draw it to the one who praises you.” Citragupta, presiding deity of Ketu, is invoked on his right.

brahmāṇaṃ raktagaurāṅgaṃ caturvaktraṃ jagatprabhum |
akṣasrakkuṇḍikābhītivarapāṇiṃ vicintaye ||
oṃ brahmā devānāṃ padavīḥ kavīnāmṛṣirviprāṇāṃ mahiṣo mṛgāṇām |
śyeno gṛdhrāṇāgṃ svadhitirvanānāgṃ somaḥ pavitramatyeti rebhan ||
ketugaṇapratyadhidevatāṃ brahmāṇaṃ sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ
ketugaṇasyottarataḥ brahmāṇamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

brahmāṇam Brahmā rakta-gaura-aṅgam of ruddy-fair limbs catuḥ-vaktram four-faced jagat-prabhum lord of the world akṣa-srak-kuṇḍikā-abhīti-vara-pāṇim whose hands hold rosary and water-pot and grant fearlessness and boons vicintaye I contemplate om om brahmā the priest devānām of the gods pada-vīḥ the leader kavīnām of poets ṛṣiḥ the seer viprāṇām of the inspired mahiṣaḥ the buffalo mṛgāṇām of beasts śyenaḥ the falcon gṛdhrāṇām of birds of prey sva-dhitiḥ the axe vanānām of forests somaḥ Soma pavitram the strainer atyeti passes through rebhan singing ketu-gaṇa-pratyadhidevatām the co-presiding deity of the host of Ketus brahmāṇam Brahmā sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue ketu-gaṇasya of the host of Ketus uttarataḥ to the north brahmāṇam Brahmā āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

I contemplate Brahmā … “Brahmā among the gods …” Brahmā, counter-deity of Ketu, is invoked on his left.

adhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahita ādityādinavagrahadevatābhyo namaḥ |
dhyāyāmi āvāhayāmi ratnasiṃhāsanaṃ samarpayāmi pādyaṃ samarpayāmi
arghyaṃ samarpayāmi ācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi snānaṃ samarpayāmi
śuddhācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi vastraṃ samarpayāmi yajñopavītaṃ samarpayāmi
gandhaṃ samarpayāmi akṣatān samarpayāmi puṣpāṇi samarpayāmi
dhūpamāghrāpayāmi dīpaṃ darśayāmi naivedyaṃ samarpayāmi
tāmbūlaṃ samarpayāmi mantrapuṣpaṃ samarpayāmi ||
adhidevatāpratyadhidevatāsahita ādityādinavagrahadevatāprasādasiddhirastu ||

word by word · translation

adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita together with their presiding and co-presiding deities āditya-ādi-nava-graha-devatābhyaḥ to the deities of the nine planets, the Sun and the rest namaḥ salutation dhyāyāmi I meditate on them āvāhayāmi I invoke them ratna-siṃha-āsanam a jewelled lion-throne samarpayāmi I offer pādyam water for the feet samarpayāmi I offer arghyam water of welcome samarpayāmi I offer ācamanīyam water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer snānam a bath samarpayāmi I offer śuddha-ācamanīyam pure water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer vastram a garment samarpayāmi I offer yajña-upavītam the sacred thread samarpayāmi I offer gandham sandal paste samarpayāmi I offer akṣatān unbroken rice samarpayāmi I offer puṣpāṇi flowers samarpayāmi I offer dhūpam incense āghrāpayāmi I offer to be smelt dīpam a lamp darśayāmi I show naivedyam food samarpayāmi I offer tāmbūlam betel samarpayāmi I offer mantra-puṣpam the flower of mantra samarpayāmi I offer adhidevatā-pratyadhidevatā-sahita together with their presiding and co-presiding deities āditya-ādi-nava-graha-devatā-prasāda-siddhiḥ the gaining of the grace of the deities of the nine planets, the Sun and the rest astu may there be

Salutation to the nine planets beginning with the Sun, with their presiding and counter-deities; the sixteen services are offered. May their grace be gained.

❧

Aṣṭadikpāla Pūjā · the guardians of the quarters ↑

Around the planets, the guardians are set in their quarters: Indra east, Agni south-east, Yama south, Nirṛti south-west, Varuṇa west, Vāyu north-west, Kubera north and Īśāna north-east.

oṃ indraṃ vo viśvataspari havāmahe janebhyaḥ | asmākamastu kevalaḥ ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ indraṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om indram Indra vaḥ for you viśvataḥ from every side pari above havāmahe we call janebhyaḥ all peoples asmākam ours astu may he be kevalaḥ alone sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue indram Indra dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

1. Indra: “We call Indra for you from among all peoples; may he be ours alone.” I invoke, install and worship Indra as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ agniṃ dūtaṃ vṛṇīmahe hotāraṃ viśvavedasam | asya yajñasya sukratum ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ agniṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om agnim Agni dūtam as messenger vṛṇīmahe we choose hotāram the invoker viśva-vedasam who knows all asya of this yajñasya sacrifice su-kratum the skilful one sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue agnim Agni dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

2. Agni: “We choose Agni as messenger …” I invoke Agni as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ yamāya somagṃ sunuta yamāya juhutā haviḥ |
yamagṃ ha yajño gacchatyagnidūto araṅkṛtaḥ ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ yamaṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om yamāya for Yama somam Soma sunuta press yamāya to Yama juhuta offer haviḥ the oblation yamam to Yama ha indeed yajñaḥ the sacrifice gacchati goes agni-dūtaḥ with Agni as messenger aram-kṛtaḥ well prepared sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue yamam Yama dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

3. Yama: “Press Soma for Yama …” I invoke Yama as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ mo ṣu ṇaḥ parāparā nirṛtirdurhaṇā vadhīt | padīṣṭa tṛṣṇayā saha ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ nirṛtiṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om mo let not su indeed naḥ us parā-parā further and further nirṛtiḥ Nirṛti durhaṇā hard to overcome vadhīt slay padīṣṭa may she fall away tṛṣṇayā thirst saha together with sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue nirṛtim Nirṛti dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

4. Nirṛti: “Let not Nirṛti, hard to strike down, smite us from far and near; may she perish together with thirst.” I invoke Nirṛti as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ imaṃ me varuṇa śrudhī havamadyā ca mṛḍaya | tvāmavasyurācake ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ varuṇaṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om imam this me my varuṇa O Varuṇa śrudhi hear havam call adya today ca and mṛḍaya be gracious tvām you avasyuḥ seeking help ā cake I long for sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue varuṇam Varuṇa dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

5. Varuṇa: “Hear this call of mine, Varuṇa, and be gracious today; seeking help, I long for you.” I invoke Varuṇa as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ tava vāyavṛtaspate tvaṣṭurjāmātaradbhuta | avāgṃsyā vṛṇīmahe ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ vāyuṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tava yours vāyo O Vāyu ṛtaḥ-pate lord of order tvaṣṭuḥ of Tvaṣṭṛ jāmātaḥ O son-in-law adbhuta wondrous one avāṃsi the helps ā vṛṇīmahe we choose sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue vāyum Vāyu dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

6. Vāyu: “Wondrous Vāyu, lord of order, son-in-law of Tvaṣṭṛ, we choose your protection.” I invoke Vāyu as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ somo dhenugṃ somo arvantamāśugṃ somo vīraṃ karmaṇyaṃ dadāti |
sādanyaṃ vidathyagṃ sabheyaṃ pitṛśravaṇaṃ yo dadāśadasmai ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ kuberaṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om somaḥ Soma dhenum a milch cow somaḥ Soma arvantam a steed āśum swift somaḥ Soma vīram a hero karmaṇyam fit for work dadāti gives sādanyam fit for the home vidathyam fit for the assembly sabheyam fit for the council pitṛ-śravaṇam bringing fame to his father yaḥ who dadāśat worships asmai to him sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue kuberam Kubera dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

7. Kubera: “Soma gives a milch-cow, Soma a swift steed, Soma a hero skilled in work — fit for home, for council and assembly, the glory of his father — to whoever worships him.” I invoke Kubera as guardian of the quarter.

oṃ tamīśānaṃ jagatastasthuṣaspatiṃ dhiyañjinvamavase hūmahe vayam |
pūṣā no yathā vedasāmasadvṛdhe rakṣitā pāyuradabdhassvastaye ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ īśānaṃ dikpālakamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tam him īśānam the ruler jagataḥ of what moves tasthuṣaḥ and what stands patim the lord dhiyam-jinvam who quickens thought avase for help hūmahe call vayam we pūṣā Pūṣan naḥ our yathā so that vedasām of wealth asat he may be vṛdhe for the increase rakṣitā protector pāyuḥ guardian adabdhaḥ undeceived svastaye for well-being sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue īśānam Īśāna dik-pālakam guardian of the quarter āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

8. Īśāna: “We call for help on that Lord, master of the moving and the unmoving, who quickens thought; that Pūṣan may be for the increase of our wealth, our guardian and protector, unassailable, for our welfare.” I invoke Īśāna as guardian of the quarter.

indrādyaṣṭadikpālakadevatābhyo namaḥ |
dhyāyāmi āvāhayāmi ratnasiṃhāsanaṃ samarpayāmi pādyaṃ samarpayāmi
arghyaṃ samarpayāmi ācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi snapayāmi
vastraṃ samarpayāmi yajñopavītaṃ samarpayāmi gandhaṃ samarpayāmi
akṣatān samarpayāmi puṣpāṇi samarpayāmi dhūpamāghrāpayāmi
dīpaṃ darśayāmi naivedyaṃ samarpayāmi tāmbūlaṃ samarpayāmi
mantrapuṣpaṃ samarpayāmi ||
indrādyaṣṭadikpālakadevatāprasādasiddhirastu ||

word by word · translation

indra-ādi-aṣṭa-dik-pālaka-devatābhyaḥ to the eight guardian deities of the quarters, Indra and the rest namaḥ salutation dhyāyāmi I meditate on them āvāhayāmi I invoke them ratna-siṃha-āsanam a jewelled lion-throne samarpayāmi I offer pādyam water for the feet samarpayāmi I offer arghyam water of welcome samarpayāmi I offer ācamanīyam water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer snapayāmi I bathe them vastram a garment samarpayāmi I offer yajña-upavītam the sacred thread samarpayāmi I offer gandham sandal paste samarpayāmi I offer akṣatān unbroken rice samarpayāmi I offer puṣpāṇi flowers samarpayāmi I offer dhūpam incense āghrāpayāmi I offer to be smelt dīpam a lamp darśayāmi I show naivedyam food samarpayāmi I offer tāmbūlam betel samarpayāmi I offer mantra-puṣpam the flower of mantra samarpayāmi I offer indra-ādi-aṣṭa-dik-pālaka-devatā-prasāda-siddhiḥ the gaining of the grace of the eight guardian deities of the quarters, Indra and the rest astu may there be

Salutation to the eight guardians of the quarters beginning with Indra; the services are offered. May their grace be gained.

❧

Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa Pūjā ↑

Set the image of Satyanārāyaṇa on a betel leaf and cleanse it with the five nectars in turn — milk, curd, ghee, honey — and then pure water.

āpyāyasveti kṣīram ||
oṃ āpyāyasva sametu te viśvatassoma vṛṣṇiyam | bhavā vājasya saṅgathe ||

word by word · translation

āpyāyasva (the opening words) iti with this kṣīram milk om om āpyāyasva swell sametu may there gather te in you viśvataḥ from every side soma O Soma vṛṣṇiyam vigour bhava be vājasya of strength saṅgathe at the gathering

With “āpyāyasva”, milk: “Swell, O Soma; let vigour gather to you from every side; be present where strength is won.”

dadhikrāvṇṇa iti dadhi ||
dadhikrāvṇṇo akāriṣaṃ jiṣṇoraśvasya vājinaḥ |
surabhi no mukhā karatpra ṇa āyūgṃṣi tāriṣat ||

word by word · translation

dadhikrāvṇaḥ (the opening words) iti with this dadhi curd dadhikrāvṇaḥ of Dadhikrāvan akāriṣam I have sung jiṣṇoḥ the victorious aśvasya horse vājinaḥ the swift surabhi fragrant naḥ our mukhā mouths karat may he make pra forth naḥ our āyūṃṣi lives tāriṣat may he lengthen

With “dadhikrāvṇaḥ”, curd: “I have praised Dadhikrāvan, the victorious, swift steed; may he make our mouths fragrant and lengthen our lives.”

śukramasītyājyam ||
śukramasi jyotirasi tejo'si devo vassavitotpunātvacchidreṇa
pavitreṇa vasossūryasya raśmibhiḥ ||

word by word · translation

śukram asi (the opening words) iti with this ājyam ghee śukram bright asi you are jyotiḥ light asi you are tejaḥ splendour asi you are devaḥ may the god vaḥ you savitā Savitṛ utpunātu purify acchidreṇa with the flawless pavitreṇa strainer vasoḥ of the good sūryasya sun raśmibhiḥ with the rays

With “śukramasi”, ghee: “You are bright, you are light, you are splendour; may god Savitṛ purify you with the flawless strainer, with the rays of the good sun.”

madhu vātā ṛtāyateti madhu ||
madhu vātā ṛtāyate madhu kṣaranti sindhavaḥ | mādhvīrnassantvoṣadhīḥ |
madhu naktamutoṣasi madhumatpārthivagṃ rajaḥ | madhu dyaurastu naḥ pitā |
madhumānno vanaspatirmadhumāgṃ astu sūryaḥ | mādhvīrgāvo bhavantu naḥ ||

word by word · translation

madhu vātāḥ ṛtāyate (the opening words) iti with this madhu honey madhu sweetly vātāḥ the winds blow ṛtāyate for one who keeps the order madhu sweetness kṣaranti pour sindhavaḥ the rivers mādhvīḥ sweet naḥ for us santu may be oṣadhīḥ the plants madhu sweet naktam be the night uta and uṣasi the dawns madhu-mat full of sweetness pārthivam the earthly rajaḥ air madhu sweet dyauḥ Heaven astu may be naḥ our pitā father madhu-mān full of sweetness naḥ for us vanaspatiḥ be the tree madhu-mān full of sweetness astu may be sūryaḥ the sun mādhvīḥ sweet gāvaḥ the cows bhavantu may be naḥ for us

With “madhu vātāḥ”, honey: “For the one who keeps the order the winds blow honey, the rivers stream honey; sweet be the plants to us. Honey by night and at dawn, sweet the dust of the earth, sweet be Heaven our father; sweet be the tree to us, sweet the sun, sweet the cows.”

svāduḥ pavasveti śuddhodakam ||
svāduḥ pavasva divyāya janmane svādurindrāya suhavītu nāmne |
svādurmitrāya varuṇāya vāyave bṛhaspataye madhumāgṃ adābhyaḥ ||

word by word · translation

svāduḥ pavasva (the opening words) iti with this śuddha-udakam pure water svāduḥ sweet pavasva flow divyāya for the divine janmane race svāduḥ sweet indrāya for Indra su-havītu-nāmne whose name is good to invoke svāduḥ sweet mitrāya for Mitra varuṇāya Varuṇa vāyave Vāyu bṛhaspataye for Bṛhaspati madhu-mān full of honey adābhyaḥ never to be deceived

With “svāduḥ pavasva”, pure water: “Flow sweet for the heavenly race, sweet for Indra whose name is good to call, sweet for Mitra, Varuṇa and Vāyu, for Bṛhaspati, honeyed and unharmed.”

āpo hi ṣṭheti śuddhodakasnānam ||
āpo hi ṣṭhā mayobhuvastā na ūrje dadhātana mahe raṇāya cakṣase |
yo vaśśivatamo rasastasya bhājayateha naḥ uśatīriva mātaraḥ |
tasmā araṃ gamāma vo yasya kṣayāya jinvatha āpo janayathā ca naḥ ||

word by word · translation

āpaḥ hi sthā (the opening words) iti with this śuddha-udaka-snānam the bath in pure water āpaḥ O waters hi indeed sthā you are mayaḥ-bhuvaḥ bringers of joy tāḥ as such naḥ us ūrje to strength dadhātana set mahe to great raṇāya delight cakṣase and to vision yaḥ which vaḥ your śiva-tamaḥ most auspicious rasaḥ essence tasya of that bhājayata give a share iha here naḥ to us uśatīḥ loving iva like mātaraḥ mothers tasmai to him aram readily gamāma may we go vaḥ for you yasya whose kṣayāya abode jinvatha you quicken āpaḥ O waters janayatha you bring forth ca and naḥ us

With “āpo hi ṣṭhā”, the bath of pure water: “O waters, you are the source of well-being …”

Prāṇapratiṣṭhā · installing the life-breath ↑

prāṇapratiṣṭhāpanaṃ kariṣye |
oṃ asya śrīprāṇapratiṣṭhāpanamahāmantrasya brahmaviṣṇumaheśvarā ṛṣayaḥ |
ṛgyajussāmātharvaṇāni chandāṃsi | prāṇaśśaktiḥ parā devatā |
hrāṃ bījaṃ | hrīṃ śaktiḥ | kroṃ kīlakam |
śrīsatyanārāyaṇaprāṇapratiṣṭhājape viniyogaḥ ||

word by word · translation

prāṇa-pratiṣṭhāpanam the installing of the life-breath kariṣye I shall perform om om asya of this śrī-prāṇa-pratiṣṭhāpana-mahā-mantrasya great mantra for installing the life-breath brahma-viṣṇu-maheśvarāḥ Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśvara ṛṣayaḥ are the seers ṛk-yajuḥ-sāma-atharvaṇāni Ṛk, Yajus, Sāman and Atharvan chandāṃsi are the metres prāṇa-śaktiḥ the Power of life parā the supreme devatā is the deity hrām hrāṃ bījam is the seed hrīm hrīṃ śaktiḥ is the power krom kroṃ kīlakam is the pin śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-prāṇa-pratiṣṭhā-jape in the recitation for installing life in Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa viniyogaḥ is its use

I will install the life-breath. Of this great mantra the seers are Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśvara; the metres the four Vedas; the deity the supreme power of breath; hrāṃ the seed, hrīṃ the power, kroṃ the pin; it is applied to installing the life of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa.

karanyāsaḥ — hrāṃ aṅguṣṭhābhyāṃ namaḥ | hrīṃ tarjanībhyāṃ namaḥ |
hrūṃ madhyamābhyāṃ namaḥ | hraiṃ anāmikābhyāṃ namaḥ |
hrauṃ kaniṣṭhikābhyāṃ namaḥ | hraḥ karatalakarapṛṣṭhābhyāṃ namaḥ ||
aṅganyāsaḥ — hrāṃ hṛdayāya namaḥ | hrīṃ śirase svāhā |
hrūṃ śikhāyai vaṣaṭ | hraiṃ kavacāya hum |
hrauṃ netratrayāya vauṣaṭ | hraḥ astrāya phaṭ ||
bhūrbhuvassuvaromiti digbandhaḥ ||

word by word · translation

kara-nyāsaḥ placing on the hands hrām hrāṃ aṅguṣṭhābhyām to the thumbs namaḥ salutation hrīm hrīṃ tarjanībhyām to the forefingers namaḥ salutation hrūm hrūṃ madhyamābhyām to the middle fingers namaḥ salutation hraim hraiṃ anāmikābhyām to the ring fingers namaḥ salutation hraum hrauṃ kaniṣṭhikābhyām to the little fingers namaḥ salutation hraḥ hraḥ kara-tala-kara-pṛṣṭhābhyām to the palms and backs of the hands namaḥ salutation aṅga-nyāsaḥ placing on the limbs hrām hrāṃ hṛdayāya to the heart namaḥ salutation hrīm hrīṃ śirase to the head svāhā hail hrūm hrūṃ śikhāyai to the top-knot vaṣaṭ vaṣaṭ hraim hraiṃ kavacāya to the armour hum hum hraum hrauṃ netra-trayāya to the three eyes vauṣaṭ vauṣaṭ hraḥ hraḥ astrāya to the weapon phaṭ phaṭ bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ om earth, mid-region, heaven, om iti thus dik-bandhaḥ the sealing of the quarters

The placings on the hands — thumbs, forefingers, middle, ring and little fingers, palms and backs — and on the body — heart, head, crown of the head, the armour of the arms, the three eyes, and the weapon; then the quarters are sealed with “bhūr bhuvaḥ suvar om”.

śāntākāraṃ bhujagaśayanaṃ padmanābhaṃ sureśaṃ
viśvākāraṃ gaganasadṛśaṃ meghavarṇaṃ śubhāṅgam |
lakṣmīkāntaṃ kamalanayanaṃ yogihṛddhyānagamyaṃ
vande viṣṇuṃ bhavabhayaharaṃ sarvalokaikanātham ||

word by word · translation

śānta-ākāram of peaceful form bhujaga-śayanam resting on the serpent padma-nābham lotus-navelled sura-īśam lord of the gods viśva-ākāram whose form is the universe gagana-sadṛśam like the sky megha-varṇam cloud-coloured śubha-aṅgam of auspicious limbs lakṣmī-kāntam beloved of Lakṣmī kamala-nayanam lotus-eyed yogi-hṛt-dhyāna-gamyam reached by yogis through meditation in the heart vande I salute viṣṇum Viṣṇu bhava-bhaya-haram remover of the fear of worldly existence sarva-loka-eka-nātham sole lord of all the worlds

Dhyāna: I bow to Viṣṇu — of peaceful form, lying on the serpent, lotus-naveled, lord of the gods, the universe his form, like the sky, cloud-coloured, of lovely limbs, Lakṣmī's beloved, lotus-eyed, reached by yogins in the heart's meditation, remover of the fear of worldly life, sole lord of all the worlds.

oṃ hrāṃ hrīṃ kroṃ aṃ yaṃ raṃ laṃ vaṃ śaṃ ṣaṃ saṃ haṃ ḷaṃ kṣaṃ
śrīsatyanārāyaṇaprāṇa iha prāṇa |
oṃ hrāṃ hrīṃ kroṃ śrīsatyanārāyaṇasarvendriyāṇi
vāṅmanastvakcakṣuśśrotrajihvāghrāṇa
ihaivāgatya sukhaṃ ciraṃ tiṣṭhantu svāhā ||

word by word · translation

om om hrām hrāṃ hrīm hrīṃ krom kroṃ am yam ram lam vam śam ṣam sam ham ḷam kṣam the seed syllables śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-prāṇaḥ the life-breath of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa iha here prāṇaḥ is the life-breath om om hrām hrāṃ hrīm hrīṃ krom kroṃ śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-sarva-indriyāṇi all the senses of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa vāk-manaḥ-tvak-cakṣuḥ-śrotra-jihvā-ghrāṇa speech, mind, skin, eye, ear, tongue and nose iha here eva itself āgatya having come sukham happily ciram long tiṣṭhantu may they stay svāhā hail

With the seed-syllables: the life of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa is here; may all his senses — speech, mind, touch, sight, hearing, taste and smell — come here and dwell long and happily. Svāhā.

oṃ asunīte punarasmāsu cakṣuḥ punaḥ prāṇamiha no dhehi bhogam |
jyokpaśyema sūryamuccarantamanumate mṛḍayā nassvasti ||
amṛtaṃ vai prāṇā amṛtamāpaḥ prāṇāneva yathāsthānamupahvayate ||
sāṅgaṃ sāyudhaṃ savāhanaṃ saśaktiṃ patnīputraparivārasametaṃ śrīsatyanārāyaṇamāvāhayāmi sthāpayāmi pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om asu-nīte O guide of life punaḥ again asmāsu in us cakṣuḥ sight punaḥ again prāṇam breath iha here naḥ to us dhehi grant bhogam enjoyment jyok long paśyema may we see sūryam the sun uccarantam rising anumate O Anumati mṛḍaya be gracious naḥ to us svasti with well-being amṛtam immortal vai truly prāṇāḥ are the breaths amṛtam immortal āpaḥ are the waters prāṇān the breaths eva indeed yathā-sthānam each to its place upahvayate he calls sa-aṅgam with his limbs sa-āyudham his weapons sa-vāhanam his mount sa-śaktim his power patnī-putra-parivāra-sametam and with wife, sons and retinue śrī-satyanārāyaṇam Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa āvāhayāmi I invoke sthāpayāmi I install pūjayāmi I worship

“O Asunīti, give us again sight, again breath here, and enjoyment; long may we see the sun rising; Anumati, be gracious to us, for our welfare.” The breaths are immortal, the waters are immortal: so he calls the breaths each to its place. With his limbs, weapons, mount and power, with consort, sons and retinue, I invoke, install and worship Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa.

dhyāyetsatyaṃ guṇātītaṃ guṇatrayasamanvitam |
lokanāthaṃ trilokeśaṃ kaustubhābharaṇaṃ harim ||
pītāmbaraṃ nīlavarṇaṃ śrīvatsapadabhūṣitam |
govindaṃ gokulānandaṃ brahmādyairabhipūjitam ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ dhyānaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

dhyāyet one should meditate on satyam Satya guṇa-atītam beyond the qualities guṇa-traya-samanvitam yet possessed of the three qualities loka-nātham lord of the world tri-loka-īśam ruler of the three worlds kaustubha-ābharaṇam adorned with the Kaustubha harim Hari pīta-ambaram yellow-robed nīla-varṇam dark in colour śrīvatsa-pada-bhūṣitam adorned with the Śrīvatsa mark govindam Govinda gokula-ānandam joy of Gokula brahma-ādyaiḥ by Brahmā and the others abhipūjitam worshipped ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation dhyānam meditation samarpayāmi I offer

Dhyāna: let one meditate on Satya, beyond the three qualities yet endowed with them, lord of the world, master of the three worlds, Hari adorned with the Kaustubha gem, yellow-robed, blue of hue, marked with the Śrīvatsa, Govinda, joy of Gokula, worshipped by Brahmā and the gods. — Salutation to Śrī Vīra Veṅkaṭa Satyanārāyaṇa Svāmi, with Ādyā Mahālakṣmī: I offer meditation.

Āvāhanam · invocation ↑

oṃ sahasraśīrṣā puruṣaḥ | sahasrākṣassahasrapāt |
sa bhūmiṃ viśvato vṛtvā | atyatiṣṭhaddaśāṅgulam ||
hiraṇyavarṇāṃ hariṇīṃ suvarṇarajatasrajām |
candrāṃ hiraṇmayīṃ lakṣmīṃ jātavedo mamāvaha ||
jyotiśśāntaṃ sarvalokāntarastham oṅkārākhyaṃ yogihṛddhyānagamyam |
sāṅgaṃ śaktiṃ sāyudhaṃ bhaktisevyaṃ sarvākāraṃ viṣṇumāvāhayāmi ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ āvāhayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om sahasra-śīrṣā thousand-headed puruṣaḥ is the Person sahasra-akṣaḥ thousand-eyed sahasra-pāt thousand-footed saḥ he bhūmim the earth viśvataḥ on every side vṛtvā covering atyatiṣṭhat stood beyond it daśa-aṅgulam by ten fingers hiraṇya-varṇām golden-hued hariṇīm like a doe suvarṇa-rajata-srajām garlanded with gold and silver candrām moon-bright hiraṇmayīm golden lakṣmīm Lakṣmī jāta-vedaḥ O Jātavedas mama for me āvaha bring hither jyotiḥ the light śāntam serene sarva-loka-antara-stham dwelling within all the worlds oṅkāra-ākhyam named Oṃ yogi-hṛt-dhyāna-gamyam reached by yogis through meditation in the heart sa-aṅgam with his limbs śaktim his power sa-āyudham his weapons bhakti-sevyam served through devotion sarva-ākāram of every form viṣṇum Viṣṇu āvāhayāmi I invoke ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation āvāhayāmi I invoke

Puruṣa Sūkta 1: “The Puruṣa has a thousand heads, a thousand eyes, a thousand feet; enveloping the earth on every side, he stands beyond it by ten fingers.” Śrī Sūkta 1: “Bring me, Jātavedas, Lakṣmī — golden of hue, the doe, wearing garlands of gold and silver, moon-like, made of gold.” The light, peaceful, dwelling within all worlds, called Oṃ, reached in the yogin's heart — with his limbs, power and weapons, served by devotion, whose form is everything: Viṣṇu I invoke.

Āsanam · seat ↑

oṃ puruṣa evedagṃ sarvam | yadbhūtaṃ yacca bhavyam |
utāmṛtatvasyeśānaḥ | yadannenātirohati ||
tāṃ ma āvaha jātavedo lakṣmīmanapagāminīm |
yasyāṃ hiraṇyaṃ vindeyaṃ gāmaśvaṃ puruṣānaham ||
kalpadrumūle maṇivedimadhye siṃhāsanaṃ svarṇamayaṃ vicitram |
vicitravastrāvṛtamacyuta prabho gṛhāṇa lakṣmīdharaṇīsamanvita ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ ratnasiṃhāsanaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om puruṣaḥ the Person eva alone idam is this sarvam all yat what bhūtam has been yat what ca and bhavyam is to be uta and amṛtatvasya of immortality īśānaḥ the lord yat which annena by food atirohati grows beyond tām her me for me āvaha bring hither jāta-vedaḥ O Jātavedas lakṣmīm Lakṣmī anapagāminīm who never departs yasyām in whom hiraṇyam gold vindeyam may I win gām cattle aśvam horses puruṣān men aham I kalpa-dru-mūle at the foot of the wish-fulfilling tree maṇi-vedi-madhye in the midst of a jewelled dais siṃha-āsanam a lion-throne svarṇa-mayam made of gold vicitram wondrous vicitra-vastra-āvṛtam covered with wondrous cloth acyuta O Acyuta prabho O Lord gṛhāṇa accept lakṣmī-dharaṇī-samanvita accompanied by Lakṣmī and the Earth ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation ratna-siṃha-āsanam a jewelled lion-throne samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 2: “The Puruṣa alone is all this, what has been and what is to be; he is lord of immortality, which he transcends through food.” Śrī Sūkta 2: “Bring me, Jātavedas, that Lakṣmī who does not depart, in whom I may find gold, cattle, horses and people.” At the foot of the wishing-tree, on a jewelled altar, a golden throne of many colours, spread with rich cloths — accept it, Lord Acyuta, with Lakṣmī and Earth. — I offer a jewelled throne.

Pādyam · water for the feet ↑

oṃ etāvānasya mahimā | ato jyāyāgṃśca pūruṣaḥ |
pādo'sya viśvā bhūtāni | tripādasyāmṛtaṃ divi ||
aśvapūrvāṃ rathamadhyāṃ hastinādaprabodhinīm |
śriyaṃ devīmupahvaye śrīrmā devī juṣatām ||
nārāyaṇa namaste'stu narakārṇavatāraka |
pādyaṃ gṛhāṇa deveśa mama saukhyaṃ vivardhaya ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ pādayoḥ pādyaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om etāvān so great asya is his mahimā greatness ataḥ than this jyāyān greater ca and pūruṣaḥ is the Person pādaḥ one quarter asya of him viśvā are all bhūtāni beings tri-pāt three quarters asya of him amṛtam are the immortal divi in heaven aśva-pūrvām with horses before her ratha-madhyām chariots in her midst hasti-nāda-prabodhinīm awakened by the trumpeting of elephants śriyam Śrī devīm the goddess upahvaye I call near śrīḥ Śrī mā me devī the goddess juṣatām may she favour nārāyaṇa O Nārāyaṇa namaḥ salutation te to you astu be naraka-arṇava-tāraka who carry us across the ocean of hell pādyam water for the feet gṛhāṇa accept deva-īśa lord of gods mama my saukhyam happiness vivardhaya increase ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation pādayoḥ at his feet pādyam water for the feet samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 3: “Such is his greatness, and the Puruṣa is greater still; all beings are a quarter of him, three quarters the immortal in heaven.” Śrī Sūkta 3: “I call the goddess Śrī, with horses before and chariots in the midst, roused by the trumpeting of elephants; may the goddess Śrī delight in me.” Salutation, Nārāyaṇa, who carries us across the sea of hell; accept water for your feet, lord of gods, and increase my happiness.

Arghyam · water for the hands ↑

oṃ tripādūrdhva udaitpuruṣaḥ | pādo'syehābhavātpunaḥ |
tato viṣvaṅvyakrāmat | sāśanānaśane abhi ||
kāṃ so'smitāṃ hiraṇyaprākārāmārdrāṃ jvalantīṃ tṛptāṃ tarpayantīm |
padme sthitāṃ padmavarṇāṃ tāmihopahvaye śriyam ||
vyaktāvyaktasvarūpāya hṛṣīkapataye namaḥ |
mayā nivedito bhaktyā hyarghyo'yaṃ pratigṛhyatām ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ hastayorarghyaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tri-pāt with three quarters ūrdhvaḥ upward udait rose puruṣaḥ the Person pādaḥ one quarter asya of him iha here abhavat came to be punaḥ again tataḥ from that viṣvaṅ in every direction vyakrāmat he strode sa-aśana-anaśane what eats and what does not eat abhi over kām her sosmitām gently smiling hiraṇya-prākārām with a golden rampart ārdrām tender jvalantīm blazing tṛptām content tarpayantīm giving contentment padme on a lotus sthitām standing padma-varṇām lotus-coloured tām her iha here upahvaye I call near śriyam Śrī vyakta-avyakta-svarūpāya whose nature is the manifest and the unmanifest hṛṣīka-pataye to the lord of the senses namaḥ salutation mayā by me niveditaḥ offered bhaktyā with devotion hi indeed arghyaḥ water of welcome ayam this pratigṛhyatām may it be accepted ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation hastayoḥ in his hands arghyam water of welcome samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 4: “With three quarters the Puruṣa rose up; one quarter of him came to be here again; thence he spread everywhere, over what eats and what does not.” Śrī Sūkta 4: “Her who is bliss, gently smiling, walled in gold, moist, blazing, contented and contenting, standing on the lotus, lotus-hued — Śrī I call here.” Salutation to the lord of the senses, whose form is manifest and unmanifest; accept this water I offer with devotion.

Ācamanīyam · water to sip ↑

oṃ tasmādvirāḍajāyata | virājo adhi pūruṣaḥ |
sa jāto atyaricyata | paścādbhūmimatho puraḥ ||
candrāṃ prabhāsāṃ yaśasā jvalantīṃ śriyaṃ loke devajuṣṭāmudārām |
tāṃ padminīmīṃ śaraṇamahaṃ prapadye'lakṣmīrme naśyatāṃ tvāṃ vṛṇe ||
mandākinyāstu yadvāri sarvapāpaharaṃ śubham |
tadidaṃ kalpitaṃ deva samyagācamyatāṃ vibho ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ ācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tasmāt from him virāṭ Virāj ajāyata was born virājaḥ from Virāj adhi again pūruṣaḥ the Person saḥ he jātaḥ when born atyaricyata extended beyond paścāt behind bhūmim the earth atho and puraḥ before candrām moon-bright prabhāsām radiant yaśasā with glory jvalantīm blazing śriyam Śrī loke in the world deva-juṣṭām loved by the gods udārām bountiful tām her padminīm the lotus lady īm her śaraṇam as refuge aham I prapadye take alakṣmīḥ misfortune me my naśyatām may it perish tvām you vṛṇe I choose mandākinyāḥ of the Mandākinī tu indeed yat which vāri water sarva-pāpa-haram removes all sin śubham auspicious tat that idam here kalpitam is prepared deva O god samyak well ācamyatām let it be sipped vibho O Lord ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation ācamanīyam water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 5: “From him Virāj was born, and from Virāj the Puruṣa; once born he reached beyond the earth, behind and before.” Śrī Sūkta 5: “Moon-like, shining, blazing with glory, Śrī honoured by the gods in the world, generous — in her, the lotus-lady, I take refuge: may my misfortune perish; I choose you.” The water of the heavenly Gaṅgā, pure, taking away all sin — this is prepared for you, Lord: sip it.

Snānam · bath ↑

oṃ yatpuruṣeṇa haviṣā | devā yajñamatanvata |
vasanto asyāsīdājyam | grīṣma idhmaśśaraddhaviḥ ||
ādityavarṇe tapaso'dhijāto vanaspatistava vṛkṣo'tha bilvaḥ |
tasya phalāni tapasā nudantu māyāntarāyāśca bāhyā alakṣmīḥ ||
tīrthodakaiḥ kāñcanakumbhasaṃsthaissuvāsitairdeva kṛpārasārdraiḥ |
mayārpitaṃ snānavidhiṃ gṛhāṇa pādābjaniṣṭhyūtanadīpravāha ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ snapayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om yat when puruṣeṇa with the Person haviṣā as oblation devāḥ the gods yajñam the sacrifice atanvata spread out vasantaḥ spring asya its āsīt was ājyam ghee grīṣmaḥ summer idhmaḥ the fuel śarat autumn haviḥ the oblation āditya-varṇe O you of the sun's colour tapasaḥ from austerity adhi-jātaḥ was born vanaspatiḥ the lord of the forest tava your vṛkṣaḥ tree atha then bilvaḥ the bilva tasya its phalāni fruits tapasā by austerity nudantu may they drive away māyā illusions antarāḥ inner yāḥ which ca and bāhyāḥ outer alakṣmīḥ misfortunes tīrtha-udakaiḥ with waters of holy places kāñcana-kumbha-saṃsthaiḥ held in golden jars su-vāsitaiḥ well perfumed deva O god kṛpā-rasa-ārdraiḥ moist with the essence of compassion mayā by me arpitam offered snāna-vidhim the rite of bathing gṛhāṇa accept pāda-abja-niṣṭhyūta-nadī-pravāha O you from whose lotus feet the river's stream flows ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation snapayāmi I bathe him

Puruṣa Sūkta 6: “When the gods spread the sacrifice with the Puruṣa as oblation, spring was its ghee, summer the fuel, autumn the offering.” Śrī Sūkta 6: “O sun-hued one, by your austerity was born the bilva, king of trees; may its fruits by austerity drive away illusion, the inner hindrances and outer misfortune.” With holy waters in golden jars, scented and soft with compassion, accept the bath I offer, O Lord from whose lotus-feet the river flowed.

Pañcāmṛtasnānam · bath of the five nectars ↑

āpyāyasva sametu te viśvatassoma vṛṣṇiyam | bhavā vājasya saṅgathe ||
dadhikrāvṇṇo akāriṣaṃ jiṣṇoraśvasya vājinaḥ | surabhi no mukhā karatpra ṇa āyūgṃṣi tāriṣat ||
śukramasi jyotirasi tejo'si devo vassavitotpunātvacchidreṇa pavitreṇa vasossūryasya raśmibhiḥ ||
madhu vātā ṛtāyate madhu kṣaranti sindhavaḥ mādhvīrnassantvoṣadhīḥ |
madhu naktamutoṣasi madhumatpārthivagṃ rajaḥ madhu dyaurastu naḥ pitā |
madhumānno vanaspatirmadhumāgṃ astu sūryaḥ mādhvīrgāvo bhavantu naḥ ||
svāduḥ pavasva divyāya janmane svādurindrāya suhavītu nāmne |
svādurmitrāya varuṇāya vāyave bṛhaspataye madhumāgṃ adābhyaḥ ||

word by word · translation

āpyāyasva swell sametu may there gather te in you viśvataḥ from every side soma O Soma vṛṣṇiyam vigour bhava be vājasya of strength saṅgathe at the gathering dadhikrāvṇaḥ of Dadhikrāvan akāriṣam I have sung jiṣṇoḥ the victorious aśvasya horse vājinaḥ the swift surabhi fragrant naḥ our mukhā mouths karat may he make pra forth naḥ our āyūṃṣi lives tāriṣat may he lengthen śukram bright asi you are jyotiḥ light asi you are tejaḥ splendour asi you are devaḥ may the god vaḥ you savitā Savitṛ utpunātu purify acchidreṇa with the flawless pavitreṇa strainer vasoḥ of the good sūryasya sun raśmibhiḥ with the rays madhu sweetly vātāḥ the winds blow ṛtāyate for one who keeps the order madhu sweetness kṣaranti pour sindhavaḥ the rivers mādhvīḥ sweet naḥ for us santu may be oṣadhīḥ the plants madhu sweet naktam be the night uta and uṣasi the dawns madhu-mat full of sweetness pārthivam the earthly rajaḥ air madhu sweet dyauḥ Heaven astu may be naḥ our pitā father madhu-mān full of sweetness naḥ for us vanaspatiḥ be the tree madhu-mān full of sweetness astu may be sūryaḥ the sun mādhvīḥ sweet gāvaḥ the cows bhavantu may be naḥ for us svāduḥ sweet pavasva flow divyāya for the divine janmane race svāduḥ sweet indrāya for Indra su-havītu-nāmne whose name is good to invoke svāduḥ sweet mitrāya for Mitra varuṇāya Varuṇa vāyave Vāyu bṛhaspataye for Bṛhaspati madhu-mān full of honey adābhyaḥ never to be deceived

The five mantras of milk, curd, ghee, honey and pure water, recited together.

snānaṃ pañcāmṛtairdeva gṛhāṇa puruṣottama |
anāthanātha sarvajña gīrvāṇapraṇatipriya ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ pañcāmṛtasnānaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

snānam the bath pañca-amṛtaiḥ with the five nectars deva O god gṛhāṇa accept puruṣa-uttama O supreme Person anātha-nātha lord of the helpless sarva-jña all-knowing gīrvāṇa-praṇati-priya who delight in the homage of the gods ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation pañca-amṛta-snānam the bath of five nectars samarpayāmi I offer

Accept the bath of the five nectars, O god, Puruṣottama, protector of the unprotected, all-knowing, fond of the homage of the gods. (The nectars are sprinkled on the image with a flower.)

Śuddhodakasnānam · bath of pure water ↑

āpo hi ṣṭhā mayobhuvastā na ūrje dadhātana mahe raṇāya cakṣase |
yo vaśśivatamo rasastasya bhājayateha naḥ uśatīriva mātaraḥ |
tasmā araṃ gamāma vo yasya kṣayāya jinvatha āpo janayathā ca naḥ ||
nadīnāṃ caiva sarvāsāmānītaṃ nirmalodakam |
snānaṃ svīkuru deveśa mayā dattaṃ sureśvara ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ śuddhodakasnānaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

āpaḥ O waters hi indeed sthā you are mayaḥ-bhuvaḥ bringers of joy tāḥ as such naḥ us ūrje to strength dadhātana set mahe to great raṇāya delight cakṣase and to vision yaḥ which vaḥ your śiva-tamaḥ most auspicious rasaḥ essence tasya of that bhājayata give a share iha here naḥ to us uśatīḥ loving iva like mātaraḥ mothers tasmai to him aram readily gamāma may we go vaḥ for you yasya whose kṣayāya abode jinvatha you quicken āpaḥ O waters janayatha you bring forth ca and naḥ us nadīnām of the rivers ca and eva indeed sarvāsām all ānītam brought nirmala-udakam pure water snānam the bath svīkuru accept deva-īśa lord of gods mayā by me dattam given sura-īśvara lord of the gods ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation śuddha-udaka-snānam the bath in pure water samarpayāmi I offer

“O waters, you are the source of well-being …” Pure water brought from all the rivers — accept this bath I give you, lord of gods. (Water is shown to the image with the spoon and poured into the plate.)

Vastram · garments ↑

oṃ saptāsyāsanparidhayaḥ | trissapta samidhaḥ kṛtāḥ |
devā yadyajñaṃ tanvānāḥ | abadhnanpuruṣaṃ paśum ||
upaitu māṃ devasakhaḥ kīrtiśca maṇinā saha |
prādurbhūto'smi rāṣṭre'smin kīrtimṛddhiṃ dadātu me ||
vedasūktasamāyukte yajñasāmasamanvite |
sarvavarṇaprade deva vāsasī te vinirmite ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ vastrayugmaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om sapta seven asya its āsan were paridhayaḥ enclosing sticks triḥ thrice sapta seven samidhaḥ fuel-sticks kṛtāḥ were made devāḥ the gods yat when yajñam the sacrifice tanvānāḥ spreading abadhnan bound puruṣam the Person paśum as the victim upaitu may he come mām to me deva-sakhaḥ the friend of the gods kīrtiḥ Fame ca and maṇinā the jewel saha with prādurbhūtaḥ born asmi I am rāṣṭre in kingdom asmin this kīrtim fame ṛddhim prosperity dadātu may he give me me veda-sūkta-samāyukte joined with Vedic hymns yajña-sāma-samanvite attended by sacrifice and Sāman sarva-varṇa-prade bestowing every colour deva O god vāsasī two garments te for you vinirmite are made ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation vastra-yugmam a pair of garments samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 7: “Seven were its enclosing sticks, thrice seven its fuel, when the gods, spreading the sacrifice, bound the Puruṣa as victim.” Śrī Sūkta 7: “May the friend of the gods come to me, and fame with the jewel; I am born in this land — may he grant me fame and plenty.” These two garments, joined with Vedic hymns and sacrificial chants, giving every colour, are made for you, O god. — I offer a pair of garments.

Yajñopavītam · sacred thread ↑

oṃ taṃ yajñaṃ barhiṣi praukṣan | puruṣaṃ jātamagrataḥ |
tena devā ayajanta | sādhyā ṛṣayaśca ye ||
kṣutpipāsāmalāṃ jyeṣṭhāmalakṣmīṃ nāśayāmyaham |
abhūtimasamṛddhiṃ ca sarvāṃ nirṇuda me gṛhāt ||
brahmaviṣṇumaheśānāṃ nirmitaṃ brahmasūtrakam |
gṛhāṇa bhagavan viṣṇo sarveṣṭaphalado bhava ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ yajñopavītaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tam that yajñam sacrifice barhiṣi on the sacred grass praukṣan they sprinkled puruṣam the Person jātam born agrataḥ in the beginning tena with him devāḥ the gods ayajanta sacrificed sādhyāḥ the Sādhyas ṛṣayaḥ the seers ca and ye who were kṣut-pipāsā-malām foul with hunger and thirst jyeṣṭhām the elder sister alakṣmīm Misfortune nāśayāmi destroy aham I abhūtim want asamṛddhim ill success ca and sarvām all nirṇuda drive out me from my gṛhāt house brahma-viṣṇu-maheśānām of Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśa nirmitam fashioned brahma-sūtrakam the sacred thread gṛhāṇa accept bhagavan O blessed one viṣṇo O Viṣṇu sarva-iṣṭa-phala-daḥ giver of every wished-for fruit bhava be ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation yajña-upavītam the sacred thread samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 8: “They sprinkled on the sacred grass that sacrifice, the Puruṣa born in the beginning; with him the gods sacrificed, the Sādhyas and the seers.” Śrī Sūkta 8: “I destroy Alakṣmī, the elder sister, defiled with hunger and thirst; drive out of my house all ill-fortune and want.” The sacred thread made by Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva — accept it, Lord Viṣṇu, and grant every wished-for fruit.

Gandham · sandal ↑

oṃ tasmādyajñātsarvahutaḥ | sambhṛtaṃ pṛṣadājyam |
paśūgṃstāgṃścakre vāyavyān | āraṇyāngrāmyāśca ye ||
gandhadvārāṃ durādharṣāṃ nityapuṣṭāṃ karīṣiṇīm |
īśvarīgṃ sarvabhūtānāṃ tāmihopahvaye śriyam ||
śrīkhaṇḍaṃ candanaṃ divyaṃ gandhāḍhyaṃ sumanoharam |
vilepanaṃ suraśreṣṭha prītyarthaṃ pratigṛhyatām ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ divyaśrīcandanaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tasmāt from that yajñāt sacrifice sarva-hutaḥ in which all was offered sambhṛtam was gathered pṛṣat-ājyam the curdled ghee paśūn the beasts tān those cakre he made vāyavyān of the air āraṇyān of the forest grāmyāḥ of the village ca and ye which are gandha-dvārām known by her fragrance durādharṣām unassailable nitya-puṣṭām ever abundant karīṣiṇīm rich in the earth's bounty īśvarīm the sovereign sarva-bhūtānām of all beings tām her iha here upahvaye I call śriyam Śrī śrī-khaṇḍam fine sandalwood candanam sandal divyam divine gandha-āḍhyam rich in fragrance su-manaḥ-haram most charming vilepanam unguent sura-śreṣṭha O best of gods prīti-artham for your pleasure pratigṛhyatām may it be accepted ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation divya-śrī-candanam divine sandal paste samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 9: “From that sacrifice, offered whole, the clotted ghee was gathered; he made the creatures of the air, and those of the forest and the village.” Śrī Sūkta 9: “Her whose door is fragrance … Śrī I call here.” Divine sandal, fragrant and lovely — accept this unguent for your pleasure, best of gods.

Ābharaṇam · ornaments ↑

oṃ tasmādyajñātsarvahutaḥ | ṛcassāmāni jajñire |
chandāgṃsi jajñire tasmāt | yajustasmādajāyata ||
manasaḥ kāmamākūtiṃ vācassatyamaśīmahi |
paśūnāgṃ rūpamannasya mayi śrīśśrayatāṃ yaśaḥ ||
hiraṇyahārakeyūragraiveyamaṇikaṅkaṇaiḥ |
suhāraṃ bhūṣaṇairyuktaṃ gṛhāṇa puruṣottama ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ ābharaṇaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tasmāt from that yajñāt sacrifice sarva-hutaḥ in which all was offered ṛcaḥ the verses sāmāni the chants jajñire were born chandāṃsi the metres jajñire were born tasmāt from it yajuḥ the Yajus tasmāt from it ajāyata was born manasaḥ of the mind kāmam the desire ākūtim the intent vācaḥ of speech satyam the truth aśīmahi may we attain paśūnām of cattle rūpam the beauty annasya of food mayi in me śrīḥ Śrī śrayatām may she rest yaśaḥ and glory hiraṇya-hāra-keyūra-graiveya-maṇi-kaṅkaṇaiḥ with golden necklaces, armlets, collars and jewelled bracelets su-hāram a fine necklace bhūṣaṇaiḥ with ornaments yuktam joined gṛhāṇa accept puruṣa-uttama O supreme Person ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation ābharaṇam ornaments samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 10: “From that sacrifice, offered whole, the ṛcs and sāmans were born; the metres were born from it; from it the yajus arose.” Śrī Sūkta 10: “May we gain the mind's desire and intent, truth of speech; may the beauty of cattle and of food, fortune and fame rest in me.” With golden chains, armlets, necklaces and jewelled bracelets — accept these ornaments, Puruṣottama.

Puṣpam · flowers ↑

oṃ tasmādaśvā ajāyanta | ye ke cobhayādataḥ |
gāvo ha jajñire tasmāt | tasmājjātā ajāvayaḥ ||
kardamena prajābhūtā mayi sambhava kardama |
śriyaṃ vāsaya me kule mātaraṃ padmamālinīm ||
mallikādisugandhīni mālatyādīni vai prabho |
mayāhṛtāni pūjārthaṃ puṣpāṇi pratigṛhyatām ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ puṣpāṇi samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om tasmāt from that aśvāḥ horses ajāyanta were born ye those ke ca whichever ubhayā-dataḥ have two rows of teeth gāvaḥ cattle ha indeed jajñire were born tasmāt from that tasmāt from that jātāḥ were born aja-avayaḥ goats and sheep kardamena by Kardama prajā-bhūtā she has offspring mayi with me sambhava dwell kardama O Kardama śriyam Śrī vāsaya make dwell me in my kule family mātaram the mother padma-mālinīm lotus-garlanded mallikā-ādi-su-gandhīni fragrant jasmine and the like mālatī-ādīni mālatī and others vai indeed prabho O Lord mayā by me āhṛtāni brought pūjā-artham for worship puṣpāṇi flowers pratigṛhyatām may they be accepted ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation puṣpāṇi flowers samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 11: “From it horses were born, and all with teeth in both jaws; from it cows were born, from it goats and sheep.” Śrī Sūkta 11: “Through Kardama she has offspring; Kardama, dwell with me, and settle in my family Śrī, the mother garlanded with lotuses.” Fragrant jasmine, mālatī and other flowers I have brought for your worship — accept them, Lord.

Aṅgapūjā · worship of the limbs ↑

oṃ keśavāya namaḥ pādau pūjayāmi | oṃ govindāya namaḥ gulphau pūjayāmi |
oṃ indirāpataye namaḥ jaṅghe pūjayāmi | oṃ anaghāya namaḥ jānunī pūjayāmi |
oṃ janārdanāya namaḥ ūrū pūjayāmi | oṃ viṣṭaraśravase namaḥ kaṭiṃ pūjayāmi |
oṃ kukṣisthākhilabhuvanāya namaḥ udaraṃ pūjayāmi |
oṃ lakṣmīvakṣasthalālayāya namaḥ vakṣasthalaṃ pūjayāmi |
oṃ śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgapāṇaye namaḥ bāhūn pūjayāmi |
oṃ kambukaṇṭhāya namaḥ kaṇṭhaṃ pūjayāmi |
oṃ pūrṇendunibhavaktrāya namaḥ vaktraṃ pūjayāmi |
oṃ kundakuṭmaladantāya namaḥ dantān pūjayāmi |
oṃ nāsāgramauktikāya namaḥ nāsikāṃ pūjayāmi |
oṃ sūryacandrāgnidhāriṇe namaḥ netre pūjayāmi |
oṃ sahasraśirase namaḥ śiraḥ pūjayāmi |
oṃ śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāmine namaḥ sarvāṇyaṅgāni pūjayāmi ||
śrīmadādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrīvīraveṅkaṭasatyanārāyaṇasvāmine namaḥ
sarvāṇyaṅgāni pūjayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om keśavāya namaḥ om, salutation to Keśava pādau the feet pūjayāmi I worship om govindāya namaḥ om, salutation to Govinda gulphau the ankles pūjayāmi I worship om indirā-pataye namaḥ om, salutation to the lord of Indirā jaṅghe the shanks pūjayāmi I worship om anaghāya namaḥ om, salutation to the sinless one jānunī the knees pūjayāmi I worship om janārdanāya namaḥ om, salutation to Janārdana ūrū the thighs pūjayāmi I worship om viṣṭara-śravase namaḥ om, salutation to Viṣṭaraśravas kaṭim the waist pūjayāmi I worship om kukṣi-stha-akhila-bhuvanāya namaḥ om, salutation to him who holds all the worlds in his belly udaram the belly pūjayāmi I worship om lakṣmī-vakṣa-sthala-ālayāya namaḥ om, salutation to him on whose chest Lakṣmī dwells vakṣa-sthalam the chest pūjayāmi I worship om śaṅkha-cakra-gadā-śārṅga-pāṇaye namaḥ om, salutation to him whose hands hold conch, discus, mace and bow bāhūn the arms pūjayāmi I worship om kambu-kaṇṭhāya namaḥ om, salutation to him of the conch-like neck kaṇṭham the neck pūjayāmi I worship om pūrṇa-indu-nibha-vaktrāya namaḥ om, salutation to him whose face is like the full moon vaktram the face pūjayāmi I worship om kunda-kuṭmala-dantāya namaḥ om, salutation to him whose teeth are like jasmine buds dantān the teeth pūjayāmi I worship om nāsā-agra-mauktikāya namaḥ om, salutation to him with a pearl at the tip of his nose nāsikām the nose pūjayāmi I worship om sūrya-candra-agni-dhāriṇe namaḥ om, salutation to him who bears sun, moon and fire netre the eyes pūjayāmi I worship om sahasra-śirase namaḥ om, salutation to the thousand-headed one śiraḥ the head pūjayāmi I worship om śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmine namaḥ om, salutation to Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa sarvāṇi all aṅgāni the limbs pūjayāmi I worship śrīmat-ādya-ādi glorious, first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī-vīra-veṅkaṭa-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmine to Lord Śrī Vīra Veṅkaṭa Satyanārāyaṇa namaḥ salutation sarvāṇi all aṅgāni the limbs pūjayāmi I worship

I worship his feet as Keśava, ankles as Govinda, shanks as lord of Indirā, knees as the sinless, thighs as Janārdana, waist as Viṣṭaraśravas, belly as the one who holds all worlds within, chest as the one on whose breast Lakṣmī dwells, arms as bearer of conch, discus, mace and bow, throat as conch-necked, face as moon-faced, teeth as jasmine-bud-toothed, nose as the one with a pearl at its tip, eyes as bearer of sun, moon and fire, head as thousand-headed; and all his limbs as Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa.

om śrī-kṛṣṇāya namaḥ om, salutation to Śrī Kṛṣṇa om kamalā-nāthāya namaḥ om, salutation to the lord of Lakṣmī om vāsudevāya namaḥ om, salutation to Vāsudeva om sanātanāya namaḥ om, salutation to the eternal one om vasudeva-ātmajāya namaḥ om, salutation to the son of Vasudeva om puṇyāya namaḥ om, salutation to the holy one om līlā-mānuṣa-vigrahāya namaḥ om, salutation to him who took human form in play om śrīvatsa-kaustubha-dharāya namaḥ om, salutation to the bearer of Śrīvatsa and Kaustubha om yaśodā-vatsalāya namaḥ om, salutation to the darling of Yaśodā om haraye namaḥ om, salutation to Hari om catuḥ-bhuja-ātta-cakra-asi-gadā-śaṅkha-ādi-āyudha-dharāya namaḥ om, salutation to him whose four arms hold discus, sword, mace, conch and other weapons om devakī-nandanāya namaḥ om, salutation to the joy of Devakī

om śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-para-brahmaṇe namaḥ om, salutation to Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa, the supreme Brahman

Salutation to Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa, the supreme Brahman.

With the following names, worship with turmeric and kuṅkuma (the hundred and eight names of Lakṣmī).

Dhūpam · incense ↑

oṃ yatpuruṣaṃ vyadadhuḥ | katidhā vyakalpayan |
mukhaṃ kimasya kau bāhū | kāvūrū pādāvucyete ||
āpassṛjantu snigdhāni ciklīta vasa me gṛhe |
ni ca devīṃ mātaraṃ śriyaṃ vāsaya me kule ||
vanaspatirasodbhūtaiścandanāgarusaṃyutaiḥ |
āghreyassarvadevānāṃ dhūpo'yaṃ pratigṛhyatām ||
daśāṅgaṃ guggulūpetaṃ sugandhaṃ sumanoharam |
dhūpaṃ gṛhāṇa deveśa sarvadevanamaskṛta ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ dhūpamāghrāpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om yat when puruṣam the Person vyadadhuḥ they divided katidhā into how many parts vyakalpayan did they arrange him mukham mouth kim what asya was his kau what bāhū his two arms kau what ūrū his thighs pādau and feet ucyete are called āpaḥ may the waters sṛjantu bring forth snigdhāni soothing things ciklīta O Ciklīta vasa dwell me in my gṛhe house ni down ca and devīm the goddess mātaram mother śriyam Śrī vāsaya make dwell me in my kule family vanaspati-rasa-udbhūtaiḥ arisen from the sap of trees candana-agaru-saṃyutaiḥ mixed with sandal and aloe āghreyaḥ fit to be smelt sarva-devānām by all the gods dhūpaḥ incense ayam this pratigṛhyatām may it be accepted daśa-aṅgam of ten ingredients guggulu-upetam with bdellium su-gandham fragrant su-manaḥ-haram most charming dhūpam incense gṛhāṇa accept deva-īśa lord of gods sarva-deva-namaskṛta saluted by all the gods ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation dhūpam incense āghrāpayāmi I offer to be smelt

Puruṣa Sūkta 12: “When they divided the Puruṣa, into how many parts did they arrange him? What was his mouth, what his arms, what are his thighs and feet called?” Śrī Sūkta 12: “May the waters bring forth smooth things; Ciklīta, dwell in my house, and settle the divine mother Śrī in my family.” Made of the saps of trees, with sandal and aloe, fit to be smelled by all the gods — accept this incense; accept the tenfold fragrant incense with guggulu, O lord saluted by all the gods.

Dīpam · lamp ↑

oṃ brāhmaṇo'sya mukhamāsīt | bāhū rājanyaḥ kṛtaḥ |
ūrū tadasya yadvaiśyaḥ | padbhyāgṃ śūdro ajāyata ||
ārdrāṃ puṣkariṇīṃ puṣṭiṃ suvarṇāṃ hemamālinīm |
sūryāṃ hiraṇmayīṃ lakṣmīṃ jātavedo mamāvaha ||
sājyaṃ trivartisaṃyuktaṃ vahninā yojitaṃ priyam |
gṛhāṇa maṅgalaṃ dīpaṃ trailokyatimirāpaha ||
bhaktyā dīpaṃ prayacchāmi devāya paramātmane |
trāhi māṃ narakādghorāddivyajyotirnamo'stu te ||
ghṛtāktavartisaṃyuktaṃ vahninā yojitaṃ priyam |
dīpaṃ gṛhāṇa deveśa trailokyatimirāpaham ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ dīpaṃ darśayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om brāhmaṇaḥ the brāhmaṇa asya his mukham mouth āsīt was bāhū his two arms rājanyaḥ the warrior kṛtaḥ were made ūrū thighs tat that asya of his yat which is vaiśyaḥ the vaiśya padbhyām from his feet śūdraḥ the śūdra ajāyata was born ārdrām tender puṣkariṇīm lotus-like puṣṭim abundance suvarṇām golden hema-mālinīm gold-garlanded sūryām sun-bright hiraṇmayīm golden lakṣmīm Lakṣmī jāta-vedaḥ O Jātavedas mama for me āvaha bring hither sa-ājyam with ghee tri-varti-saṃyuktam with three wicks vahninā with fire yojitam lit priyam pleasing gṛhāṇa accept maṅgalam auspicious dīpam lamp trailokya-timira-apaha O remover of the darkness of the three worlds bhaktyā with devotion dīpam the lamp prayacchāmi I offer devāya to the god parama-ātmane the supreme Self trāhi save mām me narakāt from hell ghorāt terrible divya-jyotiḥ O divine light namaḥ salutation astu be te to you ghṛta-akta-varti-saṃyuktam with a wick soaked in ghee vahninā with fire yojitam lit priyam pleasing dīpam the lamp gṛhāṇa accept deva-īśa lord of gods trailokya-timira-apaham that removes the darkness of the three worlds ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation dīpam the lamp darśayāmi I show

Puruṣa Sūkta 13: “The brāhmaṇa was his mouth, the warrior was made his arms, his thighs the vaiśya; from his feet the śūdra was born.” Śrī Sūkta: “Bring me, Jātavedas, Lakṣmī — moist, lotus-pool, nourishing, golden, garlanded with gold, sun-like, made of gold.” The auspicious lamp with ghee and triple wick — accept it, dispeller of the three worlds' darkness; save me from dreadful hell, divine light; accept this lamp of ghee-soaked wicks.

Naivedyam · food offering ↑

oṃ candramā manaso jātaḥ | cakṣossūryo ajāyata |
mukhādindraścāgniśca | prāṇādvāyurajāyata ||
ārdrāṃ yaḥ kariṇīṃ yaṣṭiṃ piṅgalāṃ padmamālinīm |
candrāṃ hiraṇmayīṃ lakṣmīṃ jātavedo mamāvaha ||
sauvarṇe sthālimadhye maṇigaṇakhacite goghṛtāktān supakvān
bhakṣyān bhojyāṃśca lehyānaparimitarasāñcoṣyamannaṃ nidhāya |
nānāśākairupetaṃ dadhimadhusaguḍakṣīrapānīyayuktaṃ
tāmbūlaṃ cāpi viṣṇo pratidivasamahaṃ mānase kalpayāmi ||
rājānnaṃ sūpasaṃyuktaṃ śākacoṣyasamanvitam |
ghṛtabhakṣyasamāyuktaṃ naivedyaṃ pratigṛhyatām ||

word by word · translation

om om candramāḥ the moon manasaḥ from his mind jātaḥ was born cakṣoḥ from his eye sūryaḥ the sun ajāyata was born mukhāt from his mouth indraḥ Indra ca and agniḥ Agni ca and prāṇāt from his breath vāyuḥ the wind ajāyata was born ārdrām tender yaḥ-kariṇīm who bestows glory yaṣṭim bearing a staff piṅgalām tawny padma-mālinīm lotus-garlanded candrām moon-bright hiraṇmayīm golden lakṣmīm Lakṣmī jāta-vedaḥ O Jātavedas mama for me āvaha bring hither sauvarṇe golden sthāli-madhye in a dish maṇi-gaṇa-khacite studded with many gems go-ghṛta-aktān dressed with cow's ghee su-pakvān well cooked bhakṣyān things to be chewed bhojyān things to be eaten ca and lehyān things to be licked aparimita-rasān of boundless flavour coṣyam what is to be sucked annam food nidhāya having placed nānā-śākaiḥ with many vegetables upetam provided dadhi-madhu-sa-guḍa-kṣīra-pānīya-yuktam with curd, honey, jaggery, milk and drinking water tāmbūlam betel ca and api also viṣṇo O Viṣṇu prati-divasam every day aham I mānase in my mind kalpayāmi prepare rāja-annam royal rice sūpa-saṃyuktam with lentil soup śāka-coṣya-samanvitam with vegetables and relishes ghṛta-bhakṣya-samāyuktam with sweets made in ghee naivedyam the food offering pratigṛhyatām may it be accepted

Puruṣa Sūkta 14: “The moon was born from his mind, from his eye the sun; from his mouth Indra and Agni, from his breath the wind.” Śrī Sūkta: “… moist, active, the staff, tawny, garlanded with lotuses, moon-like, made of gold.” Daily in my mind, Viṣṇu, I set on a golden plate studded with gems well-cooked foods soaked in cow's ghee — things to chew, eat, lick and suck, of countless flavours, with rice and many vegetables, curd, honey, jaggery, milk and water, and betel too. Accept this offering of fine rice with dal, vegetables and ghee-cooked sweets.

oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ | oṃ tatsaviturvareṇyaṃ bhargo devasya dhīmahi | dhiyo yo naḥ pracodayāt ||
satyaṃ tvartena pariṣiñcāmi | amṛtamastu | amṛtopastaraṇamasi |
oṃ prāṇāya svāhā | oṃ apānāya svāhā | oṃ vyānāya svāhā |
oṃ udānāya svāhā | oṃ samānāya svāhā | oṃ brahmaṇe svāhā |
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ mahānaivedyaṃ samarpayāmi |
amṛtāpidhānamasi | uttarāpośanaṃ samarpayāmi |
hastau prakṣālayāmi | pādau prakṣālayāmi | śuddhācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven om om tat that savituḥ of Savitṛ vareṇyam most excellent bhargaḥ radiance devasya of the god dhīmahi we meditate on dhiyaḥ thoughts yaḥ who naḥ our pracodayāt may impel satyam you, truth tvā you ṛtena with order pariṣiñcāmi I sprinkle around amṛtam nectar astu may it be amṛta-upastaraṇam the under-layer of nectar asi you are om prāṇāya svāhā om, hail to the in-breath om apānāya svāhā om, hail to the out-breath om vyānāya svāhā om, hail to the diffused breath om udānāya svāhā om, hail to the up-breath om samānāya svāhā om, hail to the equalising breath om brahmaṇe svāhā om, hail to Brahman ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation mahā-naivedyam the great food offering samarpayāmi I offer amṛta-apidhānam the covering of nectar asi you are uttara-āpośanam the closing sip samarpayāmi I offer hastau the hands prakṣālayāmi I wash pādau the feet prakṣālayāmi I wash śuddha-ācamanīyam pure water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer

After the Gāyatrī the offering is sprinkled round and made over to the five breaths and to Brahman; the great food-offering is presented, then the closing sip, and water for washing hands and feet and for sipping.

Tāmbūlam · betel ↑

oṃ nābhyā āsīdantarikṣam | śīrṣṇo dyaussamavartata |
padbhyāṃ bhūmirdiśaśśrotrāt | tathā lokāgṃ akalpayan ||
tāṃ ma āvaha jātavedo lakṣmīmanapagāminīm |
yasyāṃ hiraṇyaṃ prabhūtaṃ gāvo dāsyo'śvān vindeyaṃ puruṣānaham ||
pūgīphalaissakarpūrairnāgavallīdalairyutam |
muktācūrṇasamāyuktaṃ tāmbūlaṃ pratigṛhyatām ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ tāmbūlaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om nābhyāḥ from his navel āsīt came antarikṣam the mid-region śīrṣṇaḥ from his head dyauḥ heaven samavartata arose padbhyām from his feet bhūmiḥ the earth diśaḥ the quarters śrotrāt from his ear tathā thus lokān the worlds akalpayan they fashioned tām her me for me āvaha bring hither jāta-vedaḥ O Jātavedas lakṣmīm Lakṣmī anapagāminīm who never departs yasyām in whom hiraṇyam gold prabhūtam abundant gāvaḥ cattle dāsyaḥ maidservants aśvān horses vindeyam may I win puruṣān men aham I pūgī-phalaiḥ with areca nuts sa-karpūraiḥ and camphor nāga-vallī-dalaiḥ with betel leaves yutam joined muktā-cūrṇa-samāyuktam combined with pearl-lime tāmbūlam betel pratigṛhyatām may it be accepted ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation tāmbūlam betel samarpayāmi I offer

Puruṣa Sūkta 15: “From his navel came the mid-air; from his head the sky came forth; from his feet the earth, from his ear the quarters: so they fashioned the worlds.” Śrī Sūkta 15: “Bring me, Jātavedas, that Lakṣmī who does not depart, in whom I may find abundant gold, cattle, servants, horses and people.” Accept this betel with areca, camphor and pearl-lime.

Nīrājanam · waving of lights ↑

oṃ vedāhametaṃ puruṣaṃ mahāntam | ādityavarṇaṃ tamasastu pāre |
sarvāṇi rūpāṇi vicitya dhīraḥ | nāmāni kṛtvā'bhivadan yadāste ||
hiraṇyapātraṃ madhoḥ pūrṇaṃ dadāti madhavyo'sānīti |
ekadhā brahmaṇa upaharati | ekadhaiva yajamāna āyustejo dadhāti ||
yaśśuciḥ prayato bhūtvā juhuyādājyamanvaham |
śriyaḥ pañcadaśarcaṃ ca śrīkāmassatataṃ japet ||
caturvartisamāyuktaṃ goghṛtena supūritam |
nīrājanena santuṣṭo bhavatveva jagatpatiḥ ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ karpūranīrājanaṃ darśayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om veda know aham I etam this puruṣam Person mahāntam great āditya-varṇam of the sun's colour tamasaḥ darkness tu indeed pāre beyond sarvāṇi all rūpāṇi forms vicitya having shaped dhīraḥ the wise one nāmāni names kṛtvā having given abhivadan calling them yat who āste abides hiraṇya-pātram a golden vessel madhoḥ of honey pūrṇam full dadāti he gives madhavyaḥ rich in sweetness asāni may I be iti thinking thus ekadhā as one brahmaṇe to the priest upaharati he presents it ekadhā as one eva indeed yajamāne in the sacrificer āyuḥ life tejaḥ and splendour dadhāti he places yaḥ he who śuciḥ pure prayataḥ restrained bhūtvā becoming juhuyāt offers ājyam ghee anu-aham day by day śriyaḥ of Śrī pañcadaśa-ṛcam the fifteen verses ca and śrī-kāmaḥ desiring fortune satatam constantly japet should recite catuḥ-varti-samāyuktam with four wicks go-ghṛtena with cow's ghee su-pūritam well filled nīrājanena by the waving of lights santuṣṭaḥ pleased bhavatu may he be eva indeed jagat-patiḥ the lord of the world ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation karpūra-nīrājanam the camphor light darśayāmi I show

Puruṣa Sūkta 16: “I know this great Puruṣa, sun-coloured, beyond the darkness; the wise one who, having fashioned all forms and given them names, dwells calling them.” He gives a golden vessel full of honey, thinking, may I be rich in sweetness; he offers it whole to the brahmin, and wholly the sacrificer gains life and lustre. Whoever, pure and restrained, offers ghee daily and, desiring fortune, constantly recites the fifteen verses of Śrī. With four wicks filled with cow's ghee — may the lord of the world be pleased by this waving of lights.

narya prajāṃ me gopāya | amṛtatvāya jīvase | jātāṃ janiṣyamāṇāṃ ca | amṛte satye pratiṣṭhitām |
atharva pituṃ me gopāya | rasamannamihāyuṣe | adabdhāyo'śītatano | aviṣaṃ naḥ pituṃ kṛṇu |
śagṃsya paśūnme gopāya | dvipādo ye catuṣpadaḥ | aṣṭāśaphāśca ya ihāgne | ye caikaśaphā āśugāḥ |
saprātha sabhāṃ me gopāya | ye ca sabhyāssabhāsadaḥ | tānindriyāvataḥ kuru | sarvamāyurupāsatām |
ahe budhniya mantraṃ me gopāya | yamṛṣayastraividā viduḥ | ṛcassāmāni yajūgṃṣi | sā hi śrīramṛtā satām |
mā no higṃsījjātavedaḥ | gāmaśvaṃ puruṣaṃ jagat | abibhradagna āgahi | śriyā mā paripātaya ||
samrājaṃ ca virājaṃ cābhiśrīryā ca no gṛhe |
lakṣmī rāṣṭrasya yā mukhe tayā mā sagṃsṛjāmasi ||
santataśrīrastu | sarvamaṅgalāni bhavantu | nityaśrīrastu | nityamaṅgalāni bhavantu ||

word by word · translation
