narya O Narya prajām offspring me my gopāya guard amṛtatvāya for immortality jīvase for life jātām born janiṣyamāṇām and yet to be born ca and amṛte in the immortal satye truth pratiṣṭhitām established atharva O Atharva pitum food me my gopāya guard rasam savour annam food iha here āyuṣe for long life adabdha-āyo O you of unharmed life aśīta-tano whose body is never cold aviṣam free from poison naḥ our pitum food kṛṇu make śaṃsya O Śaṃsya paśūn cattle me my gopāya guard dvi-pādaḥ two-footed ye those that are catuḥ-padaḥ four-footed aṣṭā-śaphāḥ eight-hoofed ca and ye those iha here agne O Agni ye those ca and eka-śaphāḥ single-hoofed āśu-gāḥ swift saprātha O Sapratha sabhām assembly me my gopāya guard ye those who ca and sabhyāḥ are members sabhā-sadaḥ who sit in the assembly tān them indriyāvataḥ vigorous kuru make sarvam full āyuḥ life upāsatām may they attain ahe budhniya O Serpent of the deep mantram sacred word me my gopāya guard yam which ṛṣayaḥ the seers traividāḥ versed in the three Vedas viduḥ know ṛcaḥ verses sāmāni chants yajūṃṣi formulae sā that hi indeed śrīḥ is the fortune amṛtā immortal satām of the good mā do not naḥ our hiṃsīt harm jāta-vedaḥ O Jātavedas gām cow aśvam horse puruṣam man jagat or living world abibhrat bearing no ill agne O Agni āgahi come śriyā with fortune mā me paripātaya surround samrājam sovereign ca and virājam far-ruling ca and abhiśrīḥ the surpassing glory yā which is ca and naḥ in our gṛhe house lakṣmīḥ the fortune rāṣṭrasya of the realm yā which is mukhe at the head tayā with that mā me saṃsṛjāmasi we unite santata-śrīḥ unbroken fortune astu may there be sarva-maṅgalāni all blessings bhavantu may there be nitya-śrīḥ lasting fortune astu may there be nitya-maṅgalāni lasting blessings bhavantu may there be

O manly one, guard my offspring, born and yet to be born, for immortality and life, established in the immortal truth. Atharvan, guard my food, the savoury nourishment here, for long life; unharmed one, of unfevered body, make our food free of poison. Praiseworthy one, guard my cattle, two-footed and four-footed, cloven-hoofed and whole-hoofed. Far-reaching one, guard my assembly and those who sit in it; make them strong of sense, and let them attain full life. Ahi Budhnya, guard my mantra, which the seers of the three Vedas know — ṛc, sāman and yajus; for that is the immortal fortune of the good. Jātavedas, harm not our cows, horses, people or world; come, Agni, without hostility, and guard me with fortune. The sovereign, the far-ruling, the fortune that is in our house, the Lakṣmī at the head of the realm — with her we unite ourselves. May fortune be unceasing, all blessings be; may fortune be constant, blessings be constant.

nīrājanaṃ gṛhāṇedaṃ pañcavartisamanvitam |
tejorāśimayaṃ dattaṃ gṛhāṇa tvaṃ sureśvara ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ karpūranīrājanaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

nīrājanam the waving of lights gṛhāṇa accept idam this pañca-varti-samanvitam with five wicks tejaḥ-rāśi-mayam a mass of light dattam offered gṛhāṇa accept tvam you sura-īśvara lord of the gods ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation karpūra-nīrājanam the camphor light samarpayāmi I offer

Accept this waving of five-wicked lights, a mass of radiance given to you, lord of gods. — I offer the camphor light.

Mantrapuṣpam · the flower of mantra ↑

oṃ dhātā purastādyamudājahāra | śakraḥ pravidvānpradiśaścatasraḥ |
tamevaṃ vidvānamṛta iha bhavati | nānyaḥ panthā ayanāya vidyate ||

word by word · translation

om om dhātā the Creator purastāt in the beginning yam whom udājahāra proclaimed śakraḥ Indra pravidvān knowing well pradiśaḥ the quarters catasraḥ four tam him evam thus vidvān one who knows amṛtaḥ immortal iha here bhavati becomes na no anyaḥ other panthāḥ path ayanāya for the going vidyate is there

He whom the Creator proclaimed in the beginning, whom Indra knew in all four quarters — one who knows him thus becomes immortal here; there is no other path to go.

oṃ sahasraśīrṣaṃ devaṃ viśvākṣaṃ viśvaśambhuvam |
viśvaṃ nārāyaṇaṃ devamakṣaraṃ paramaṃ padam ||
viśvataḥ paramānnityaṃ viśvaṃ nārāyaṇagṃ harim |
viśvamevedaṃ puruṣastadviśvamupajīvati ||
patiṃ viśvasyātmeśvaragṃ śāśvatagṃ śivamacyutam |
nārāyaṇaṃ mahājñeyaṃ viśvātmānaṃ parāyaṇam ||
nārāyaṇa paro jyotirātmā nārāyaṇaḥ paraḥ |
nārāyaṇa paraṃ brahma tattvaṃ nārāyaṇaḥ paraḥ |
nārāyaṇa paro dhyātā dhyānaṃ nārāyaṇaḥ paraḥ ||
yacca kiñcijjagatsarvaṃ dṛśyate śrūyate'pi vā |
antarbahiśca tatsarvaṃ vyāpya nārāyaṇassthitaḥ ||

word by word · translation

om om sahasra-śīrṣam thousand-headed devam the god viśva-akṣam all-seeing viśva-śambhuvam source of good for all viśvam the all nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa devam the god akṣaram imperishable paramam the supreme padam goal viśvataḥ than the universe paramāt higher nityam eternal viśvam the all nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa harim Hari viśvam universe eva indeed idam this puruṣaḥ is the Person tat that viśvam universe upajīvati he sustains patim the lord viśvasya of the universe ātma-īśvaram lord of the self śāśvatam everlasting śivam auspicious acyutam unfailing nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa mahā-jñeyam the great thing to be known viśva-ātmānam the self of all parāyaṇam the final refuge nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa paraḥ is the supreme jyotiḥ light ātmā the Self nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa paraḥ is supreme nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa param is the supreme brahma Brahman tattvam the reality nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa paraḥ is supreme nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa paraḥ is the supreme dhyātā meditator dhyānam the meditation nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa paraḥ is supreme yat what ca and kiñcit ever jagat in the world sarvam all dṛśyate is seen śrūyate is heard api vā or antaḥ within bahiḥ without ca and tat that sarvam all vyāpya pervading nārāyaṇaḥ Nārāyaṇa sthitaḥ abides

Nārāyaṇa Sūkta: the thousand-headed god, all-eyed, source of all happiness, the All, god Nārāyaṇa, the imperishable, the highest state; eternal, beyond the universe, the universe itself, Nārāyaṇa, Hari. The Person is all this universe; the universe lives on him. Lord of the universe, lord of selves, eternal, auspicious, unfailing, Nārāyaṇa, the great object of knowledge, the Self of all, the final refuge. Nārāyaṇa is the supreme light, the supreme Self, the supreme Brahman, the supreme reality; Nārāyaṇa is the supreme meditator and the supreme meditation. Whatever in the world is seen or heard, pervading all of it within and without, Nārāyaṇa stands.

anantamavyayaṃ kavigṃ samudre'ntaṃ viśvaśambhuvam |
padmakośapratīkāśagṃ hṛdayaṃ cāpyadhomukham ||
adho niṣṭyā vitastyānte nābhyāmupari tiṣṭhati |
jvālamālākulaṃ bhāti viśvasyāyatanaṃ mahat ||
santatagṃ śilābhistu lambatyākośasannibham |
tasyānte suṣiragṃ sūkṣmaṃ tasminsarvaṃ pratiṣṭhitam ||
tasya madhye mahānagnirviśvārcirviśvatomukhaḥ |
so'grabhugvibhajantiṣṭhannāhāramajaraḥ kaviḥ ||
tiryagūrdhvamadhaśśāyī raśmayastasya santatā |
santāpayati svaṃ dehamāpādatalamastakam |
tasya madhye vahniśikhā aṇīyordhvā vyavasthitā ||
nīlatoyadamadhyasthādvidyullekheva bhāsvarā |
nīvāraśūkavattanvī pītā bhāsvatyaṇūpamā ||
tasyāśśikhāyā madhye paramātmā vyavasthitaḥ |
sa brahma sa śivassa harissendrassokṣaraḥ paramassvarāṭ ||

word by word · translation

anantam endless avyayam imperishable kavim the seer samudre in the ocean antam the end viśva-śambhuvam source of good for all padma-kośa-pratīkāśam like a lotus bud hṛdayam the heart ca and api also adhaḥ-mukham faces downward adhaḥ below niṣṭyāḥ the throat vitastyāḥ of a span ante at the end nābhyām the navel upari above tiṣṭhati it stands jvāla-mālā-ākulam wreathed in garlands of flame bhāti it shines viśvasya of the universe āyatanam the abode mahat great santatam surrounded śilābhiḥ by veins tu indeed lambati it hangs ākośa-sannibham like a lotus bud tasya its ante at the end suṣiram is a hollow sūkṣmam subtle tasmin in that sarvam all pratiṣṭhitam is established tasya its madhye in the middle mahān is the great agniḥ fire viśva-arciḥ with flames everywhere viśvataḥ-mukhaḥ facing every way saḥ it agra-bhuk eating first vibhajan distributing tiṣṭhan it stays āhāram the food ajaraḥ unageing kaviḥ the seer tiryak across ūrdhvam upward adhaḥ-śāyī and spreading downward raśmayaḥ the rays tasya its santatā are spread santāpayati it warms svam its own deham body ā-pāda-tala-mastakam from the soles of the feet to the head tasya its madhye in the middle vahni-śikhā a tongue of fire aṇīyā most subtle ūrdhvā upright vyavasthitā stands nīla-toyada-madhya-sthāt from the midst of a dark cloud vidyut-lekhā a streak of lightning iva like bhāsvarā shining nīvāra-śūka-vat like the awn of wild rice tanvī slender pītā yellow bhāsvatī bright aṇu-upamā comparable to an atom tasyāḥ of that śikhāyāḥ flame madhye in the middle parama-ātmā the supreme Self vyavasthitaḥ abides saḥ he brahma is Brahmā saḥ he śivaḥ is Śiva saḥ he hariḥ is Hari saḥ he indraḥ is Indra saḥ he akṣaraḥ the imperishable paramaḥ the supreme sva-rāṭ the self-ruler

The endless, imperishable seer, the goal of the ocean of existence, source of all happiness — the heart, like a lotus bud hanging downward, a span below the throat and above the navel, shines wreathed in flames, the great dwelling of the universe. Surrounded by nerves, it hangs like a lotus bud; within it is a subtle space in which all is established. In its midst is a great fire, all-radiant, facing every way, which eats first, dividing the food, unaging, the seer; its rays spread across, above and below, warming its body from sole to crown. In its centre stands a tongue of flame, very subtle, rising upward, shining like lightning in the middle of a dark rain-cloud, slender as the awn of wild rice, golden, radiant, subtle beyond compare. In the midst of that flame dwells the supreme Self: he is Brahmā, he is Śiva, he is Hari, he is Indra; he is the imperishable, supreme, self-luminous.

oṃ rājādhirājāya prasahyasāhine | namo vayaṃ vaiśravaṇāya kurmahe |
sa me kāmānkāmakāmāya mahyam | kāmeśvaro vaiśravaṇo dadātu |
kuberāya vaiśravaṇāya | mahārājāya namaḥ ||
tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padagṃ sadā paśyanti sūrayaḥ | divīva cakṣurātatam |
tadviprāso vipanyavo jāgṛvāgṃsassamindhate | viṣṇoryatparamaṃ padam ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ suvarṇamantrapuṣpaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

om om rāja-adhirājāya to the king of kings prasahya-sāhine who conquers by force namaḥ homage vayam we vaiśravaṇāya to Vaiśravaṇa kurmahe pay saḥ he me my kāmān desires kāma-kāmāya who long for them mahyam to me kāma-īśvaraḥ the lord of desires vaiśravaṇaḥ Vaiśravaṇa dadātu may he grant kuberāya to Kubera vaiśravaṇāya Vaiśravaṇa mahā-rājāya the great king namaḥ salutation tat that viṣṇoḥ of Viṣṇu paramam highest padam step sadā always paśyanti see sūrayaḥ the wise divi in heaven iva like cakṣuḥ an eye ātatam spread out tat that viprāsaḥ the inspired vipanyavaḥ full of praise jāgṛvāṃsaḥ wakeful samindhate kindle viṣṇoḥ of Viṣṇu yat which is paramam the highest padam step ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation suvarṇa-mantra-puṣpam the golden flower of mantra samarpayāmi I offer

To the king of kings, who conquers by force, to Vaiśravaṇa we pay homage; may he, the lord of desires, Vaiśravaṇa, grant desires to me who desire. Salutation to Kubera Vaiśravaṇa, the great king. That highest step of Viṣṇu the wise ever behold, like an eye spread across the sky; that the sages, wakeful and eloquent, kindle — the highest step of Viṣṇu. — I offer the golden flower of mantra.

Pradakṣiṇa Namaskārāḥ · circumambulation ↑

yāni kāni ca pāpāni janmāntarakṛtāni ca |
tāni tāni praṇaśyanti pradakṣiṇapade pade ||
pāpo'haṃ pāpakarmā'haṃ pāpātmā pāpasambhavaḥ |
trāhi māṃ kṛpayā deva śaraṇāgatavatsala ||
anyathā śaraṇaṃ nāsti tvameva śaraṇaṃ mama |
tasmātkāruṇyabhāvena rakṣa rakṣa janārdana ||
pradakṣiṇaṃ kariṣyāmi sarvabhramanivāraṇam |
saṃsārasāgarānmāṃ tvamuddharasva mahāprabho ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ pradakṣiṇanamaskārān samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

yāni kāni whatever ca and pāpāni sins janma-antara-kṛtāni done in other births ca and tāni tāni all those praṇaśyanti perish pradakṣiṇa-pade at each step of the circling pade step by step pāpaḥ sinful aham am I pāpa-karmā of sinful deeds aham am I pāpa-ātmā sinful of soul pāpa-sambhavaḥ born of sin trāhi save mām me kṛpayā by your mercy deva O god śaraṇa-āgata-vatsala tender to those who seek refuge anyathā otherwise śaraṇam refuge na asti there is none tvam you eva alone śaraṇam are the refuge mama of me tasmāt therefore kāruṇya-bhāvena out of compassion rakṣa rakṣa protect, protect janārdana O Janārdana pradakṣiṇam circumambulation kariṣyāmi I shall perform sarva-bhrama-nivāraṇam that removes all wandering saṃsāra-sāgarāt from the ocean of worldly existence mām me tvam you uddharasva lift out mahā-prabho O great Lord ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation pradakṣiṇa-namaskārān circumambulation and prostrations samarpayāmi I offer

Whatever sins were done, in this and other births, perish at every step of the circling. I am sinful, my deeds are sinful, my self is sinful, born of sin: save me in your mercy, Lord, kind to those who seek refuge. I have no other refuge; you alone are my refuge — so, out of compassion, protect me, protect me, Janārdana. I will circle you, which removes all delusion; lift me out of the ocean of worldly life, great Lord.

chatraṃ samarpayāmi | cāmaraṃ samarpayāmi | gītaṃ śrāvayāmi |
nṛtyaṃ darśayāmi | nāṭyaṃ samarpayāmi | samastarājopacārān samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

chatram a parasol samarpayāmi I offer cāmaram a fly-whisk samarpayāmi I offer gītam song śrāvayāmi I sing for him nṛtyam dance darśayāmi I show nāṭyam drama samarpayāmi I offer samasta-rāja-upacārān all royal honours samarpayāmi I offer

I offer the royal parasol and the fly-whisk; I sing to you, show you dance and drama, and offer all the honours due to a king.

Prārthanā · prayer ↑

amoghaṃ puṇḍarīkākṣaṃ nṛsiṃhaṃ daityasūdanam |
hṛṣīkeśaṃ jagannāthaṃ vāgīśaṃ varadāyakam ||
saguṇaṃ ca guṇātītaṃ govindaṃ garuḍadhvajam |
janārdanaṃ janānandaṃ jānakīvallabhaṃ harim ||
praṇamāmi sadā bhaktyā nārāyaṇamajaṃ param |
durgame viṣame ghore śatruṇā paripīḍite ||
nistārayatu sarveṣu tathāniṣṭabhayeṣu ca |
nāmānyetāni saṅkīrtya phalamīpsitamāpnuyāt ||
satyanārāyaṇaṃ devaṃ vande'haṃ kāmadaṃ prabhum |
līlayā vitataṃ viśvaṃ yena tasmai namo namaḥ ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrī vīraveṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa svāmine namaḥ prārthanānamaskārān samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

amogham unfailing puṇḍarīka-akṣam lotus-eyed nṛsiṃham the Man-lion daitya-sūdanam slayer of Daityas hṛṣīkeśam Hṛṣīkeśa jagat-nātham lord of the world vāk-īśam lord of speech vara-dāyakam giver of boons sa-guṇam with qualities ca and guṇa-atītam beyond the qualities govindam Govinda garuḍa-dhvajam whose banner bears Garuḍa janārdanam Janārdana jana-ānandam joy of the people jānakī-vallabham beloved of Jānakī harim Hari praṇamāmi I bow to sadā always bhaktyā with devotion nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa ajam the unborn param the supreme durgame in the impassable viṣame in hardship ghore in terror śatruṇā by an enemy paripīḍite when oppressed nistārayatu may he deliver sarveṣu in all tathā and aniṣṭa-bhayeṣu fears of evil ca too nāmāni names etāni these saṅkīrtya having recited phalam the fruit īpsitam wished for āpnuyāt one obtains satyanārāyaṇam Satyanārāyaṇa devam the god vande salute aham I kāma-dam the granter of desires prabhum the lord līlayā in play vitatam was spread out viśvam the universe yena by whom tasmai to him namaḥ namaḥ salutation again and again ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī Śrī vīra-veṅkaṭa Vīra Veṅkaṭa satyanārāyaṇa Satyanārāyaṇa svāmine to the Lord namaḥ salutation prārthanā-namaskārān prayers and prostrations samarpayāmi I offer

The unfailing, the lotus-eyed, Nṛsiṃha, slayer of daityas, Hṛṣīkeśa, lord of the world, master of speech, giver of boons; with qualities and beyond them, Govinda, whose banner bears Garuḍa, Janārdana, joy of the people, beloved of Jānakī, Hari — to Nārāyaṇa, unborn and supreme, I ever bow with devotion. In hard, rough and dreadful straits, when pressed by an enemy, and in every dread of harm, may he carry one through; singing these names one gains the fruit one seeks. I bow to the god Satyanārāyaṇa, the lord who grants desires, by whom the universe was spread out in play: to him, salutation again and again.

idaṃ phalaṃ mayā deva sthāpitaṃ puratastava |
tena me saphalāvāptirbhavejjanmani janmani ||
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāmine namaḥ phalaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

idam this phalam fruit mayā by me deva O god sthāpitam is placed purataḥ before tava you tena by it me for me sa-phala-avāptiḥ fruitful attainment bhavet may there be janmani janmani in birth after birth śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmine to Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa namaḥ salutation phalam a fruit samarpayāmi I offer

This fruit, O God, I have set before you; by it may I gain fulfilment in birth after birth. — I offer fruit.

yasya smṛtyā ca nāmoktyā tapaḥpūjākriyādiṣu |
nyūnaṃ sampūrṇatāṃ yāti sadyo vande tamacyutam ||
mantrahīnaṃ kriyāhīnaṃ bhaktihīnaṃ janārdana |
yatpūjitaṃ mayā deva paripūrṇaṃ tadastu te ||

word by word · translation

yasya of whom smṛtyā by the remembrance ca and nāma-uktyā by uttering the name tapaḥ-pūjā-kriyā-ādiṣu in austerity, worship, rites and the rest nyūnam what is deficient sampūrṇatām completeness yāti attains sadyaḥ at once vande I salute tam that acyutam Acyuta mantra-hīnam lacking in mantra kriyā-hīnam lacking in rite bhakti-hīnam lacking in devotion janārdana O Janārdana yat whatever pūjitam worship has been done mayā by me deva O god paripūrṇam complete tat that astu may it be te for you

I bow to Acyuta, by remembering whom and speaking whose name whatever is lacking in austerity, worship and rite at once becomes complete. Lacking in mantra, in rite, in devotion — whatever worship I have done, Janārdana, may it be made complete for you.

anayā dhyānāvāhanādiṣoḍaśopacārapūjayā ca bhagavān sarvātmakaḥ
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrīvīraveṅkaṭasatyanārāyaṇasvāmī suprīto varado bhavatu ||
ādyādi mahālakṣmīsameta śrīvīraveṅkaṭasatyanārāyaṇasvāmiprasādaṃ śirasā gṛhṇāmi ||

word by word · translation

anayā by this dhyāna-āvāhana-ādi-ṣoḍaśa-upacāra-pūjayā worship of sixteen services, beginning with meditation and invocation ca and bhagavān the blessed sarva-ātmakaḥ Self of all ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī-vīra-veṅkaṭa-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmī Lord Śrī Vīra Veṅkaṭa Satyanārāyaṇa su-prītaḥ well pleased vara-daḥ and granting boons bhavatu may he be ādya-ādi first and foremost mahā-lakṣmī-sameta accompanied by Mahālakṣmī śrī-vīra-veṅkaṭa-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmi-prasādam the grace of Lord Śrī Vīra Veṅkaṭa Satyanārāyaṇa śirasā on my head gṛhṇāmi I receive

By this sixteen-fold worship may the Lord, the Self of all, Śrī Vīra Veṅkaṭa Satyanārāyaṇa Svāmi with Ādyā Mahālakṣmī, be well pleased and grant boons. I receive his grace upon my head.

❧

Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa Vratakathā ↑

The kathā is heard after the pūjā, holding akṣata, which are offered to the Lord at the close of each chapter.

Prathamo'dhyāyaḥ · Chapter One ↑

ekadā naimiśāraṇye ṛṣayaśśaunakādayaḥ |
papracchurānataṃ sarve sūtaṃ paurāṇikaṃ khalu ||
vratena tapasā kiṃ vā prāpyate vāñchitaṃ phalam |
tatsarvaṃ śrotumicchāmaḥ kathayasva mahāmune ||

word by word · translation

ekadā once naimiśa-araṇye in the Naimiṣa forest ṛṣayaḥ the sages śaunaka-ādayaḥ Śaunaka and the others papracchuḥ asked ānatam bowing sarve all sūtam the Sūta paurāṇikam teller of the Purāṇas khalu indeed vratena by a vow tapasā by austerity kim vā or by what prāpyate is obtained vāñchitam the desired phalam fruit tat that sarvam all śrotum to hear icchāmaḥ we wish kathayasva tell us mahā-mune O great sage

Once, in the Naimiśa forest, Śaunaka and the other sages all questioned Sūta, the teller of the Purāṇas, who bowed before them: “By what vow or austerity is the fruit one longs for obtained? We wish to hear all of it; tell us, great sage.”

sūtovāca —
nāradenaiva sampṛṣṭo bhagavān kamalāpatiḥ |
surarṣaye yathaivāha tacchṛṇudhvaṃ samāhitāḥ ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said nāradena by Nārada eva indeed sampṛṣṭaḥ asked bhagavān the blessed kamalā-patiḥ lord of Lakṣmī sura-ṛṣaye to the divine sage yathā as eva just āha he spoke tat that śṛṇudhvam hear samāhitāḥ with attention

Sūta said: “When Nārada asked this very question, the Lord, husband of Kamalā, answered the divine sage. Hear it as he told it, with attentive minds.”

ekadā nārado yogī parānugrahakāṅkṣayā |
paryaṭan vividhān lokān martyalokamupāgataḥ ||
tatra dṛṣṭvā janān sarvān nānākleśasamanvitān |
nānāyonisamutpannān kliśyamānān svakarmabhiḥ ||
kenopāyena caiteṣāṃ duḥkhanāśo bhaviṣyati |
iti sañcintya manasā viṣṇulokaṃ gatastadā ||

word by word · translation

ekadā once nāradaḥ Nārada yogī the yogi para-anugraha-kāṅkṣayā wishing to do good to others paryaṭan wandering vividhān through the various lokān worlds martya-lokam to the world of mortals upāgataḥ came tatra there dṛṣṭvā seeing janān the people sarvān all nānā-kleśa-samanvitān beset by many troubles nānā-yoni-samutpannān born in many wombs kliśyamānān suffering sva-karmabhiḥ through their own deeds kena by what upāyena means ca and eteṣām of these duḥkha-nāśaḥ the end of sorrow bhaviṣyati will come iti thus sañcintya thinking manasā in his mind viṣṇu-lokam to Viṣṇu's world gataḥ he went tadā then

Once the yogin Nārada, wishing to help others, wandered through the many worlds and came to the world of mortals. There he saw all the people beset by many troubles, born in many kinds of wombs, suffering through their own deeds. Thinking, “By what means will their sorrow be ended?”, he went to the world of Viṣṇu.

tatra nārāyaṇaṃ devaṃ śuklavarṇaṃ caturbhujam |
śaṅkhacakragadāpadmavanamālāvibhūṣitam |
dṛṣṭvā taṃ devadeveśaṃ stotuṃ samupacakrame ||

word by word · translation

tatra there nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa devam the god śukla-varṇam white in colour catuḥ-bhujam four-armed śaṅkha-cakra-gadā-padma-vana-mālā-vibhūṣitam adorned with conch, discus, mace, lotus and forest garland dṛṣṭvā seeing tam him deva-deva-īśam the lord of the god of gods stotum to praise samupacakrame he began

There he saw the god Nārāyaṇa, white of hue, four-armed, adorned with conch, discus, mace, lotus and the forest garland; seeing that lord of the gods, he began to praise him.

namo vāṅmanasātītarūpāyāmitaśaktaye |
ādimadhyāntahīnāya nirguṇāya guṇātmane |
sarveṣāmādibhūtāya bhaktānāmārtināśine ||

word by word · translation

namaḥ salutation vāk-manasa-atīta-rūpāya to him whose form is beyond speech and mind amita-śaktaye of measureless power ādi-madhya-anta-hīnāya without beginning, middle or end nirguṇāya without qualities guṇa-ātmane yet the soul of the qualities sarveṣām of all ādi-bhūtāya who is the origin bhaktānām of devotees ārti-nāśine the destroyer of distress

“Salutation to you, whose form is beyond speech and mind, of boundless power, without beginning, middle or end, beyond qualities yet the self of qualities, the origin of all, destroyer of your devotees' distress.”

śrutvā stotraṃ tato viṣṇurnāradaṃ pratyabhāṣata ||
śrībhagavānuvāca —
kimarthamāgato'si tvaṃ kiṃ te manasi vartate |
kathayasva mahābhāga tatsarvaṃ kathayāmi te ||

word by word · translation

śrutvā hearing stotram the hymn tataḥ then viṣṇuḥ Viṣṇu nāradam to Nārada pratyabhāṣata replied śrī-bhagavān the blessed Lord uvāca said kim-artham for what purpose āgataḥ come asi have tvam you kim what te your manasi in mind vartate is kathayasva tell me mahā-bhāga O fortunate one tat that sarvam all kathayāmi I shall tell te you

Hearing the hymn, Viṣṇu replied to Nārada. The Lord said: “Why have you come? What is in your mind? Tell me, fortunate one, and I will tell you all.”

nārada uvāca —
martyaloke janāssarve nānākleśasamanvitāḥ |
nānāyonisamutpannāḥ pacyante pāpakarmabhiḥ ||
tatkathaṃ śamayennātha laghūpāyena tadvada |
śrotumicchāmi tatsarvaṃ kṛpāsti yadi te mayi ||

word by word · translation

nāradaḥ Nārada uvāca said martya-loke in the world of mortals janāḥ people sarve all nānā-kleśa-samanvitāḥ beset by many troubles nānā-yoni-samutpannāḥ born in many wombs pacyante are tormented pāpa-karmabhiḥ by sinful deeds tat that katham how śamayet might one calm nātha O Lord laghu-upāyena by an easy means tat that vada tell śrotum to hear icchāmi I wish tat that sarvam all kṛpā compassion asti there is yadi if te of yours mayi towards me

Nārada said: “In the world of mortals all people are beset by many troubles; born in many kinds of wombs, they are tormented by their sinful deeds. How, Lord, may that be quieted by some easy means? Tell me; I wish to hear it all, if you have compassion for me.”

śrībhagavānuvāca —
sādhu pṛṣṭaṃ tvayā vatsa lokānugrahakāṅkṣayā |
yatkṛtvā mucyate mohāttacchṛṇuṣva vadāmi te ||
vratamasti mahāpuṇyaṃ svargamartyeṣu durlabham |
tava snehānmayā vatsa prakāśaḥ kriyate'dhunā ||
satyanārāyaṇasyedaṃ vrataṃ samyagvidhānataḥ |
kṛtvā sadyassukhaṃ bhuktvā cāmuṣminmokṣamāpnuyāt ||

word by word · translation

śrī-bhagavān the blessed Lord uvāca said sādhu well pṛṣṭam asked tvayā by you vatsa dear child loka-anugraha-kāṅkṣayā wishing to do good to the world yat which kṛtvā having done mucyate one is freed mohāt from delusion tat that śṛṇuṣva hear vadāmi I tell te you vratam a vow asti there is mahā-puṇyam of great merit svarga-martyeṣu in heaven and among mortals durlabham hard to find tava for you snehāt out of affection mayā by me vatsa dear child prakāśaḥ revelation kriyate is made adhunā now satyanārāyaṇasya of Satyanārāyaṇa idam this vratam vow samyak rightly vidhānataḥ by the rule kṛtvā having performed sadyaḥ at once sukham happiness bhuktvā having enjoyed ca and amuṣmin in the next world mokṣam liberation āpnuyāt one attains

The Lord said: “Well asked, my child, out of a wish to help the world. Hear what I tell you, by doing which one is freed from delusion. There is a vow of great merit, rare in heaven and on earth; out of love for you, child, I now make it known. This is the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa: keeping it duly, one enjoys happiness at once and gains liberation in the world beyond.”

nārada uvāca —
tacchrutvā bhagavadvākyaṃ nārado munirabravīt |
kiṃ phalaṃ kiṃ vidhānaṃ ca kṛtaṃ kenaiva tadvratam |
tatsarvaṃ vistarādbrūhi kathaṃ kāryaṃ ca tadvratam ||

word by word · translation

nāradaḥ Nārada uvāca said tat that śrutvā hearing bhagavat-vākyam word of the Lord nāradaḥ Nārada muniḥ the sage abravīt spoke kim what phalam is the fruit kim what vidhānam the method ca and kṛtam was performed kena by whom eva indeed tat that vratam vow tat that sarvam all vistarāt in full brūhi tell katham how kāryam is to be done ca and tat that vratam vow

Hearing the Lord's words the sage Nārada said: “What is its fruit, what its method? Who has kept this vow? Tell me all of it in detail, and how the vow is to be kept.”

śrībhagavānuvāca —
duḥkhaśokādiśamanaṃ dhanadhānyavivardhanam |
saubhāgyasantatikaraṃ sarvatra vijayapradam ||
māghe vā mādhave māsi kārtike vā śubhe dine |
saṅgrāmārambhavelāyāṃ yadā kleśasya sambhavaḥ |
dāridryaśamanārthaṃ ca vrataṃ kāryaṃ varepsubhiḥ ||

word by word · translation

śrī-bhagavān the blessed Lord uvāca said duḥkha-śoka-ādi-śamanam it calms sorrow, grief and the rest dhana-dhānya-vivardhanam increases wealth and grain saubhāgya-santati-karam brings good fortune and offspring sarvatra everywhere vijaya-pradam gives victory māghe in Māgha vā or mādhave in Vaiśākha māsi the month kārtike in Kārtika vā or śubhe on an auspicious dine day saṅgrāma-ārambha-velāyām at the time of setting out for battle yadā when kleśasya of trouble sambhavaḥ the arising dāridrya-śamana-artham for the ending of poverty ca and vratam the vow kāryam should be performed vara-īpsubhiḥ by those who seek boons

The Lord said: “It quiets sorrow and grief, increases wealth and grain, brings good fortune and offspring, and gives victory everywhere. In Māgha, Vaiśākha or Kārttika, on an auspicious day; at the outset of a conflict; whenever trouble arises; and to end poverty — the vow should be kept by those who seek blessings.”

māse māse ca kartavyaṃ varṣe varṣe'thavā punaḥ |
kartavyatāsti he vipra yathāvibhavasārataḥ ||

word by word · translation

māse māse month after month ca and kartavyam it should be done varṣe varṣe year after year athavā or punaḥ again kartavyatā the duty asti stands he O vipra brāhmaṇa yathā-vibhava-sārataḥ according to one's means

“It may be done every month, or again every year; it is to be done, O brahmin, according to one's means.”

ekādaśyāṃ pūrṇimāyāṃ ravisaṅkramaṇe'pi vā |
vrataṃ kāryaṃ muniśreṣṭha satyanārāyaṇasya hi ||
prātarutthāya niyato dantadhāvanapūrvakam |
nityakarma vidhāyādāvevaṃ saṅkalpayennaraḥ ||
bhagavandevadeveśa satyanārāyaṇa vratam |
tvatpriyārthaṃ kariṣyāmi prasīda kamalāpate ||

word by word · translation

ekādaśyām on the eleventh day pūrṇimāyām on the full-moon day ravi-saṅkramaṇe at the sun's passage api vā or else vratam the vow kāryam should be performed muni-śreṣṭha O best of sages satyanārāyaṇasya of Satyanārāyaṇa hi indeed prātaḥ at dawn utthāya rising niyataḥ disciplined danta-dhāvana-pūrvakam after cleaning the teeth nitya-karma the daily rite vidhāya performing ādau first evam thus saṅkalpayet should resolve naraḥ a man bhagavan O blessed one deva-deva-īśa lord of the god of gods satyanārāyaṇa O Satyanārāyaṇa vratam the vow tvat-priya-artham to please you kariṣyāmi I shall perform prasīda be gracious kamalā-pate O lord of Lakṣmī

“On Ekādaśī, on the full moon, or when the sun enters a sign, best of sages, the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa should be kept. Rising at dawn, self-controlled, cleaning the teeth and doing the daily rites first, a person should resolve thus: ‘Lord, god of gods, Satyanārāyaṇa, I will keep your vow for your pleasure; be gracious, husband of Kamalā.’”

evaṃ saṅkalpya madhyāhne kṛtvā mādhyāhnikīḥ kriyāḥ |
sāyaṅkāle punassnātvā yajeddevaṃ niśāmukhe ||
pūjāgṛhaṃ samāsādya nānālaṅkāraśobhitam |
pūjāsthānaviśuddhyarthaṃ gomayena vilepayet ||
tataḥ pañcavidhaiścūrṇai raṅgavallīṃ prakalpayet |
tasyopari nyasedvastraṃ sadaśaṃ nūtanaṃ dṛḍham ||
taṇḍulaṃ tatra vinyasya tanmadhye kalaśaṃ nyaset |
rājataṃ vā'thavā tāmramārakūṭena nirmitam ||
dravyābhāve mārtikaṃ vā vittaśāṭhyaṃ na kārayet |
tasyopari nyasedvastraṃ sadaśaṃ nūtanaṃ dṛḍham ||

word by word · translation

evam thus saṅkalpya having resolved madhya-ahne at midday kṛtvā performing mādhyāhnikīḥ the midday kriyāḥ rites sāyam-kāle in the evening punaḥ again snātvā having bathed yajet one should worship devam the god niśā-mukhe at nightfall pūjā-gṛham the room of worship samāsādya entering nānā-alaṅkāra-śobhitam adorned with many decorations pūjā-sthāna-viśuddhi-artham to purify the place of worship gomayena with cow-dung vilepayet one should smear it tataḥ then pañca-vidhaiḥ with five kinds of cūrṇaiḥ powder raṅga-vallīm a floor design prakalpayet one should draw tasya of that upari on top nyaset one should lay vastram a cloth sa-daśam fringed nūtanam new dṛḍham and strong taṇḍulam rice tatra there vinyasya spreading tat-madhye in its middle kalaśam a vessel nyaset one should set rājatam of silver vā or athavā or else tāmram of copper ārakūṭena of brass nirmitam made dravya-abhāve for want of means mārtikam of clay vā or vitta-śāṭhyam miserliness with wealth na not kārayet one should practise tasya of that upari on top nyaset one should lay vastram a cloth sa-daśam fringed nūtanam new dṛḍham and strong

“Having so resolved, and done the midday rites at noon, one should bathe again in the evening and worship the god at nightfall. Coming to the place of worship, adorned with many ornaments, one should smear it with cow-dung to purify it, draw a rangoli with five kinds of powder, and spread on it a new, strong, bordered cloth. Placing rice there, one should set a vessel in its midst — of silver, copper or brass, or, lacking means, of clay; but one should not be miserly with one's wealth — and spread on it a new, strong, bordered cloth.”

tasyopari nyaseddevaṃ satyanārāyaṇaṃ prabhum |
karṣamātrasuvarṇena tadardhārdhena vā punaḥ ||
pratimāṃ kārayedvipra satyadevasya satpateḥ |
pañcāmṛtena susnātaṃ maṇṭapopari vinyaset ||

word by word · translation

tasya of that upari on top nyaset one should set devam the god satyanārāyaṇam Satyanārāyaṇa prabhum the lord karṣa-mātra-suvarṇena with a karṣa's weight of gold tat-ardha-ardhena with a quarter of that vā or punaḥ again pratimām an image kārayet one should have made vipra O brāhmaṇa satya-devasya of the god Satya sat-pateḥ lord of the good pañca-amṛtena with the five nectars su-snātam well bathed maṇṭapa-upari on the pavilion vinyaset one should place it

“On it one should place the Lord Satyanārāyaṇa. One should have an image made of Satyadeva, lord of the good, with a karṣa of gold, or a half, or a quarter of that, O brahmin; bathed well in the five nectars, it is set upon the maṇḍapa.”

vighneśaḥ padmajā viṣṇurmahādevaśca pārvatī |
ādityādigrahāssarve śakrādyaṣṭadigīśvarāḥ |
atrāṅgadevatāḥ proktāstasmādagre prapūjayet ||
agrataḥ kalaśe devaṃ varuṇaṃ pṛthagarcayet ||

word by word · translation

vighna-īśaḥ Gaṇeśa padma-jā the lotus-born goddess viṣṇuḥ Viṣṇu mahā-devaḥ Mahādeva ca and pārvatī Pārvatī āditya-ādi-grahāḥ the planets, the Sun and the rest sarve all śakra-ādi-aṣṭa-dik-īśvarāḥ the eight lords of the quarters, Indra and the rest atra here aṅga-devatāḥ as attendant deities proktāḥ are declared tasmāt therefore agre first prapūjayet one should worship them agrataḥ in front kalaśe in the vessel devam the god varuṇam Varuṇa pṛthak separately arcayet one should worship

“Gaṇeśa, Lakṣmī, Viṣṇu, Mahādeva and Pārvatī, all the planets beginning with the Sun, and the eight guardians of the quarters beginning with Indra — these are declared the attendant deities here, so they should be worshipped first. Before them, one should worship Varuṇa separately in the vessel.”

gaṇeśaprabhṛtīnpañca kalaśasyottare nyaset |
udaksamāptyā saṃsthāpya pūjanīyāḥ prayatnataḥ ||
pūrvādidikṣu cendrādīnpūjayecchuddhamānasaḥ |
tato nārāyaṇaṃ devaṃ kalaśe pūjayetsudhīḥ ||

word by word · translation

gaṇeśa-prabhṛtīn Gaṇeśa and the rest pañca the five kalaśasya of the vessel uttare to the north nyaset one should place udak-samāptyā ending towards the north saṃsthāpya having set them pūjanīyāḥ they are to be worshipped prayatnataḥ with care pūrva-ādi-dikṣu in the quarters beginning with the east ca and indra-ādīn Indra and the rest pūjayet one should worship śuddha-mānasaḥ pure in mind tataḥ then nārāyaṇam Nārāyaṇa devam the god kalaśe in the vessel pūjayet should worship su-dhīḥ the wise man

“The five beginning with Gaṇeśa are placed north of the vessel, set in a row ending toward the north, and worshipped with care. With a pure mind one should worship Indra and the others in the eastern and other quarters; then the wise one should worship the god Nārāyaṇa on the vessel.”

cāturvarṇyairvrataṃ kāryaṃ strībhirvāpi munīśvara |
paurāṇikairvaidikaiśca mantrairbrāhmaṇasattamaḥ ||
kalpoktavidhinā kuryātsatyadevasya pūjanam |
paurāṇikaireva mantraiḥ kuryātpūjāṃ dvijetaraḥ ||

word by word · translation

cāturvarṇyaiḥ by those of the four classes vratam the vow kāryam should be performed strībhiḥ by women vā or api also muni-īśvara O lord of sages paurāṇikaiḥ with Purāṇic vaidikaiḥ with Vedic ca and mantraiḥ mantras brāhmaṇa-sattamaḥ the best of brāhmaṇas kalpa-ukta-vidhinā by the method stated in the ritual kuryāt should perform satya-devasya of the god Satya pūjanam the worship paurāṇikaiḥ with Purāṇic eva only mantraiḥ mantras kuryāt should perform pūjām the worship dvija-itaraḥ one who is not twice-born

“The vow may be kept by people of all four classes, and by women too, lord of sages. The best of brahmins should worship Satyadeva by the method of the ritual manual, with Purāṇic and Vedic mantras; others should worship with Purāṇic mantras alone.”

yasminkasmindine martyo bhaktiśraddhāsamanvitaḥ |
satyanārāyaṇaṃ devaṃ yajeccaivaṃ niśāmukhe ||

word by word · translation

yasmin kasmin on any dine day martyaḥ a mortal bhakti-śraddhā-samanvitaḥ filled with devotion and faith satyanārāyaṇam Satyanārāyaṇa devam the god yajet should worship ca and evam thus niśā-mukhe at nightfall

“On any day at all, a mortal endowed with devotion and faith should worship the god Satyanārāyaṇa thus at nightfall.”

brāhmaṇairbāndhavaiścaiva sahito dharmatatparaḥ |
naivedyaṃ bhaktito dadyātprasādaṃ bhakṣyamuttamam ||
rambhāphalaṃ ghṛtaṃ kṣīraṃ godhūmasya ca cūrṇakam |
abhāve śālicūrṇaṃ vā śarkarāṃ ca guḍaṃ tathā |
sapādaṃ sarvabhakṣyāṇi caikīkṛtya nivedayet ||

word by word · translation

brāhmaṇaiḥ with brāhmaṇas bāndhavaiḥ with kinsmen ca and eva indeed sahitaḥ together dharma-tatparaḥ intent on dharma naivedyam the food offering bhaktitaḥ with devotion dadyāt one should give prasādam as the consecrated food bhakṣyam an eatable uttamam excellent rambhā-phalam plantain fruit ghṛtam ghee kṣīram milk godhūmasya of wheat ca and cūrṇakam flour abhāve in its absence śāli-cūrṇam rice flour vā or śarkarām sugar ca and guḍam jaggery tathā likewise sa-pādam with a quarter more sarva-bhakṣyāṇi all the eatables ca and ekīkṛtya mixing together nivedayet one should offer

“Devoted to dharma, together with brahmins and kinsfolk, one should offer with devotion the food-offering, the excellent prasāda: plantain, ghee, milk and wheat semolina — or, lacking it, rice semolina — with sugar or jaggery; taking each ingredient in the measure of one and a quarter, one should mix them and offer them.”

viprebhyo dakṣiṇāṃ dadyātkathāṃ śrutvā janaissaha |
tataśca bandhubhissārdhaṃ viprānsaprīti bhojayet ||
prasādaṃ bhakṣayedbhaktyā nṛtyagītādikaṃ caret |
tataśca svagṛhaṃ gacchetsatyanārāyaṇaṃ smaran ||
evaṃ kṛte manuṣyāṇāṃ vāñchāsiddhirbhaveddhruvam |
viśeṣataḥ kaliyuge laghūpāyo hi bhūtale ||

word by word · translation

viprebhyaḥ to the brāhmaṇas dakṣiṇām a gift dadyāt one should give kathām the story śrutvā having heard janaiḥ people saha together with tataḥ then ca and bandhubhiḥ kinsmen sārdham together with viprān the brāhmaṇas sa-prīti with affection bhojayet one should feed prasādam the consecrated food bhakṣayet one should eat bhaktyā with devotion nṛtya-gīta-ādikam dance, song and the like caret one should hold tataḥ then ca and sva-gṛham to one's own house gacchet one should go satyanārāyaṇam Satyanārāyaṇa smaran remembering evam thus kṛte when it is done manuṣyāṇām of men vāñchā-siddhiḥ the fulfilment of wishes bhavet comes about dhruvam surely viśeṣataḥ especially kali-yuge in the Kali age laghu-upāyaḥ it is the easy means hi indeed bhū-tale on earth

“Having heard the story together with the people, one should give gifts to the brahmins, then feed them gladly along with one's kin; eat the prasāda with devotion, and have dancing and singing; then go home remembering Satyanārāyaṇa. When this is done, people's wishes are surely fulfilled. Above all in the Kali age, this is the easy means on earth.”

iti prathamo'dhyāyaḥ ||

word by word · translation

iti thus ends prathamaḥ the first adhyāyaḥ chapter

Thus ends the first chapter.

Dvitīyo'dhyāyaḥ · Chapter Two ↑

sūta uvāca —
athānyatsampravakṣyāmi kṛtaṃ yena purā dvija |
kaścitkāśīpure ramye hyāsīdvipro'tinirdhanaḥ ||
kṣuttṛḍbhyāṃ vyākulo bhūtvā nityaṃ babhrāma bhūtale ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said atha now anyat another thing sampravakṣyāmi I shall relate kṛtam it was performed yena by whom purā of old dvija O twice-born kaścit a certain kāśī-pure in the city of Kāśī ramye lovely hi indeed āsīt there was vipraḥ brāhmaṇa ati-nirdhanaḥ very poor kṣut-tṛḍbhyām by hunger and thirst vyākulaḥ distressed bhūtvā being nityam always babhrāma he wandered bhū-tale over the earth

Sūta said: “Now I will tell you further, O brahmins, who kept this vow of old. In the lovely city of Kāśī there was a very poor brahmin. Distressed by hunger and thirst, he wandered the earth every day.”

duḥkhitaṃ brāhmaṇaṃ dṛṣṭvā bhagavānbrāhmaṇapriyaḥ |
vṛddhabrāhmaṇarūpastaṃ papraccha dvijamādarāt ||
kimarthaṃ bhramase vipra mahīṃ nityaṃ suduḥkhitaḥ |
tatsarvaṃ śrotumicchāmi kathyatāṃ dvijasattama ||

word by word · translation

duḥkhitam suffering brāhmaṇam the brāhmaṇa dṛṣṭvā seeing bhagavān the blessed Lord brāhmaṇa-priyaḥ who loves brāhmaṇas vṛddha-brāhmaṇa-rūpaḥ in the form of an old brāhmaṇa tam that papraccha asked dvijam twice-born man ādarāt with regard kim-artham why bhramase do you wander vipra O brāhmaṇa mahīm the earth nityam always su-duḥkhitaḥ so sorrowful tat that sarvam all śrotum to hear icchāmi I wish kathyatām let it be told dvija-sattama O best of the twice-born

Seeing the brahmin in distress, the Lord, who loves brahmins, took the form of an old brahmin and asked him kindly: “Why, brahmin, do you wander the earth every day in such sorrow? I wish to hear all of it; tell me, best of the twice-born.”

brāhmaṇa uvāca —
brāhmaṇo'tidaridro'haṃ bhikṣārthaṃ vai bhrame mahīm |
upāyaṃ yadi jānāsi kṛpayā kathaya prabho ||

word by word · translation

brāhmaṇaḥ the brāhmaṇa uvāca said brāhmaṇaḥ a brāhmaṇa ati-daridraḥ very poor aham I am bhikṣā-artham for alms vai indeed bhrame I wander mahīm the earth upāyam a remedy yadi if jānāsi you know kṛpayā kindly kathaya tell it prabho O lord

The brahmin said: “I am a brahmin, very poor; I wander the earth for alms. If you know a remedy, sir, tell it to me in your kindness.”

vṛddhabrāhmaṇa uvāca —
satyanārāyaṇo viṣṇurvāñchitārthaphalapradaḥ |
tasya tvaṃ pūjanaṃ vipra kuruṣva vratamuttamam |
yatkṛtvā sarvaduḥkhebhyo mukto bhavati mānavaḥ ||

word by word · translation

vṛddha-brāhmaṇaḥ the old brāhmaṇa uvāca said satyanārāyaṇaḥ Satyanārāyaṇa viṣṇuḥ is Viṣṇu vāñchita-artha-phala-pradaḥ who grants the fruit of what is desired tasya his tvam you pūjanam worship vipra O brāhmaṇa kuruṣva perform vratam the vow uttamam excellent yat which kṛtvā having done sarva-duḥkhebhyaḥ from all sorrows muktaḥ freed bhavati becomes mānavaḥ a man

The old brahmin said: “Satyanārāyaṇa is Viṣṇu, who grants the fruit of one's wishes. Worship him, brahmin; keep his excellent vow, by which a person is freed from every sorrow.”

vidhānaṃ ca vratasyāsya viprāyābhāṣya yatnataḥ |
satyanārāyaṇo vṛddhastatraivāntaradhīyata ||

word by word · translation

vidhānam the method ca and vratasya of vow asya this viprāya to the brāhmaṇa ābhāṣya having told yatnataḥ with care satyanārāyaṇaḥ Satyanārāyaṇa vṛddhaḥ as the old man tatra there eva itself antaradhīyata vanished

Having carefully told the brahmin the method of the vow, Satyanārāyaṇa in the form of the old man vanished on the spot.

tadvrataṃ śvaḥ kariṣyāmi yaduktaṃ brāhmaṇena vai |
iti sañcintya vipro'sau rātrau nidrāṃ na labdhavān ||
tataḥ prātassamutthāya satyanārāyaṇavratam |
kariṣya iti saṅkalpya bhikṣārthamagamaddvijaḥ ||

word by word · translation

tat that vratam vow śvaḥ tomorrow kariṣyāmi I shall perform yat which uktam was told brāhmaṇena by the brāhmaṇa vai indeed iti thus sañcintya thinking vipraḥ brāhmaṇa asau that rātrau at night nidrām sleep na no labdhavān got tataḥ then prātaḥ at dawn samutthāya rising satyanārāyaṇa-vratam the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa kariṣye I shall perform iti thus saṅkalpya resolving bhikṣā-artham for alms agamat went dvijaḥ the twice-born

“Tomorrow I will keep the vow the brahmin described,” thought the brahmin, and could not sleep that night. Rising at dawn, resolving “I will keep the Satyanārāyaṇa vow,” he went out for alms.

etasmindivase vipraḥ pracuraṃ dravyamāptavān |
tenaiva bandhubhissārdhaṃ satyasya vratamācarat ||

word by word · translation

etasmin on that divase day vipraḥ the brāhmaṇa pracuram abundant dravyam wealth āptavān received tena with it eva itself bandhubhiḥ kinsmen sārdham together with satyasya of Satya vratam the vow ācarat he performed

That very day the brahmin gained much wealth, and with it, together with his kinsfolk, he kept the vow of Satya.

sarvaduḥkhavinirmuktassarvasampatsamanvitaḥ |
babhūva sa dvijaśreṣṭho vratasyāsya prabhāvataḥ ||

word by word · translation

sarva-duḥkha-vinirmuktaḥ freed from all sorrow sarva-sampat-samanvitaḥ endowed with every prosperity babhūva became saḥ that dvija-śreṣṭhaḥ best of the twice-born vratasya of vow asya this prabhāvataḥ by the power

By the power of this vow that excellent brahmin was freed from every sorrow and blessed with every kind of wealth.

tataḥ prabhṛti vipreṇa māsi māsi vrataṃ kṛtam ||
evaṃ nārāyaṇasyedaṃ vrataṃ kṛtvā dvijottamaḥ |
sarvapāpavinirmukto durlabhaṃ mokṣamāptavān ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ from then prabhṛti onward vipreṇa by the brāhmaṇa māsi māsi month after month vratam the vow kṛtam was performed evam thus nārāyaṇasya of Nārāyaṇa idam this vratam vow kṛtvā having performed dvija-uttamaḥ the best of the twice-born sarva-pāpa-vinirmuktaḥ freed from all sin durlabham hard to gain mokṣam liberation āptavān attained

From then on the brahmin kept the vow every month. Having thus kept Nārāyaṇa's vow, the best of brahmins was freed from all sins and attained liberation, which is hard to win.

vratametadyadā vipraḥ pṛthivyāṃ saṅkariṣyati |
tathaiva sarvaduḥkhaṃ ca manujasya vinaśyati ||

word by word · translation

vratam vow etat this yadā whenever vipraḥ a brāhmaṇa pṛthivyām on earth saṅkariṣyati shall perform tathā so eva indeed sarva-duḥkham all the sorrow ca and manujasya of that man vinaśyati perishes

Whenever anyone on earth keeps this vow, just so every sorrow of that person is destroyed.

sūta uvāca —
evaṃ nārāyaṇenoktaṃ nāradāya mahātmane |
mayā tatkathitaṃ viprāḥ kimanyatkathayāmi vaḥ ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said evam thus nārāyaṇena by Nārāyaṇa uktam it was told nāradāya to Nārada mahā-ātmane the great-souled mayā by me tat that kathitam has been told viprāḥ O brāhmaṇas kim what anyat else kathayāmi shall I tell vaḥ you

Sūta said: “Thus Nārāyaṇa told it to the great-souled Nārada, and I have told it to you, brahmins. What more shall I tell you?”

ṛṣaya ūcuḥ —
tasmādviprācchrutaṃ kena pṛthivyāṃ caritaṃ mune |
tatsarvaṃ śrotumicchāmaśśraddhāsmākaṃ prajāyate ||

word by word · translation

ṛṣayaḥ the sages ūcuḥ said tasmāt from that viprāt brāhmaṇa śrutam was it heard kena by whom pṛthivyām on earth caritam and practised mune O sage tat that sarvam all śrotum to hear icchāmaḥ we wish śraddhā faith asmākam in us prajāyate is born

The sages said: “Who on earth heard it from that brahmin and kept it, sage? We wish to hear all of it; faith is arising in us.”

sūta uvāca —
śṛṇudhvaṃ munayassarve vrataṃ kena kṛtaṃ bhuvi |
ekadā sa dvijavaro yathāvibhavavistaraiḥ |
bandhubhissvajanaissārdhaṃ vrataṃ kartuṃ samudyataḥ ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said śṛṇudhvam hear munayaḥ O sages sarve all vratam the vow kena by whom kṛtam was performed bhuvi on earth ekadā once saḥ that dvija-varaḥ excellent brāhmaṇa yathā-vibhava-vistaraiḥ as amply as his means allowed bandhubhiḥ with kinsmen sva-janaiḥ his own people sārdham together with vratam the vow kartum to perform samudyataḥ made ready

Sūta said: “Hear, all you sages, who kept the vow on earth. Once that excellent brahmin, with all the splendour his means allowed, was about to keep the vow together with his kin and people.”

etasminnantare kāle kāṣṭhakretā samāgataḥ |
bahiḥ kāṣṭhāni saṃsthāpya viprasya gṛhamāyayau ||
tṛṣṇayā pīḍitātmā ca dṛṣṭvā vipraṃ kṛtavratam |
praṇipatya dvijaṃ prāha kimidaṃ kriyate tvayā |
kṛte kiṃ phalamāpnoti vistarādvada me prabho ||

word by word · translation

etasmin at that antare same kāle time kāṣṭha-kretā a woodseller samāgataḥ arrived bahiḥ outside kāṣṭhāni his wood saṃsthāpya setting down viprasya the brāhmaṇa's gṛham house āyayau he came into tṛṣṇayā by thirst pīḍita-ātmā tormented ca and dṛṣṭvā seeing vipram the brāhmaṇa kṛta-vratam performing the vow praṇipatya bowing down dvijam to the twice-born prāha he said kim what idam is this kriyate being done tvayā by you kṛte when it is done kim what phalam fruit āpnoti does one gain vistarāt in full vada tell me me prabho O lord

Just then a woodcutter came by. Leaving his wood outside, he came into the brahmin's house. Parched with thirst, he saw the vow the brahmin was keeping, bowed to him and said: “What is this you are doing? What fruit does one gain by it? Tell me in full, sir.”

vipra uvāca —
satyanārāyaṇasyedaṃ vrataṃ sarvepsitapradam |
tasya prapūjane sarvaṃ dhanadhānyādikaṃ mahat ||

word by word · translation

vipraḥ the brāhmaṇa uvāca said satyanārāyaṇasya of Satyanārāyaṇa idam this vratam is the vow sarva-īpsita-pradam that grants all that is wished tasya his prapūjane by the worship sarvam all dhana-dhānya-ādikam wealth, grain and the rest mahat in plenty

The brahmin said: “This is the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa, which grants all that one desires. By worshipping him comes every great thing — wealth, grain and all the rest.”

tasmādetadvrataṃ jñātvā kāṣṭhakretā'tiharṣitaḥ |
papau jalaṃ prasādaṃ ca bhuktvā svanagaraṃ yayau ||

word by word · translation

tasmāt from him etat this vratam vow jñātvā learning kāṣṭha-kretā the woodseller ati-harṣitaḥ overjoyed papau drank jalam water prasādam the consecrated food ca and bhuktvā having eaten sva-nagaram to his own town yayau went

Learning of this vow from him, the woodcutter was overjoyed. He drank water, ate the prasāda, and went back to his own town.

satyanārāyaṇaṃ devaṃ mānasenetyacintayat |
kāṣṭhavikrayato grāme prāpsyate cādya yaddhanam |
tenaiva satyadevasya kariṣye vratamuttamam ||
iti sañcintya manasā kāṣṭhaṃ kṛtvā tu mastake ||

word by word · translation

satyanārāyaṇam Satyanārāyaṇa devam the god mānasena in his mind iti thus acintayat he thought of kāṣṭha-vikrayataḥ from the sale of wood grāme in the village prāpsyate will be got ca and adya today yat whatever dhanam money tena with that eva alone satya-devasya of the god Satya kariṣye I shall perform vratam the vow uttamam excellent iti thus sañcintya thinking manasā in his mind kāṣṭham the wood kṛtvā placing tu then mastake on his head

In his mind he thought of the god Satyanārāyaṇa thus: “With whatever money I get today by selling wood in the town, I will keep the excellent vow of Satyadeva.” So resolving, he set the wood upon his head …

jagāma nagare ramye dhanināṃ yatra saṃsthitiḥ |
taddine kāṣṭhamūlyaṃ ca dviguṇaṃ prāptavānasau ||
tataḥ prasannahṛdayassupakvaṃ kadalīphalam |
śarkarāghṛtadugdhaṃ ca godhūmasya ca cūrṇakam ||
kṛtvaikatra sapādaṃ ca gṛhītvā svagṛhaṃ yayau ||

word by word · translation

jagāma he went nagare to a town ramye lovely dhaninām of the wealthy yatra where saṃsthitiḥ was the dwelling tat-dine that day kāṣṭha-mūlyam the price of wood ca and dvi-guṇam twofold prāptavān got asau he tataḥ then prasanna-hṛdayaḥ glad at heart su-pakvam well ripened kadalī-phalam plantain fruit śarkarā-ghṛta-dugdham sugar, ghee and milk ca and godhūmasya of wheat ca and cūrṇakam flour kṛtvā putting ekatra together sa-pādam with a quarter more ca and gṛhītvā taking it sva-gṛham to his own house yayau he went

… and went to the lovely part of the city where the wealthy lived. That day he got twice the price for his wood. Glad at heart, he took ripe plantains, sugar, ghee, milk and wheat semolina, each a measure and a quarter, and went home.

tato bandhūnsamāhūya cakāra vidhinā vratam |
tadvratasya prabhāvena dhanaputrānvito'bhavat |
ihaloke sukhaṃ bhuktvā cānte satyapuraṃ yayau ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then bandhūn his kinsmen samāhūya calling together cakāra he performed vidhinā by the rule vratam the vow tat-vratasya of that vow prabhāvena by the power dhana-putra-anvitaḥ blessed with wealth and sons abhavat he became iha-loke in this world sukham happiness bhuktvā having enjoyed ca and ante at the end satya-puram to the city of Satya yayau he went

Then he called his kinsfolk together and kept the vow as prescribed. By the power of that vow he was blessed with wealth and sons; he enjoyed happiness in this world and at the end went to the city of Satya.

iti dvitīyo'dhyāyaḥ ||

word by word · translation

iti thus ends dvitīyaḥ the second adhyāyaḥ chapter

Thus ends the second chapter.

Tṛtīyo'dhyāyaḥ · Chapter Three ↑

sūta uvāca —
punaragre pravakṣyāmi śṛṇudhvaṃ munisattamāḥ |
purā colkāmukho nāma nṛpaścāsīnmahīpatiḥ ||
jitendriyassatyavādī gacchandevālayaṃ prati |
dine dine dhanaṃ dattvā dvijānsantoṣayetsudhīḥ ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said punaḥ again agre further pravakṣyāmi I shall relate śṛṇudhvam hear muni-sattamāḥ O best of sages purā of old ca and ulkāmukhaḥ Ulkāmukha nāma by name nṛpaḥ a king ca and āsīt there was mahī-patiḥ lord of the earth jita-indriyaḥ master of his senses satya-vādī truthful gacchan going deva-ālayam the temple prati to dine dine day by day dhanam wealth dattvā giving dvijān the twice-born santoṣayet he would gladden su-dhīḥ the wise one

Sūta said: “I will tell you more; listen, best of sages. Long ago there was a king, a lord of the earth, named Ulkāmukha — master of his senses, truthful. Going to the temple, the wise king pleased the brahmins every day with gifts of wealth.”

bhāryā tasya pramugdhā ca sarojavadanā satī |
bhadraśīlānadītīre satyasya vratamācarat ||

word by word · translation

bhāryā wife tasya his pramugdhā most charming ca and saroja-vadanā lotus-faced satī virtuous bhadraśīlā-nadī-tīre on the bank of the river Bhadraśīlā satyasya of Satya vratam the vow ācarat performed

His wife, most charming, lotus-faced and virtuous — together they kept the vow of Satya on the bank of the river Bhadraśīlā.

etasminnantare tatra sādhurekassamāgataḥ |
vāṇijyārthaṃ bahudhanairanekaiḥ paripūritām ||
nāvaṃ saṃsthāpya tattīre jagāma nṛpatiṃ prati |
dṛṣṭvā ca vratinaṃ bhūpaṃ papraccha vinayānvitaḥ ||

word by word · translation

etasmin at that antare time tatra there sādhuḥ a merchant named Sādhu ekaḥ a certain samāgataḥ arrived vāṇijya-artham for trade bahu-dhanaiḥ with much wealth anekaiḥ of many kinds paripūritām laden nāvam his boat saṃsthāpya mooring tat-tīre on that bank jagāma he went nṛ-patim the king prati to dṛṣṭvā seeing ca and vratinam engaged in the vow bhū-pam the king papraccha he asked vinaya-anvitaḥ with humility

Just then a merchant named Sādhu arrived there, his boat laden with much wealth of many kinds for trade. Mooring the boat at the bank, he went to the king, and seeing him keeping the vow, asked him humbly:

sādhuruvāca —
kimidaṃ kuruṣe rājanbhaktiyuktena cetasā |
prakāśaṃ kuru tatsarvaṃ śrotumicchāmi sāmpratam ||

word by word · translation

sādhuḥ Sādhu uvāca said kim what idam is this kuruṣe that you do rājan O king bhakti-yuktena full of devotion cetasā with a mind prakāśam plain kuru make tat that sarvam all śrotum to hear icchāmi I wish sāmpratam now

Sādhu said: “What is this you are doing, O king, with a mind so full of devotion? Make it all known to me; I wish to hear it now.”

rājā uvāca —
pūjanaṃ kriyate sādho viṣṇoratulatejasaḥ |
vrataṃ ca svajanaissārdhaṃ putrādiprāptikāmyayā ||

word by word · translation

rājā the king uvāca said pūjanam worship kriyate is being done sādho O Sādhu viṣṇoḥ of Viṣṇu atula-tejasaḥ of matchless splendour vratam the vow ca and sva-janaiḥ my own people sārdham together with putra-ādi-prāpti-kāmyayā from the wish to gain sons and the rest

The king said: “Sādhu, we are worshipping Viṣṇu of matchless splendour and keeping his vow with our people, wishing for sons and other blessings.”

bhūpasya vacanaṃ śrutvā sādhuḥ provāca sādaram |
mamāpi santatirnāsti hyetasmājjāyate dhruvam ||

word by word · translation

bhū-pasya the king's vacanam words śrutvā hearing sādhuḥ Sādhu provāca said sa-ādaram with respect mama to me api also santatiḥ offspring na asti there is none hi indeed etasmāt from this jāyate it is born dhruvam surely

Hearing the king's words, Sādhu said respectfully: “I too have no children; surely through this they will be born.”

tato nivṛtya vāṇijyātsānando gṛhamāyayau |
bhāryāyai kathitaṃ sarvaṃ vrataṃ santatidāyakam ||
tadā vrataṃ kariṣyāmi yadā me santatirbhavet |
iti līlāvatīṃ prāha svapatnīṃ sādhusattamaḥ ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then nivṛtya returning vāṇijyāt from trade sa-ānandaḥ joyful gṛham home āyayau he came bhāryāyai to his wife kathitam was told sarvam all about vratam the vow santati-dāyakam that gives offspring tadā then vratam the vow kariṣyāmi I shall perform yadā when me to me santatiḥ a child bhavet is born iti thus līlāvatīm to Līlāvatī prāha said sva-patnīm his wife sādhu-sattamaḥ the worthy Sādhu

Returning from his trading, he came home happy and told his wife all about the vow that grants children. “When a child is born to me, I will keep the vow,” said that excellent merchant to his wife Līlāvatī.

ekasmindivase tasya bhāryā līlāvatī satī |
bhartṛyuktānandacittā'bhavaddharmaparāyaṇā ||
garbhiṇī sā'bhavattasya bhāryā satyaprasādataḥ |
daśame māsi vai tasyāḥ kanyāratnamajāyata ||

word by word · translation

ekasmin on a certain divase day tasya his bhāryā wife līlāvatī Līlāvatī satī the virtuous bhartṛ-yuktā united with her husband ānanda-cittā glad at heart abhavat became dharma-parāyaṇā devoted to dharma garbhiṇī with child sā she abhavat became tasya his bhāryā wife satya-prasādataḥ by the grace of Satya daśame in the tenth māsi month vai indeed tasyāḥ to her kanyā-ratnam a jewel of a daughter ajāyata was born

One day his virtuous wife Līlāvatī, devoted to dharma, was joyful with her husband; by the grace of Satya she conceived, and in the tenth month a jewel of a daughter was born to her.

dine dine sā vavṛdhe śuklapakṣe yathā śaśī |
nāmnā kalāvatī ceti tannāmakaraṇaṃ kṛtam ||

word by word · translation

dine dine day by day sā she vavṛdhe grew śukla-pakṣe in the bright fortnight yathā as śaśī the moon nāmnā by name kalāvatī Kalāvatī ca and iti thus tat-nāma-karaṇam her naming kṛtam was done

Day by day she grew, like the moon in the bright fortnight; she was given the name Kalāvatī.

tato līlāvatī prāha svāminaṃ madhuraṃ vacaḥ |
na karoṣi kimarthaṃ vai purā saṅkalpitaṃ vratam ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then līlāvatī Līlāvatī prāha spoke svāminam to her husband madhuram sweet vacaḥ words na not karoṣi do you perform kim-artham why vai indeed purā earlier saṅkalpitam resolved upon vratam the vow

Then Līlāvatī spoke sweetly to her husband: “Why do you not keep the vow you resolved on before?”

vivāhasamaye tasyāḥ kariṣyāmi vrataṃ priye |
iti bhāryāṃ samāśvāsya jagāma nagaraṃ prati ||

word by word · translation

vivāha-samaye at the time of the wedding tasyāḥ of her kariṣyāmi I shall perform vratam the vow priye my dear iti thus bhāryām his wife samāśvāsya reassuring jagāma he went nagaram the city prati to

“At the time of her marriage I will keep the vow, my dear.” So reassuring his wife, he went off to the city.

tataḥ kalāvatī kanyā vavṛdhe pitṛveśmani |
dṛṣṭvā kanyāṃ tatassādhurnagare sakhibhissaha |
mantrayitvā drutaṃ dūtaṃ preṣayāmāsa dharmavit ||
vivāhārthaṃ ca kanyāyā varaśreṣṭhaṃ vicāraya ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then kalāvatī Kalāvatī kanyā the girl vavṛdhe grew up pitṛ-veśmani in her father's house dṛṣṭvā seeing kanyām his daughter tataḥ then sādhuḥ Sādhu nagare in the city sakhibhiḥ his friends saha with mantrayitvā having taken counsel drutam quickly dūtam a messenger preṣayāmāsa sent dharma-vit knowing what is right vivāha-artham for the marriage ca and kanyāyāḥ of my daughter vara-śreṣṭham the best of bridegrooms vicāraya seek out

The girl Kalāvatī grew up in her father's house. Seeing her grown, Sādhu, knower of dharma, consulted his friends in the city and quickly sent a messenger: “Seek out an excellent groom for my daughter's marriage.”

tenājñaptaśca dūto'sau kāñcanaṃ nagaraṃ yayau |
tasmādekaṃ vaṇikputraṃ samādāyāgato hi saḥ ||
dṛṣṭvā taṃ sundaraṃ bālaṃ vaṇikputraṃ guṇānvitam |
dadau sādhuḥ kumārīṃ tāṃ kanyāṃ vidhividhānataḥ ||

word by word · translation

tena by him ājñaptaḥ commanded ca and dūtaḥ messenger asau that kāñcanam to Kāñcana nagaram the city yayau went tasmāt from there ekam a certain vaṇik-putram merchant's son samādāya bringing āgataḥ returned hi indeed saḥ he dṛṣṭvā seeing tam that sundaram handsome bālam youth vaṇik-putram the merchant's son guṇa-anvitam endowed with virtues dadau gave sādhuḥ Sādhu kumārīm maiden tām that kanyām his daughter vidhi-vidhānataḥ with the due rites

So commanded, the messenger went to the city of Kāñcana and came back with a merchant's son. Seeing that handsome youth, a merchant's son endowed with virtues, Sādhu gave him his daughter in marriage with due rites.

tato'bhāgyavaśāttena vismṛtaṃ vratamuttamam |
vivāhasamaye tasyāstena ruṣṭo'bhavatprabhuḥ ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then abhāgya-vaśāt by ill fortune tena by him vismṛtam was forgotten vratam the vow uttamam excellent vivāha-samaye at the time of the wedding tasyāḥ of her tena by that ruṣṭaḥ angered abhavat became prabhuḥ the Lord

But by ill fortune he forgot the excellent vow at the time of her wedding, and the Lord was angered by it.

tataḥ kālena niyato nijakarmaviśāradaḥ |
vāṇijyārthaṃ gataśśīghraṃ jāmātṛsahito vaṇik ||
ratnasānupure ramye sthitvā sindhusamīpataḥ |
vāṇijyamakarotsādhurjāmātrā śrīmatā saha ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then kālena in time niyataḥ regular nija-karma-viśāradaḥ skilled in his own trade vāṇijya-artham for trade gataḥ went śīghram quickly jāmātṛ-sahitaḥ with his son-in-law vaṇik the merchant ratnasānu-pure in the city of Ratnasānu ramye lovely sthitvā staying sindhu-samīpataḥ near the sea vāṇijyam trade akarot carried on sādhuḥ Sādhu jāmātrā his son-in-law śrīmatā the prosperous saha with

In time the merchant, diligent and skilled in his work, soon set out to trade together with his son-in-law. Staying in the lovely city of Ratnasānu by the sea, Sādhu traded there with his prosperous son-in-law.

tau gatau nagaraṃ ramyaṃ candraketornṛpasya ca |
etasminnantare kāle satyanārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ ||
bhraṣṭapratijñamālokya śāpaṃ tasmai pradattavān |
dāruṇaṃ kaṭhinaṃ cāsya mahadduḥkhaṃ bhaviṣyati ||

word by word · translation

tau those two gatau went nagaram to the city ramyam lovely candraketoḥ of Candraketu nṛpasya the king ca and etasmin at that antare same kāle time satyanārāyaṇaḥ Satyanārāyaṇa prabhuḥ the Lord bhraṣṭa-pratijñam one who had broken his promise ālokya seeing śāpam a curse tasmai on him pradattavān laid dāruṇam dreadful kaṭhinam harsh ca and asya his mahat great duḥkham sorrow bhaviṣyati shall be

The two had come to the lovely city of King Candraketu. Just then the Lord Satyanārāyaṇa, seeing him fall from his promise, laid a curse on him: “Dreadful, hard and great sorrow shall come upon him.”

etasmindivase rājño dhanamādāya taskaraḥ |
tatraiva cāgataścauryādvaṇijau yatra saṃsthitau ||
tatpaścāddhāvakāndūtāndṛṣṭvā bhītena cetasā |
dhanaṃ saṃsthāpya tatraiva sa tu śīghramalakṣitaḥ ||

word by word · translation

etasmin on that divase day rājñaḥ the king's dhanam wealth ādāya taking taskaraḥ a thief tatra there eva itself ca and āgataḥ came cauryāt after the theft vaṇijau the two merchants yatra where saṃsthitau were staying tat-paścāt behind him dhāvakān running dūtān the guards dṛṣṭvā seeing bhītena frightened cetasā with a mind dhanam the wealth saṃsthāpya setting down tatra there eva itself saḥ he tu then śīghram quickly alakṣitaḥ slipped away unseen

That day a thief who had stolen the king's treasure came to the very place where the two merchants were staying. Seeing the king's men running after him, he took fright, left the treasure right there, and quickly slipped away unseen.

tato dūtāssamāyātā yatrāste sajjano vaṇik |
dṛṣṭvā nṛpadhanaṃ tatra baddhvā nītau vaṇiksutau ||
harṣeṇa dhāvamānāśca ūcurnṛpasamīpataḥ |
taskarau dvau samānītau vilokyājñāpaya prabho ||
rājñājñaptāstataśśīghraṃ dṛḍhaṃ baddhvā tu tāvubhau |
sthāpitau ca mahādurge kārāgāre'vicārataḥ ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then dūtāḥ the guards samāyātāḥ arrived yatra where āste was sat-janaḥ the good vaṇik merchant dṛṣṭvā seeing nṛpa-dhanam the king's wealth tatra there baddhvā having bound nītau they led away vaṇik-sutau the two merchants harṣeṇa with joy dhāvamānāḥ running ca and ūcuḥ they said nṛpa-samīpataḥ before the king taskarau thieves dvau two samānītau have been brought vilokya having looked ājñāpaya give your command prabho O lord rājñā by the king ājñaptāḥ commanded tataḥ then śīghram quickly dṛḍham tightly baddhvā binding tu indeed tau those ubhau two sthāpitau they were put ca and mahā-durge in a great stronghold kārā-āgāre in prison avicārataḥ without inquiry

The messengers came to where the good merchant was, saw the king's wealth there, bound the two merchants and led them away. Running gleefully to the king they said: “We have brought two thieves; look at them and give your command, lord.” At the king's command they quickly bound both fast and, without any inquiry, put them in prison in a great fortress.

māyayā satyadevasya na śrutaṃ taistayorvacaḥ |
tatastayordhanaṃ rājñā gṛhītaṃ candraketunā ||
tacchāpācca tayorgehe bhāryā caivātiduḥkhitā |
coreṇāpahṛtaṃ sarvaṃ gṛhe yacca sthitaṃ dhanam ||

word by word · translation

māyayā by the illusion satya-devasya of the god Satya na not śrutam heard taiḥ by them tayoḥ of the two vacaḥ the plea tataḥ then tayoḥ their dhanam wealth rājñā by king gṛhītam was seized candraketunā Candraketu tat-śāpāt from that curse ca and tayoḥ their gehe at home bhāryā the wife ca and eva indeed ati-duḥkhitā was sorely afflicted coreṇa by a thief apahṛtam was stolen sarvam all gṛhe in the house yat that ca and sthitam was dhanam the wealth

By Satyadeva's māyā no one listened to their words, and King Candraketu seized their wealth. Through that curse, at their home the wife too fell into great sorrow: all the wealth kept in the house was carried off by a thief.

ādhivyādhisamāyuktā kṣutpipāsātiduḥkhitā |
annacintāparā bhūtvā babhrāma ca gṛhe gṛhe ||
kalāvatī tu kanyāpi babhrāma prativāsaram ||

word by word · translation

ādhi-vyādhi-samāyuktā beset by anxiety and illness kṣut-pipāsā-ati-duḥkhitā sorely afflicted by hunger and thirst anna-cintā-parā thinking only of food bhūtvā being babhrāma she wandered ca and gṛhe gṛhe from house to house kalāvatī Kalāvatī tu too kanyā the daughter api also babhrāma wandered prati-vāsaram every day

Beset by grief and sickness, tormented by hunger and thirst, anxious for food, she wandered from house to house; and the girl Kalāvatī too wandered about every day.

ekasmindivase yātā kṣudhārtā dvijamandiram |
gatā'paśyadvrataṃ tatra satyanārāyaṇasya ca ||
upaviśya kathāṃ śrutvā devaṃ prārthitavatyapi |
prasādabhakṣaṇaṃ kṛtvā yayau rātriṃ gṛhaṃ prati ||

word by word · translation

ekasmin on a certain divase day yātā she went kṣudhā-ārtā pained by hunger dvija-mandiram to a brāhmaṇa's house gatā having gone apaśyat she saw vratam the vow tatra there satyanārāyaṇasya of Satyanārāyaṇa ca and upaviśya sitting down kathām the story śrutvā hearing devam the god prārthitavatī she prayed to api also prasāda-bhakṣaṇam the eating of the consecrated food kṛtvā having done yayau she went rātrim at night gṛham home prati towards

One day, pinched with hunger, she went to a brahmin's house, and there she saw the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa being kept. She sat down, heard the story, prayed to the god, ate the prasāda, and went home at night.

mātā kalāvatīṃ kanyāṃ kathayāmāsa premataḥ |
putri rātrau sthitā kutra kiṃ te manasi vartate ||
kanyā kalāvatī prāha mātaraṃ prati satvaram |
dvijālaye vrataṃ mātardṛṣṭaṃ vāñchitasiddhidam ||

word by word · translation

mātā the mother kalāvatīm to Kalāvatī kanyām her daughter kathayāmāsa spoke premataḥ with love putri daughter rātrau at night sthitā have you stayed kutra where kim what te your manasi in mind vartate is kanyā the girl kalāvatī Kalāvatī prāha said mātaram her mother prati to sa-tvaram quickly dvija-ālaye in a brāhmaṇa's house vratam a vow mātaḥ mother dṛṣṭam I saw vāñchita-siddhi-dam that grants what one desires

Her mother spoke lovingly to Kalāvatī: “Daughter, where were you till night? What is on your mind?” The girl Kalāvatī answered her mother at once: “Mother, at a brahmin's house I saw a vow that fulfils one's wishes.”

tacchrutvā kanyakāvākyaṃ vrataṃ kartuṃ samudyatā |
sā mudā tu vaṇigbhāryā satyanārāyaṇasya vai ||
vrataṃ cakre tadā sādhvī bandhubhissvajanaissaha |
bhartṛjāmātarau kṣipramāgacchetāṃ svamāśrayam ||
aparādhaṃ ca me bhartuḥ jāmātuḥ kṣantumarhasi ||

word by word · translation

tat that śrutvā hearing kanyakā-vākyam word of her daughter vratam the vow kartum to perform samudyatā she made ready sā she mudā with joy tu indeed vaṇik-bhāryā the merchant's wife satyanārāyaṇasya of Satyanārāyaṇa vai indeed vratam the vow cakre performed tadā then sādhvī the good woman bandhubhiḥ with kinsmen sva-janaiḥ her own people saha together with bhartṛ-jāmātarau may my husband and son-in-law kṣipram quickly āgacchetām come svam to their own āśrayam home aparādham the offence ca and me of my bhartuḥ husband jāmātuḥ and son-in-law kṣantum to forgive arhasi be pleased

Hearing her daughter's words, she resolved to keep the vow. Joyfully the merchant's good wife kept the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa with her kin and people: “May my husband and son-in-law quickly come home. Please forgive the offence of my husband and son-in-law.”

vratenānena santuṣṭassatyanārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ |
darśayāmāsa svapnaṃ hi candraketornṛpasya ca ||
bandhitau mocaya prātarvaṇijau nṛpasattama |
deyaṃ dhanaṃ ca tatsarvaṃ gṛhītaṃ yattvayādhunā ||
no cettvāṃ nāśayiṣyāmi sarājyadhanaputrakam |
evamābhāṣya rājānaṃ dhyānagamyo'bhavatprabhuḥ ||

word by word · translation

vratena by vow anena this santuṣṭaḥ pleased satyanārāyaṇaḥ Satyanārāyaṇa prabhuḥ the Lord darśayāmāsa showed svapnam a dream hi indeed candraketoḥ to Candraketu nṛpasya the king ca and bandhitau imprisoned mocaya release prātaḥ at dawn vaṇijau the two merchants nṛpa-sattama O best of kings deyam is to be given back dhanam wealth ca and tat that sarvam all gṛhītam taken yat which was tvayā by you adhunā now no cet if not tvām you nāśayiṣyāmi I shall destroy sa-rājya-dhana-putrakam with your kingdom, wealth and sons evam thus ābhāṣya having addressed rājānam the king dhyāna-gamyaḥ lost to sight abhavat became prabhuḥ the Lord

Pleased by this vow, the Lord Satyanārāyaṇa appeared in a dream to King Candraketu: “Best of kings, release the two bound merchants at dawn, and give back all the wealth you have taken. Otherwise I will destroy you with your kingdom, wealth and sons.” Having spoken so to the king, the Lord withdrew, to be reached only in meditation.

tataḥ prabhātasamaye rājā ca svajanaissaha |
upaviśya sabhāmadhye prāha svapnaṃ janaṃ prati ||
baddhau mahājanau śīghraṃ mocayadhvaṃ vaṇiksutau |
iti rājño vacaśśrutvā mocayitvā mahājanau ||
samānīya nṛpasyāgre prāhuste vinayānvitāḥ |
ānītau dvau vaṇikputrau muktau nigalabandhanāt ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then prabhāta-samaye at daybreak rājā the king ca and sva-janaiḥ his people saha with upaviśya sitting sabhā-madhye in the assembly prāha told svapnam the dream janam the people prati to baddhau bound mahā-janau merchants śīghram quickly mocayadhvam release vaṇik-sutau the two iti thus rājñaḥ the king's vacaḥ words śrutvā hearing mocayitvā having released mahā-janau the two merchants samānīya bringing them nṛpasya the king agre before prāhuḥ said te they vinaya-anvitāḥ with humility ānītau have been brought dvau the two vaṇik-putrau merchants muktau freed nigala-bandhanāt from the bondage of fetters

At daybreak the king sat in the midst of his court with his people and told them the dream: “Quickly release the two worthy merchants who are in bonds.” Hearing the king's words, they released the two good men, brought them before the king, and said humbly: “The two merchants have been brought, freed from their fetters.”

tato mahājanau natvā candraketuṃ nṛpottamam |
smarantau pūrvavṛttāntaṃ nocaturbhayavihvalau ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then mahā-janau the two merchants natvā bowing to candraketum Candraketu nṛpa-uttamam the excellent king smarantau remembering pūrva-vṛttāntam what had happened before na nothing ūcatuḥ said bhaya-vihvalau overcome with fear

The two merchants bowed to Candraketu, best of kings, and remembering what had happened before, stood speechless, trembling with fear.

rājā vaṇiksutau vīkṣya vacaḥ provāca sādaram |
daivātprāptaṃ mahadduḥkhamidānīṃ nāsti vai bhayam ||
tadā nigalasantyāgaṃ kṣaurakarmādyakārayat |
vastrādyalaṅkṛtiṃ dattvā paritoṣya nṛpaśca tau ||
puraskṛtya vaṇikputrau vacasā'toṣayadbhṛśam |
purānītaṃ tu yaddravyaṃ dviguṇīkṛtya dattavān ||

word by word · translation

rājā the king vaṇik-sutau the two merchants vīkṣya looking at vacaḥ words provāca spoke sa-ādaram with respect daivāt by fate prāptam has come mahat great duḥkham sorrow idānīm now na asti there is no vai indeed bhayam fear tadā then nigala-santyāgam the removal of the fetters kṣaura-karma-ādi shaving and the rest akārayat he had done vastra-ādi-alaṅkṛtim adornment with clothes and the like dattvā giving paritoṣya gladdening nṛpaḥ the king ca and tau the two puraskṛtya honouring vaṇik-putrau the two merchants vacasā with words atoṣayat he pleased them bhṛśam greatly purā earlier ānītam taken tu indeed yat which dravyam wealth dvi-guṇī-kṛtya doubling it dattavān he gave

Looking at the merchants, the king said kindly: “By fate great sorrow befell you; now there is nothing to fear.” He had their fetters removed, had them shaved and bathed, gave them clothes and ornaments and made them content. Honouring the two merchants, he cheered them greatly with his words, and gave back twice the wealth he had taken.

provāca tau tato rājā gaccha sādho nijāśramam |
rājānaṃ praṇipatyāha gantavyaṃ tvatprasādataḥ ||

word by word · translation

provāca spoke tau to the two tataḥ then rājā the king gaccha go sādho O Sādhu nija-āśramam to your own home rājānam to the king praṇipatya bowing āha he said gantavyam I must go tvat-prasādataḥ by your grace

Then the king said to them: “Go home, Sādhu.” Bowing to the king he said: “By your grace we shall go.”

iti tṛtīyo'dhyāyaḥ ||

word by word · translation

iti thus ends tṛtīyaḥ the third adhyāyaḥ chapter

Thus ends the third chapter.

Caturtho'dhyāyaḥ · Chapter Four ↑

sūta uvāca —
yātrāṃ kṛtvā tatassādhurmaṅgalācārapūrvakam |
brāhmaṇebhyo dhanaṃ dattvā yayau svanagaraṃ prati ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said yātrām his journey kṛtvā beginning tataḥ then sādhuḥ Sādhu maṅgala-ācāra-pūrvakam after auspicious rites brāhmaṇebhyaḥ to brāhmaṇas dhanam wealth dattvā giving yayau set out sva-nagaram his own town prati for

Sūta said: “Then Sādhu, having performed the auspicious rites for a journey and given wealth to brahmins, set out for his own city.”

kiyaddūraṃ gate cābdhau satyanārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ |
jijñāsāṃ kṛtavānsādho naukāyāmasti kiṃ tava ||

word by word · translation

kiyat-dūram some distance gate when he had gone ca and abdhau on the sea satyanārāyaṇaḥ Satyanārāyaṇa prabhuḥ the Lord jijñāsām a test kṛtavān made sādho O Sādhu naukāyām in boat asti is there kim what tava your

When he had gone some distance on the sea, the Lord Satyanārāyaṇa, wishing to test him, asked: “Sādhu, what is in your boat?”

tato mahājanau mattau helayā ca prahasya vai |
kathaṃ pṛcchasi bho daṇḍin mudrāṃ netuṃ kimicchasi ||
latāpatrādikaṃ caiva vartate taraṇau mama |
ityuktaṃ vacanaṃ śrutvā satyaṃ bhavatu te vacaḥ ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then mahā-janau the two merchants mattau puffed up helayā with scorn ca and prahasya laughing vai indeed katham why pṛcchasi do you ask bho O daṇḍin mendicant mudrām our money netum to carry off kim do icchasi you wish latā-patra-ādikam creepers, leaves and the like ca and eva only vartate there is taraṇau in boat mama my iti thus uktam spoken vacanam the word śrutvā hearing satyam true bhavatu may be te your vacaḥ word

The two merchants, proud of their wealth, laughed at him scornfully: “Why do you ask, ascetic? Do you want to carry off our money? There is nothing in my boat but creepers and leaves.” Hearing these words he said: “Let your word be true.”

evamuktvā gataśśīghraṃ daṇḍī tasya samīpataḥ |
kiyaddūraṃ tato gatvā sthitassindhusamīpataḥ ||
gate daṇḍini sādhuśca kṛtanityakriyastathā |
utthitāṃ taraṇiṃ dṛṣṭvā vismayaṃ paramaṃ yayau ||

word by word · translation

evam thus uktvā having spoken gataḥ went śīghram quickly daṇḍī the mendicant tasya his samīpataḥ from presence kiyat-dūram some distance tataḥ then gatvā going sthitaḥ he stayed sindhu-samīpataḥ near the sea gate had gone daṇḍini when the mendicant sādhuḥ Sādhu ca and kṛta-nitya-kriyaḥ having done his daily rites tathā then utthitām riding high taraṇim the boat dṛṣṭvā seeing vismayam astonishment paramam utter yayau he fell into

Having said this, the ascetic quickly left him, went some distance away, and stayed near the shore. When the ascetic had gone, Sādhu finished his daily rites, and seeing the boat riding high in the water, was utterly amazed.

dṛṣṭvā latādikaṃ caiva mūrchito nyapatadbhuvi |
labdhasaṃjño vaṇikputrastataśśokānvito'bhavat ||

word by word · translation

dṛṣṭvā seeing latā-ādikam creepers and the like ca and eva only mūrchitaḥ fainting nyapatat he fell bhuvi on the ground labdha-saṃjñaḥ regaining his senses vaṇik-putraḥ the merchant tataḥ then śoka-anvitaḥ filled with grief abhavat became

Seeing only creepers and leaves in it, he fainted and fell to the ground; when he came to, the merchant was overcome with grief.

tadā tu duhituḥ kānto vacanaṃ cedamabravīt |
kimarthaṃ kriyate śokaśśāpo dattaśca daṇḍinā ||
śakyate'nena sarvaṃ hi kartuṃ cātra na saṃśayaḥ |
atastaṃ śaraṇaṃ yāvo vāñchitārtho bhaviṣyati ||

word by word · translation

tadā then tu indeed duhituḥ his daughter's kāntaḥ husband vacanam words ca and idam these abravīt spoke kim-artham why kriyate is made śokaḥ lament śāpaḥ a curse dattaḥ was laid ca and daṇḍinā by the mendicant śakyate it is possible anena by him sarvam everything hi indeed kartum to do ca and atra of this na there is no saṃśayaḥ doubt ataḥ so tam to him śaraṇam for refuge yāvaḥ let us two go vāñchita-arthaḥ what we desire bhaviṣyati will come about

Then his daughter's husband said: “Why grieve? The ascetic has laid a curse on us. He can do anything, no doubt of it. So let us go to him for refuge; what we wish for will come about.”

jāmāturvacanaṃ śrutvā tatsakāśaṃ gato vaṇik |
dṛṣṭvā ca daṇḍinaṃ bhaktyā natvā provāca sādaram ||
kṣamasva cāparādhaṃ me yaduktaṃ tava sannidhau |
evaṃ punaḥ punarnatvā mahāśokākulo'bhavat ||

word by word · translation

jāmātuḥ his son-in-law's vacanam words śrutvā hearing tat-sakāśam to his presence gataḥ went vaṇik the merchant dṛṣṭvā seeing ca and daṇḍinam the mendicant bhaktyā with devotion natvā bowing provāca he said sa-ādaram with respect kṣamasva forgive ca and aparādham offence me my yat what uktam was said tava your sannidhau in presence evam thus punaḥ punaḥ again and again natvā bowing mahā-śoka-ākulaḥ overwhelmed with great grief abhavat he became

Hearing his son-in-law's words the merchant went to him, and seeing the ascetic, bowed with devotion and said respectfully: “Forgive the offence of what I said before you.” Bowing again and again, he was overwhelmed with grief.

provāca vacanaṃ daṇḍī vilapantaṃ vilokya ca |
mā rodīśśṛṇu madvākyaṃ mama pūjābahirmukhaḥ ||
mamājñayaiva durbuddhe labdhaṃ duḥkhaṃ muhurmuhuḥ ||

word by word · translation
