provāca spoke vacanam words daṇḍī the mendicant vilapantam him lamenting vilokya seeing ca and mā do not rodīḥ weep śṛṇu hear mat-vākyam my word mama my pūjā-bahiḥ-mukhaḥ you turned away from worship mama my ājñayā by command eva alone durbuddhe O foolish one labdham has come to you duḥkham sorrow muhuḥ muhuḥ again and again

Seeing him lament, the ascetic said: “Do not weep; hear my words. You turned away from my worship; by my command alone, foolish one, you have met sorrow again and again.”

sādhuruvāca —
tacchrutvā bhagavadvākyaṃ stutiṃ kartuṃ samudyataḥ ||
tvanmāyāmohitāssarve brahmādyāstridivaukasaḥ |
na jānanti guṇānrūpaṃ tavāścaryamidaṃ prabho ||
mūḍho'haṃ tvāṃ kathaṃ jāne mohitastava māyayā |
prasīda pūjayiṣyāmi yathāvibhavavistaram ||
purā vittaṃ ca yatsarvamanugṛhṇīṣva tatprabho |
pāhi māṃ puṇḍarīkākṣa tvāmadya śaraṇāgatam ||

word by word · translation

sādhuḥ Sādhu uvāca said tat that śrutvā hearing bhagavat-vākyam word of the Lord stutim praise kartum to offer samudyataḥ he made ready tvat-māyā-mohitāḥ deluded by your illusion sarve are all brahma-ādyāḥ Brahmā and the other tridiva-okasaḥ dwellers in heaven na not jānanti they know guṇān qualities rūpam and form tava your āścaryam a wonder idam is this prabho O Lord mūḍhaḥ a fool aham I tvām you katham how jāne can I know mohitaḥ deluded tava by your māyayā illusion prasīda be gracious pūjayiṣyāmi I shall worship you yathā-vibhava-vistaram as amply as my means allow purā as before vittam wealth ca and yat which was sarvam all anugṛhṇīṣva graciously restore tat that prabho O Lord pāhi protect mām me puṇḍarīka-akṣa O lotus-eyed tvām to you adya today śaraṇa-āgatam come for refuge

Hearing the Lord's words, Sādhu set about praising him: “Deluded by your māyā, all the dwellers of heaven, Brahmā and the rest, do not know your qualities or your form — this is a wonder, Lord. I am a fool; how should I know you, deluded by your māyā? Be gracious: I will worship you with all the splendour my means allow. Restore, Lord, all the wealth that was mine before. Protect me, lotus-eyed one, who today come to you for refuge.”

śrutvā bhaktiyutaṃ vākyaṃ parituṣṭo janārdanaḥ |
varaṃ ca vāñchitaṃ dattvā tatraivāntaradhīyata ||

word by word · translation

śrutvā hearing bhakti-yutam filled with devotion vākyam speech parituṣṭaḥ well pleased janārdanaḥ Janārdana varam the boon ca and vāñchitam desired dattvā granting tatra there eva itself antaradhīyata vanished

Hearing these words full of devotion, Janārdana was pleased; granting the boon he wished, he vanished on the spot.

tato nāvaṃ samāruhya dṛṣṭvā vittaprapūritām |
kṛpayā satyadevasya saphalaṃ vāñchitaṃ mama ||
ityuktvā svajanaissārdhaṃ pūjāṃ kṛtvā yathāvidhi |
harṣeṇa cābhavatpūrṇassatyadevaprasādataḥ ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then nāvam the boat samāruhya boarding dṛṣṭvā seeing it vitta-prapūritām filled with wealth kṛpayā by the mercy satya-devasya of the god Satya sa-phalam is fulfilled vāñchitam wish mama my iti thus uktvā saying sva-janaiḥ his people sārdham together with pūjām the worship kṛtvā performing yathā-vidhi by the rule harṣeṇa with joy ca and abhavat he became pūrṇaḥ filled satya-deva-prasādataḥ by the grace of the god Satya

Then, boarding the boat and seeing it full of wealth, he said: “By the mercy of Satyadeva my wish is fulfilled.” Saying this, he worshipped duly with his people, and was filled with joy by Satyadeva's grace.

sādhurjāmātaraṃ prāha paśya ratnapurīṃ mama |
dūtaṃ ca preṣayāmāsa nijavittasya rakṣakam ||

word by word · translation

sādhuḥ Sādhu jāmātaram to his son-in-law prāha said paśya behold ratna-purīm Ratnapurī mama my city dūtam a messenger ca and preṣayāmāsa he sent nija-vittasya of his own wealth rakṣakam the keeper

Sādhu said to his son-in-law, “Look, there is my city of jewels,” and sent a messenger, the guardian of his wealth, ahead.

dūto'sau nagaraṃ gatvā sādhubhāryāṃ vilokya ca |
provācātmahitaṃ vākyaṃ natvā baddhāñjalistathā ||
nikaṭe nagarasyaiva jāmātrā sahito vaṇik |
āgato bandhuvargaiśca svajanairbahubhiryutaḥ ||

word by word · translation

dūtaḥ messenger asau that nagaram to the city gatvā going sādhu-bhāryām Sādhu's wife vilokya seeing ca and provāca spoke ātma-hitam welcome vākyam words natvā bowing baddha-añjaliḥ with folded hands tathā so nikaṭe near nagarasya the city eva itself jāmātrā his son-in-law sahitaḥ with vaṇik the merchant āgataḥ has arrived bandhu-vargaiḥ by his kinsfolk ca and sva-janaiḥ his own people bahubhiḥ many yutaḥ accompanied

The messenger went to the city, saw Sādhu's wife, bowed with folded hands and gave her the welcome news: “The merchant has arrived near the city with his son-in-law, together with many kinsfolk and people.”

śrutvā dūtamukhādvākyaṃ mahāharṣavatī satī |
satyapūjāṃ tataḥ kṛtvā provāca tanujāṃ prati ||
vrajāmi śīghramāgaccha sādhusandarśanāya ca |
iti mātṛvacaśśrutvā vrataṃ kṛtvā samāpya ca ||
prasādaṃ tu parityajya gatā sāpi patiṃ prati ||

word by word · translation

śrutvā hearing dūta-mukhāt from the messenger's mouth vākyam the news mahā-harṣavatī full of great joy satī the good wife satya-pūjām the worship of Satya tataḥ then kṛtvā performing provāca she said tanujām her daughter prati to vrajāmi I am going śīghram quickly āgaccha come sādhu-sandarśanāya to see Sādhu ca and iti thus mātṛ-vacaḥ her mother's words śrutvā hearing vratam the vow kṛtvā performing samāpya completing it ca and prasādam the consecrated food tu but parityajya leaving gatā went sā she api also patim her husband prati to

Hearing the message from the messenger's mouth, the good woman was overjoyed. Having done the worship of Satya, she said to her daughter: “I am going; come quickly to see Sādhu.” Hearing her mother's words, the daughter finished the vow — but leaving the prasāda behind, she too went to meet her husband.

tena ruṣṭassatyadevo bhartāraṃ taraṇiṃ tadā |
saṃhṛtya ca dhanaissārdhaṃ jale tasminnamajjayat ||
dṛṣṭvā tathāvidhāṃ nāvaṃ kanyāṃ ca bahuduḥkhitām |
bhītena manasā duḥkhamāpurāścaryamantikāḥ ||
cintayānāśca te sarve babhūvustīravāsakāḥ ||

word by word · translation

tena by that ruṣṭaḥ angered satya-devaḥ the god Satya bhartāram her husband taraṇim and the boat tadā then saṃhṛtya seizing ca and dhanaiḥ the wealth sārdham together with jale in water tasmin that amajjayat sank them dṛṣṭvā seeing tathā-vidhām in such a state nāvam the boat kanyām the girl ca and bahu-duḥkhitām greatly distressed bhītena frightened manasā with a mind duḥkham sorrow āpuḥ they felt āścaryam and wonder antikāḥ those nearby cintayānāḥ full of anxious thought ca and te they sarve all babhūvuḥ became tīra-vāsakāḥ who stood on the shore

Angered by that, Satyadeva seized the husband and the boat together with the wealth, and sank them in that water. Seeing the boat so, and the girl in great sorrow, those nearby were frightened, grieved and amazed; all who stood on the shore became anxious.

tato līlāvatī kanyāṃ dṛṣṭvā sā vihvalābhavat |
vilalāpātiduḥkhena bhartāraṃ cedamabravīt ||
idānīṃ naukayā sārdhaṃ kathaṃ so'bhūdalakṣitaḥ |
na jāne kasya devasya helayā nauśca sā hṛtā ||
satyadevasya māhātmyaṃ jñātuṃ kartuṃ na śakyate |
ityuktvā vilalāpaivaṃ tataśca svajanaissaha ||
tato līlāvatī kanyāṃ kroḍe kṛtvā ruroda hi ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then līlāvatī Līlāvatī kanyām her daughter dṛṣṭvā seeing sā she vihvalā distraught abhavat became vilalāpa she wailed ati-duḥkhena in great sorrow bhartāram to her husband ca and idam this abravīt said idānīm just now naukayā the boat sārdham together with katham how saḥ he abhūt has become alakṣitaḥ unseen na not jāne I know kasya of which devasya god helayā by the slighting nauḥ boat ca and sā that hṛtā was taken satya-devasya of the god Satya māhātmyam the greatness jñātum to know kartum or to act on na not śakyate is it possible iti thus uktvā saying vilalāpa she wailed evam so tataḥ then ca and sva-janaiḥ her people saha with tataḥ then līlāvatī Līlāvatī kanyām her daughter kroḍe on her lap kṛtvā taking ruroda wept hi indeed

Then Līlāvatī, seeing her daughter, was distraught; she wept in great sorrow and said to her husband: “How has he vanished just now together with the boat? I do not know by the slight of which god that boat was taken. Satyadeva's greatness cannot be known or wrought by us.” So saying she lamented with her people, and taking her daughter in her lap, she wept.

tataḥ kalāvatī kanyā naṣṭe svāmini duḥkhitā |
gṛhītvā pāduke tasya hyanugantuṃ mano dadhau ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then kalāvatī Kalāvatī kanyā the girl naṣṭe being lost svāmini her husband duḥkhitā grieving gṛhītvā taking pāduke the sandals tasya his hi indeed anugantum to follow him in death manaḥ her mind dadhau she set

Then the girl Kalāvatī, grieving for her lost husband, took his sandals and set her mind on following him in death.

kanyāyāścaritaṃ dṛṣṭvā sabhāryassvajano vaṇik |
atiśokena santaptaścintayāmāsa dharmavit ||
hṛtaṃ vā satyadevena bhrānto'haṃ satyamāyayā |
satyapūjāṃ kariṣyāmi yathāvibhavavistaram ||
iti sarvānsamāhūya kathayitvā manogatam |
natvā ca daṇḍavadbhūmau satyadevaṃ punaḥ punaḥ ||

word by word · translation

kanyāyāḥ his daughter's caritam conduct dṛṣṭvā seeing sa-bhāryaḥ with his wife sva-janaḥ and people vaṇik the merchant ati-śokena with great grief santaptaḥ burning cintayāmāsa reflected dharma-vit knowing what is right hṛtam taken away vā surely satya-devena by the god Satya bhrāntaḥ bewildered aham am I satya-māyayā by Satya's illusion satya-pūjām the worship of Satya kariṣyāmi I shall perform yathā-vibhava-vistaram as amply as my means allow iti thus sarvān all samāhūya calling together kathayitvā telling manaḥ-gatam what was in his mind natvā he bowed ca and daṇḍa-vat like a staff bhūmau on the ground satya-devam to the god Satya punaḥ punaḥ again and again

Seeing what his daughter was doing, the merchant, knower of dharma, with his wife and people, burned with great grief and reflected: “Surely it was taken by Satyadeva; I am bewildered by Satya's māyā. I will do the worship of Satya with all the splendour my means allow.” Calling everyone together and telling them his mind, he prostrated full length on the ground to Satyadeva again and again.

tatastuṣṭassatyadevo dīnānāṃ paripālakaḥ |
jagāda vacanaṃ caivaṃ kṛpayā bhaktavatsalaḥ ||
tyaktvā prasādaṃ te kanyā patiṃ draṣṭuṃ samāgatā |
ato'dṛṣṭo'bhavattasyāḥ kanyakāyāḥ patirdhruvam ||
gṛhaṃ gatvā tatprasādaṃ bhuktvā sāyāti cetpunaḥ |
labdhabhartrī sutā sādho bhaviṣyati na saṃśayaḥ ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then tuṣṭaḥ pleased satya-devaḥ the god Satya dīnānām of the afflicted paripālakaḥ protector jagāda spoke vacanam words ca and evam thus kṛpayā in mercy bhakta-vatsalaḥ tender to his devotees tyaktvā having left prasādam the consecrated food te your kanyā daughter patim her husband draṣṭum to see samāgatā came ataḥ therefore adṛṣṭaḥ unseen abhavat has become tasyāḥ of that kanyakāyāḥ girl patiḥ the husband dhruvam surely gṛham home gatvā going tat-prasādam that consecrated food bhuktvā having eaten sā she āyāti comes cet if punaḥ back labdha-bhartrī one whose husband is restored sutā your daughter sādho O Sādhu bhaviṣyati will be na there is no saṃśayaḥ doubt

Then Satyadeva, protector of the helpless, loving to his devotees, was pleased, and spoke thus in mercy: “Your daughter left the prasāda and came to see her husband; that is why her husband vanished. If she goes home, eats the prasāda and comes back, your daughter will regain her husband, Sādhu; there is no doubt.”

kanyakā tādṛśaṃ vākyaṃ śrutvā gaganamaṇḍalāt |
kṣipraṃ tadā gṛhaṃ gatvā prasādamanubhujya ca |
sā paścātpunarāgatya dadarśa svaṃ mudā patim ||

word by word · translation

kanyakā the girl tādṛśam such vākyam words śrutvā hearing gagana-maṇḍalāt from the sky kṣipram quickly tadā then gṛham home gatvā going prasādam the consecrated food anubhujya eating ca and sā she paścāt afterwards punaḥ again āgatya returning dadarśa saw svam her own mudā with joy patim husband

Hearing such words from the sky, the girl went home at once and ate the prasāda; then, coming back, she saw her own husband, and rejoiced.

tataḥ kalāvatī kanyā jagāda pitaraṃ prati |
idānīṃ svagṛhaṃ yāhi vilambaṃ kuruṣe katham ||
tacchrutvā kanyakāvākyaṃ santuṣṭo'bhūdvaṇiksutaḥ |
pūjanaṃ satyadevasya kṛtvā vidhividhānataḥ |
dhanairbandhugaṇaissārdhaṃ jagāma nijamandiram ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then kalāvatī Kalāvatī kanyā the girl jagāda said pitaram her father prati to idānīm now sva-gṛham home yāhi let us go vilambam delay kuruṣe do you katham why tat that śrutvā hearing kanyakā-vākyam word of his daughter santuṣṭaḥ glad abhūt was vaṇik-sutaḥ the merchant pūjanam the worship satya-devasya of the god Satya kṛtvā performing vidhi-vidhānataḥ with the due rites dhanaiḥ with his wealth bandhu-gaṇaiḥ and kinsfolk sārdham together jagāma he went nija-mandiram to his own house

Then the girl Kalāvatī said to her father: “Now let us go home; why do you delay?” Hearing his daughter's words the merchant was content; having worshipped Satyadeva with due rites, he went to his own house with his wealth and his kinsfolk.

paurṇamāsyāṃ ca saṅkrāntau kṛtavānsatyapūjanam |
ihaloke sukhaṃ bhuktvā cānte satyapuraṃ yayau ||

word by word · translation

paurṇamāsyām on the full-moon day ca and saṅkrāntau at the sun's passage kṛtavān he performed satya-pūjanam the worship of Satya iha-loke in this world sukham happiness bhuktvā having enjoyed ca and ante at the end satya-puram to the city of Satya yayau he went

On every full moon and every saṅkrānti he performed the worship of Satya; he enjoyed happiness in this world and at the end went to the city of Satya.

iti caturtho'dhyāyaḥ ||

word by word · translation

iti thus ends caturthaḥ the fourth adhyāyaḥ chapter

Thus ends the fourth chapter.

Pañcamo'dhyāyaḥ · Chapter Five ↑

sūta uvāca —
athānyatsampravakṣyāmi śṛṇudhvaṃ munisattamāḥ |
āsīttuṅgadhvajo rājā prajāpālanatatparaḥ ||

word by word · translation

sūtaḥ the Sūta uvāca said atha now anyat another thing sampravakṣyāmi I shall relate śṛṇudhvam hear muni-sattamāḥ O best of sages āsīt there was tuṅgadhvajaḥ Tuṅgadhvaja rājā a king prajā-pālana-tatparaḥ intent on protecting his people

Sūta said: “Now I will tell you another story; listen, best of sages. There was a king named Tuṅgadhvaja, devoted to protecting his people.”

ekadā sa vanaṃ gatvā hatvā bahuvidhānpaśūn |
āgatya bilvamūlaṃ ca dṛṣṭvā satyasya pūjanam ||
gopāḥ kurvanti santuṣṭā bhaktiyuktāssabāndhavāḥ |
rājā dṛṣṭvāpi darpeṇa na gatvā taṃ nanāma saḥ ||

word by word · translation

ekadā once saḥ he vanam to the forest gatvā going hatvā slaying bahu-vidhān many kinds of paśūn beasts āgatya coming bilva-mūlam to the foot of a bilva tree ca and dṛṣṭvā he saw satyasya of Satya pūjanam the worship gopāḥ cowherds kurvanti were performing santuṣṭāḥ contented bhakti-yuktāḥ full of devotion sa-bāndhavāḥ with their kinsmen rājā the king dṛṣṭvā seeing api though darpeṇa out of pride na neither gatvā went tam to him nanāma nor bowed saḥ he

Once he went into the forest and killed many kinds of animals. Coming to the foot of a bilva tree, he saw cowherds joyfully worshipping Satya with devotion, together with their kin. Though he saw it, the king in his pride did not go near and did not bow to the Lord.

tato gopagaṇāssarve prasādaṃ nṛpasannidhau |
saṃsthāpya punarāgatya bubhujuśca yathepsitam ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then gopa-gaṇāḥ the cowherds sarve all prasādam the consecrated food nṛpa-sannidhau before the king saṃsthāpya placing punaḥ again āgatya returning bubhujuḥ ate ca and yathā-īpsitam as they pleased

Then all the cowherds set the prasāda before the king, came back, and ate as they wished.

tataḥ prasādaṃ santyajya rājā duḥkhamavāpa saḥ |
tasya putraśataṃ naṣṭaṃ dhanadhānyādikaṃ ca yat ||

word by word · translation

tataḥ then prasādam the consecrated food santyajya spurning rājā king duḥkham to sorrow avāpa came saḥ that tasya his putra-śatam hundred sons naṣṭam were lost dhana-dhānya-ādikam wealth, grain and the rest ca and yat that he had

Having rejected the prasāda, the king met with sorrow: his hundred sons perished, and all his wealth, grain and possessions.

satyadevena tatsarvaṃ nāśitaṃ mama niścitam |
atastatraiva gacchāmi yatra devasya pūjanam ||
manaseti viniścitya yayau gopālasannidhim |
tato'sau satyadevasya pūjāṃ gopagaṇaissaha ||
bhaktiśraddhānvito bhūtvā cakāra vidhinā nṛpaḥ |
satyadevaprasādena dhanaputrānvito'bhavat |
ihaloke sukhaṃ bhuktvā cānte satyapuraṃ yayau ||

word by word · translation

satya-devena by the god Satya tat that sarvam all nāśitam has been destroyed mama of mine niścitam for certain ataḥ so tatra there eva itself gacchāmi I shall go yatra where devasya of the god pūjanam the worship is manasā in his mind iti thus viniścitya resolving yayau he went gopāla-sannidhim to the cowherds tataḥ then asau he satya-devasya of the god Satya pūjām the worship gopa-gaṇaiḥ the cowherds saha with bhakti-śraddhā-anvitaḥ filled with devotion and faith bhūtvā being cakāra performed vidhinā by the rule nṛpaḥ the king satya-deva-prasādena by the grace of the god Satya dhana-putra-anvitaḥ blessed with wealth and sons abhavat he became iha-loke in this world sukham happiness bhuktvā having enjoyed ca and ante at the end satya-puram to the city of Satya yayau he went

“Surely Satyadeva has destroyed all this of mine; so I will go to the very place where the god was worshipped.” Resolving so in his mind, he went to the cowherds, and with them the king, full of devotion and faith, performed the worship of Satyadeva as prescribed. By Satyadeva's grace he was blessed with wealth and sons; he enjoyed happiness in this world and at the end went to the city of Satya.

ya idaṃ kurute satyavrataṃ paramadurlabham |
śṛṇoti ca kathāṃ puṇyāṃ bhaktimuktiphalapradām ||
dhanadhānyādhikaṃ tasya bhavetsatyaprasādataḥ |
daridro labhate vittaṃ baddho mucyeta bandhanāt ||
bhīto bhayātpramucyeta satyameva na saṃśayaḥ ||

word by word · translation

yaḥ he who idam this kurute performs satya-vratam vow of Satya parama-durlabham most hard to come by śṛṇoti hears ca and kathām the story puṇyām holy bhakti-mukti-phala-pradām that gives the fruit of devotion and liberation dhana-dhānya-adhikam abundance of wealth and grain tasya to him bhavet comes satya-prasādataḥ by the grace of Satya daridraḥ the poor man labhate gains vittam wealth baddhaḥ the bound mucyeta is freed bandhanāt from bondage bhītaḥ the frightened bhayāt from fear pramucyeta is released satyam this is true eva indeed na there is no saṃśayaḥ doubt

Whoever keeps this supremely rare vow of Satya, and hears this holy story that yields devotion and liberation, gains wealth, grain and all else by Satya's grace. The poor man gains riches, the bound are freed from bonds, the fearful are freed from fear — this is truth, there is no doubt.

īpsitaṃ ca phalaṃ bhuktvā cānte satyapuraṃ vrajet |
iti vaḥ kathitaṃ viprāssatyanārāyaṇavratam |
yatkṛtvā sarvaduḥkhebhyo mukto bhavati mānavaḥ ||

word by word · translation

īpsitam the wished-for ca and phalam fruit bhuktvā having enjoyed ca and ante at the end satya-puram to the city of Satya vrajet one goes iti thus vaḥ to you kathitam has been told viprāḥ O brāhmaṇas satyanārāyaṇa-vratam the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa yat which kṛtvā having done sarva-duḥkhebhyaḥ from all sorrows muktaḥ freed bhavati becomes mānavaḥ a man

Having enjoyed the fruit he desires, at the end he goes to the city of Satya. Thus I have told you, brahmins, the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa, by keeping which a person is freed from every sorrow.

viśeṣataḥ kaliyuge satyapūjā phalapradā |
kecitkalau vadiṣyanti satyamīśaṃ tameva ca |
satyanārāyaṇaṃ kecitsatyadevaṃ tathaiva ca ||
śrīviṣṇunā dhṛtaṃ rūpaṃ sarveṣāmīpsitapradam ||

word by word · translation

viśeṣataḥ especially kali-yuge in the Kali age satya-pūjā the worship of Satya phala-pradā bears fruit kecit some kalau in the Kali age vadiṣyanti will call satyam Satya īśam the Lord tam him eva indeed ca and satyanārāyaṇam Satyanārāyaṇa kecit some satya-devam the god Satya tathā likewise eva indeed ca and śrī-viṣṇunā by Śrī Viṣṇu dhṛtam assumed rūpam the form sarveṣām to all īpsita-pradam grants what is wished

Above all in the Kali age the worship of Satya bears fruit. In Kali some will call him Satya, others Īśa, some Satyanārāyaṇa, and others Satyadeva — the form taken by Śrī Viṣṇu to grant what all desire.

nānārūpadharo bhūtvā sarveṣāmīpsitapradaḥ |
bhaviṣyati kalau satyavratarūpe sanātanaḥ ||

word by word · translation

nānā-rūpa-dharaḥ bearing many forms bhūtvā having become sarveṣām to all īpsita-pradaḥ the giver of what is wished bhaviṣyati will be kalau in the Kali age satya-vrata-rūpe in the form of the vow of Satya sanātanaḥ the eternal one

Taking many forms, granting what all desire, the Eternal One will appear in the Kali age in the form of the vow of Satya.

kathāṃ vā śṛṇuyādyastu paśyedvā vratamuttamam |
tasya naśyanti pāpāni satyadevaprasādataḥ ||

word by word · translation

kathām the story vā either śṛṇuyāt hears yaḥ he who tu indeed paśyet sees vā or vratam the vow uttamam excellent tasya his naśyanti perish pāpāni sins satya-deva-prasādataḥ by the grace of the god Satya

Whoever only hears the story, or witnesses the excellent vow — his sins are destroyed by the grace of Satyadeva.

iti śrīskāndapurāṇe revākhaṇḍe satyanārāyaṇavratakathāyāṃ
pañcamo'dhyāyassamāptaḥ ||

word by word · translation

iti thus śrī-skānda-purāṇe in the Śrī Skanda Purāṇa revā-khaṇḍe in the Revā section satyanārāyaṇa-vrata-kathāyām in the story of the vow of Satyanārāyaṇa pañcamaḥ the fifth adhyāyaḥ chapter samāptaḥ is complete

Thus ends the fifth chapter of the story of the Satyanārāyaṇa vow, in the Revākhaṇḍa of the Skānda Purāṇa.

❧

Punaḥpūjā · the worship repeated ↑

punaḥ prāṇānāyamya | śrīsatyanārāyaṇamuddiśya śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāmiprītyarthaṃ
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāminaḥ punaḥpūjāṃ kariṣye ||
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāmine namaḥ | dhyāyāmi | āvāhayāmi | ratnasiṃhāsanaṃ samarpayāmi |
pādayoḥ pādyaṃ samarpayāmi | hastayorarghyaṃ samarpayāmi | snapayāmi |
śuddhācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi | vastrayugmaṃ samarpayāmi | yajñopavītaṃ samarpayāmi |
divyaśrīcandanaṃ samarpayāmi | akṣatān samarpayāmi | nānāvidhapuṣpāṇi pūjayāmi |
oṃ keśavāya namaḥ … | dhūpamāghrāpayāmi | dīpaṃ darśayāmi |
dhūpadīpānantaram ācamanīyaṃ samarpayāmi ||

word by word · translation

punaḥ again prāṇān the breaths āyamya having restrained śrī-satyanārāyaṇam Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa uddiśya with reference to śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmi-prīti-artham to please Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāminaḥ of Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa punaḥ-pūjām the repeated worship kariṣye I shall perform śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmine to Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa namaḥ salutation dhyāyāmi I meditate on him āvāhayāmi I invoke him ratna-siṃha-āsanam a jewelled lion-throne samarpayāmi I offer pādayoḥ at his feet pādyam water for the feet samarpayāmi I offer hastayoḥ in his hands arghyam water of welcome samarpayāmi I offer snapayāmi I bathe him śuddha-ācamanīyam pure water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer vastra-yugmam a pair of garments samarpayāmi I offer yajña-upavītam the sacred thread samarpayāmi I offer divya-śrī-candanam divine sandal paste samarpayāmi I offer akṣatān unbroken rice samarpayāmi I offer nānā-vidha-puṣpāṇi flowers of many kinds pūjayāmi I offer in worship om keśavāya namaḥ om, salutation to Keśava dhūpam incense āghrāpayāmi I offer to be smelt dīpam the lamp darśayāmi I show dhūpa-dīpa-anantaram after incense and lamp ācamanīyam water for sipping samarpayāmi I offer

Restraining the breath again: for the pleasure of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa I will worship him once more. Salutation to him — meditation, invocation, throne, water for feet and hands, bath, pure water, garments, sacred thread, sandal, akṣata, many flowers; the names from Keśava onward; incense, lamp, and water to sip after them.

naivedyam — oṃ bhūrbhuvassuvaḥ … pracodayāt | satyaṃ tvartena pariṣiñcāmi |
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāmine namaḥ | amṛtamastu | amṛtopastaraṇamasi |
oṃ prāṇāya svāhā … samānāya svāhā | madhye madhye pānīyaṃ samarpayāmi |
amṛtāpidhānamasi | uttarāpośanaṃ samarpayāmi | tāmbūlaṃ samarpayāmi |
suvarṇamantrapuṣpaṃ samarpayāmi | pradakṣiṇanamaskārān samarpayāmi ||
anayā dhyānāvāhanādiṣoḍaśopacārapūjayā bhagavān sarvātmakaḥ
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvāmī suprītassuprasanno varado bhavatu |
śrīsatyanārāyaṇaprasādaṃ śirasā gṛhṇāmi ||

word by word · translation

naivedyam the food offering om om bhūḥ bhuvaḥ suvaḥ earth, mid-region, heaven pracodayāt may impel satyam you, truth tvā you ṛtena with order pariṣiñcāmi I sprinkle around śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmine to Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa namaḥ salutation amṛtam nectar astu may it be amṛta-upastaraṇam the under-layer of nectar asi you are om prāṇāya svāhā om, hail to the in-breath samānāya svāhā hail to the equalising breath madhye madhye in between pānīyam drinking water samarpayāmi I offer amṛta-apidhānam the covering of nectar asi you are uttara-āpośanam the closing sip samarpayāmi I offer tāmbūlam betel samarpayāmi I offer suvarṇa-mantra-puṣpam the golden flower of mantra samarpayāmi I offer pradakṣiṇa-namaskārān circumambulation and prostrations samarpayāmi I offer anayā by this dhyāna-āvāhana-ādi-ṣoḍaśa-upacāra-pūjayā worship of sixteen services, beginning with meditation and invocation bhagavān the blessed sarva-ātmakaḥ Self of all śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svāmī Lord Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa su-prītaḥ well pleased su-prasannaḥ most gracious vara-daḥ and granting boons bhavatu may he be śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-prasādam the grace of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa śirasā on my head gṛhṇāmi I receive

The food-offering as before, with betel, the flower of mantra, and circumambulation. By this worship may the Lord, Self of all, Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa, be well pleased, gracious, and grant boons; I receive his grace upon my head.

Udvāsanam · farewell ↑

yajñena yajñamayajanta devāstāni dharmāṇi prathamānyāsan |
te ha nākaṃ mahimānassacante yatra pūrve sādhyāssanti devāḥ ||

word by word · translation

yajñena with the sacrifice yajñam the sacrifice ayajanta sacrificed devāḥ the gods tāni those dharmāṇi ordinances prathamāni the first āsan were te those ha indeed nākam heaven mahimānaḥ mighty ones sacante reach yatra where pūrve the ancient sādhyāḥ Sādhyas santi dwell devāḥ the gods

With sacrifice the gods worshipped the Sacrifice; these were the first ordinances. Those great ones reached the vault of heaven, where the ancient Sādhyas, the gods, abide. (The deity is bidden farewell.)

Maṇṭapadānam · gift of the maṇḍapa ↑

The image and maṇḍapa are given to the officiating priest, honoured as Satyanārāyaṇa himself.

punaḥ prāṇānāyamya | parameśvaraprītyarthaṃ
svarṇasvarcitapratimāmaṇṭapadānaṃ kariṣye ||
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvarūpasya brāhmaṇasya ubhābhyāṃ pādyam ||

word by word · translation

punaḥ again prāṇān the breaths āyamya having restrained parama-īśvara-prīti-artham to please the supreme Lord svarṇa-su-arcita-pratimā-maṇṭapa-dānam the gift of the pavilion with the well-worshipped golden image kariṣye I shall make śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svarūpasya who is Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa in person brāhmaṇasya of the brāhmaṇa ubhābhyām for both feet pādyam water for the feet

Restraining the breath again: for the pleasure of the Supreme Lord I will give the maṇḍapa with its gilded image. Water for both feet of the brahmin who is the form of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa.

namo'stvanantāya sahasramūrtaye sahasrapādākṣiśirorubāhave |
sahasranāmne puruṣāya śāśvate sahasrakoṭīyugadhāriṇe namaḥ ||
śrīsatyanārāyaṇasvarūpasya brāhmaṇasya gandhaṃ akṣatān puṣpāṇi ||

word by word · translation

namaḥ salutation astu be anantāya to the endless one sahasra-mūrtaye of a thousand forms sahasra-pāda-akṣi-śiraḥ-ūru-bāhave with a thousand feet, eyes, heads, thighs and arms sahasra-nāmne of a thousand names puruṣāya to the Person śāśvate eternal sahasra-koṭī-yuga-dhāriṇe who upholds a thousand crores of ages namaḥ salutation śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-svarūpasya who is Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa in person brāhmaṇasya for the brāhmaṇa gandham sandal paste akṣatān unbroken rice puṣpāṇi flowers

Salutation to the Infinite, of a thousand forms, with a thousand feet, eyes, heads, thighs and arms; to the eternal Person of a thousand names, who sustains a thousand crores of ages — salutation. Sandal, akṣata and flowers to the brahmin who is the form of Satyanārāyaṇa.

asmai brāhmaṇāya yajuśśākhādhyāyine … gotrāya … śarmaṇe pratigṛhītre
śrīmān … gotraḥ … nāmadheyaḥ dharmapatnīsameto'haṃ
… gotrasya … nāmadheyasya dharmapatnīsametasya
mamopāttaduritakṣayadvārā śrīparameśvaraprītyarthaṃ
satyanārāyaṇasvarṇasvarcitapratimāmaṇṭapaṃ tubhyamahaṃ sampradade na mama ||

word by word · translation

asmai to this brāhmaṇāya brāhmaṇa yajuḥ-śākhā-adhyāyine a student of the Yajus branch gotrāya of the lineage named śarmaṇe bearing the name pratigṛhītre the receiver śrīmān the fortunate gotraḥ of the lineage named nāmadheyaḥ bearing the name dharma-patnī-sametaḥ together with my lawful wife aham I gotrasya of the lineage named nāmadheyasya bearing the name dharma-patnī-sametasya together with his lawful wife mama my upātta-durita-kṣaya-dvārā through the destruction of accumulated sins śrī-parama-īśvara-prīti-artham to please the supreme Lord satyanārāyaṇa-svarṇa-su-arcita-pratimā-maṇṭapam the pavilion with the well-worshipped golden image of Satyanārāyaṇa tubhyam to you aham I sampradade give na not mama mine

To this brahmin, student of the Yajur-veda branch, of such a gotra and name, the receiver — I, of such a gotra and name, with my wife, for the removal of my sins and the pleasure of the Supreme Lord, give you this maṇḍapa with the gilded image of Satyanārāyaṇa: it is no longer mine.

devasya tvā savituḥ prasave'śvinorbāhubhyāṃ pūṣṇo hastābhyāṃ pratigṛhṇāmi |
vaśśreyasī pratigṛhṇāmi ||
etaddānasādguṇyārthaṃ yathāśakti dakṣiṇāṃ tubhyamahaṃ sampradade na mama ||

word by word · translation

devasya of the god tvā you savituḥ Savitṛ prasave at the impulse aśvinoḥ of the Aśvins bāhubhyām with the arms pūṣṇaḥ of Pūṣan hastābhyām with the hands pratigṛhṇāmi I receive vaḥ you śreyasī the better pratigṛhṇāmi I receive etat-dāna-sādguṇya-artham for the full merit of this gift yathā-śakti as I am able dakṣiṇām a fee tubhyam to you aham I sampradade give na not mama mine

(The priest:) At the impulse of god Savitṛ, with the arms of the Aśvins and the hands of Pūṣan, I receive you. — To make this gift complete, I give you a fee as my means allow: it is no longer mine.

śrīsatyanārāyaṇavratakalpaḥ samāptaḥ ||

word by word · translation

śrī-satyanārāyaṇa-vrata-kalpaḥ the rite of the vow of Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa samāptaḥ is complete

Here ends the Śrī Satyanārāyaṇa Vratakalpa.
